# The China Year Book 1938 — Who's Who & Directories

955 entries, LLM-transcribed. These entries were transcribed by the vision model of Opus 5.5. Before using any of these details, you must verify specific details with the original source which is linked to whenever possible.


## Who's Who

**AUYANG Kui** see Ouyang Chü. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**AW Boon-haw** see Hu Wen-hu. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**BIEN, Z.S** see Pien Shou-sun. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**“BUTTERFLY WU,”** see Hu Tieh. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHAI Tsun-ch'ien, (翟俊千)** Born 1890; native of Kwangtung. Ph.D. (Chicago). Sometime senior professor and vice-president, Chinan University; commissioner of education, Canton city government; professor of education, Peiping and Chungshan universities. Dean of studies, Chi Nan University, Chenju (Shanghai), since March, 1935. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHAN, C. S** see Ch'en Tsung-ch'eng. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHAN Chak** see Ch'en Ts'eh. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG, C. Y** see Chang Tseh-yao. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Ch'eng-yu, (張承槱)** Courtesy name: P'eng-sheng 蓬生 Born 1882; native of Chihchiang, Hupeh. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. B.S. (George Washington). Sometime departmental director, salt chief inspectorate. 1931-4, departmental director, ministry of audit. Administrative vice-minister of audit, since December, 1934. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chi, (張繼)** Courtesy name: P'u-ch'uan 溥泉 Born 1882; native of Ts'anghsien, Hopei. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1924, member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1928, chairman, Hopei branch, Kuomintang central political council. State councillor of the National Government, since October, 1928. Concurrently vice-president, Judicial Yuan (October, 1928-December, 1931); promoted president, same yuan (January-May, 1932). Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chia-ao, (張嘉璈)** Courtesy name: Kung-ch'uan 公權 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Chang Kia-ngau. Born 1888; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. Graduate, Keio University, Tokyo. 1912, chief secretary, senate. 1917-29, vice-governor, Bank of China. 1930-4, managing director and general manager, same institution reorganized. 1935, deputy governor, Central Bank of China. Minister of railways since December, 1935. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG-CHIA Hu-t'u-k'eh-t'u, (章嘉呼圖克圖)** Individual name: Sang-chieh-cha-pu 桑結札布 Better known as the Hutukhtu, or “Living Buddha,” of Mongolia, to distinguish him from the two “Living Buddhas” of Tibet. Concurrently pacification commissioner for Mongolia, since December, 1932. Appointed state councillor of National Government in March, 1937. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Ch'ien, (張謙)** Courtesy name: Kung-hui 公撝 Prefers English rendering: Henry K. Chang. Born 1888; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. LL.B. (Pennsylvania). 1929-32, consul-general at San Francisco, then New York. Chargé d'affaires at Santiago, then minister to Chile, since November, 1932. Past president, Tientsin Rotary Club. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chih-chiang, (張之江)** Courtesy name: Tzu-chiang 子薑 Born 1881; native of Yenshan, Hopei. 1924, Tutung of Chahar. 1925, Tupan of North-West frontiers. October, 1928-November, 1930, chairman, national opium suppression commission. 1931, bandit-suppression commissioner, Kiangsu Province. President, National Physical Culture Institute, concurrently senior counsellor, military advisory council, since 1932. Christian leader. 1935, travelled in America and Europe for six months. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chih-chung, (張治中)** Courtesy name: Wen-pai 文白 Born 1896; native of Chiaohsien, Anhwei. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Once studied military science in Germany. 1931-2, commander, 87th division; assisted 19th route army in defending Shanghai area, as concurrent commander, 5th army. Dean of studies, Central Military Academy, Nanking, since May, 1932. First-class Paoting decoration; Order of the White Sun and Blue Sky. Now garrison commissioner of Nanking and Shanghai, commending all Chinese forces fighting Japan in Shanghai-Woosung area. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chih-pen, (張知本)** Courtesy name: Huai-chiu 懷九 Native of Chianglin, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. 1924, member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1927-8, chairman, Hupeh provincial government. Re-elected member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1933. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Chin-chiang** (or Chang Chin-kiang), see Chang Jen-chieh. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Ching-hsiu, (張敬修)** Courtesy name: Yün-lung 運隆 Better known as the Very Rev. Joseph Chang. Born 1881; native of Hankow, Hupeh. Studied at Wuchang Seminary. 1908-28, ordained as priest; vicar of Hankow cathedral. Promoted prefect apostolic of Puch'i (Hupeh), since April, 1929. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Ch'un, (張羣)** Courtesy name: Yueh-chün 岳軍 Born 1888; native of Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy and Tokyo Military Staff College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1928, director, Shanghai arsenal; political vice-minister of military affairs. 1929-31, mayor of Great I Shanghai. Member, Kuomintang centraerexecutive committee, since March, 1929. July, 1933-November, 1935, chairman, Hupeh provincial government. Minister for foreign affairs from December, 1935, to February, 1937. Appointed secretary general to central political council in March, 1937. — p. 142 ([scan leaf 165](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n165/mode/1up))

**CHANG Fa-k'uei, (張發奎)** Better known as Chang Fah-kwei. Born 1891; native of Shih-hsing, Kwangtung. Graduate, Wuchang Military Institute. Sometime commander, Nationalist 12th division; commander, redoubtable Nationalist 4th army (“Ironsides”). 1933-4, travelled in U.S.A. and Europe. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. 1936, bandit-suppression commissioner, Chekiang-Fukien-Anhwei-Kiangsi borders. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Fah-kwei** see above. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Fu-yun, (張福運)** Born 1890; native of Fushan, Shantung. B.A., LL.B. (Harvard). 1922-6, director, navigation department, ministry of communications; president, Communications University, Peking. 1928-32, director, customs administration, ministry of finance. Director, research division, national economic council, since July, 1934. Member, Chinese delegation to Coronation of King George VI, 1937. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Henry K** see Chang Ch'ien. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Hsiao-lin, (張嘯林)** Prominent resident, French concession, Shanghai. Proprietor, Ling Kee Lumber Co. Councillor, French municipal council, since 1933. Decorated by the National Government for munificent contributions towards flood relief and purchase of aeroplanes. June, 1936, his 60th birthday celebrated by 40,000 friends and with three-day theatrical performances. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Hsin-hai, (張歆海)** Prefers English rendering: H. H. Chang. Born 1898; native of Haiyien, Chekiang. B.A. (Johns Hopkins). M.A., Ph.D. (Harvard). 1928-33, counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs. September, 1933-June, 1934, minister to Portugal. Transferred minister to Poland, 1934-7. Author, “Matthew Arnold and His Humanistic View of Life” (doctoral thesis). — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Hsin-lang, (張星烺)** Courtesy name: Liang-ch'en 亮丞 Born 1887; native of Ssuyang, Kiangsu. B.Sc. (Harvard). 1926-7, dean, sinological institute, Amoy University. Professor of history, Yenching University and Fu Jen (Catholic) University, since 1927. Author of treatises on early intercourse between China and Western countries. Editor and secretary, Chinese Geographical Society, Peiping. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Hsueh-liang, (張學良)** Courtesy name: Han-ch'ing 漢卿 Son of late Chang Tso-lin; better known as the “Young Marshal.” Born 1898; native of Hai-ch'eng, Liaoning. July, 1928, elected commander-in-chief for preserving the peace of Manchuria. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government. December, 1931-August, 1932, director of peace preservation for North China. September, 1932-March, 1933, acting chairman Peiping branch, National Military Council. 1933, visited Europe. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1932. Deputy commander-in-chief, Honan-Hupeh-Anhwei and North-Western bandit-suppression forces, 1934-7. Now in retirement after the settlement of the Sian mutiny. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Hui-ch'ang, (張惠長)** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Chang Wai-jeung. Born 1898; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Better known as the “Chinese Lindbergh” for his successful flight, in 1928, from Canton to Mukden and back, on a duplicate machine of Colonel Lindbergh's “Spirit of St. Louis.” 1930, director of aviation, ministry of military affairs. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. Minister to Cuba since May, 1935. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Jen-chieh, (張人傑)** Courtesy name: Chin-chiang 靜江 Born 1873; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. One of Kuomintang's “Elder Statesmen.” 1924, member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1927-30, chairman, Chekiang provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1926. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Chairman, National Reconstruction Commission, since October, 1928. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Right Rev. John** see Chang Pi-teh. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Very Rev. Joseph** see Chang Ching-hsiu. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Kia-ngau** see Chang Chia-ao. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Liang-jen, (張樑任)** Prefers own rendering: Chang Liang-yen. Born 1905; native of P'inghu, Chekiang. 1931, doctor of political science (Berlin). Advisor on juridical matters, ministry of communications, since 1932. Concurrently editor, compilation and translation bureau, ministry of education (1933-4). Author, “Shanghai Gold Exchange” (in German); “Business Cycles” (in Chinese), etc. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Liang-yen** see above. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Loy** see Cheng Lai. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Mo-chun, Miss, (張默君女士)** Wife of Shao Yuan-ch'ung 邵元冲夫人 Native of Sianghsiang, Hunan. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Graduate, Columbia Teachers' College. Promoter and principal, Shen Chow Girls' School (Shanghai), for fifteen years. 1931-2, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Examining commissioner, examination commission, Examination Yuan, since 1931. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, P. C** see below. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG P'eng-ch'un, (張彭春)** Prefers English rendering: P. C. Chang. Born 1892; brother of Chang Po-ling. M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1921, wrote and produced “Mulan” at New York Court Theatre. 1930, producing director for Mei Lan-fang in his American tour. Professor of philosophy and education, Nankai University, Tientsin, since September, 1930. 1931, 1933-4, visiting professor, Chicago and Hawaii Universities. At present lecturer in England. Author, “Education for Modernization in China” (doctoral thesis). — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Pi-teh, (張弼德)** Better known as the Right Rev. John Chang. Born 1893; native of Ningtsin, Hopei. Studied at Chengting Seminary. 1917, ordained as priest. 1918-22, professor of philosophy, Chengting Seminary; missionary. 1929, appointed prefect apostolic. Promoted titular bishop of Antipyrgium and vicar apostolic of Chaohsien (Hopei), since April, 1932. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Poling** see Chang Shou-ch'un. — p. 143 ([scan leaf 166](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n166/mode/1up))

**CHANG Shih-chao, (章士釗)** Courtesy name: Hsing-yen 行嚴 Born 1886: native of Changsha, Hunan. M.A. (Edinburgh). 1923-5, minister of education and then of justice. Legal practitioner in Shanghai since 1932. Founder and editor, well-known defunct Chia Yin (“Tiger”) magazine. Recently appointed member of Hopei-Chahar political council. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Shou-ch'un, (張壽春)** Courtesy name: Po-ling 伯苓 Prefers English rendering: Poling Chang. Born 1874; native of Tientsin, Hopei. Graduate, Peiyang Naval College. Attended Teachers' College, Columbia University. Hon. Litt. D. (St. John's). Founder and president, since 1904, Nankai University, Christian leader and president, China National Amateur Athletic Federation. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Shou-yung, (張壽鏞)** Born 1885; native of Kiangsu. 1928-32, political vice-minister of finance. President. Kwang Hua University, Shanghai, since 1927, Adviser, Ministry of Finance, since 1933. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Tao-fan, (張道藩)** Born 1897; native of Kweichow. B.A. (London). 1928-9, chief secretary, Nanking city government. 1931, commissioner of education, Chekiang provincial government. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. November, 1932-November, 1935, administrative vice-minister of communications. Transferred administrative vice-minister of interior, since December, 1935. Translator, W. G. Constable's “Recent European Art.” — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Tseh-yao, (張澤垚)** Courtesy name: Hsiang-sheng 湘生 Prefers to sign himself: C. Y. Chang. Born 1894; native of Poyang, Kiangsi. M.A. (Cornell). Ph.D. (Ohio). 1930-4, senior technical expert, ministry of industries. Expert investigator, National Economic Council, since July, 1934. Author of scientific treatises on oils, fats and Chinese medicinal herbs. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Tung-sun, (張東蓀)** Prefers own rendering: Chang Tung-sheng. Born 1886; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). 1916, chief secretary, senate. Sometime editor, China Times (Shanghai); acting president, China Institute (Woosung); dean of arts, Kwang Hua University (Shanghai). Professor of philosophy, Yenching University, Peiping, since 1929. Author of treatises on ethical philosophy, new philosophy, etc. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Tzu-chung, (張自忠)** Courtesy name: Chin-ch'en 藎忱 Born 1890; native of Lintsing, Shantung. Graduate, Shantung Provincial Law College. 1914, entered army service under General Feng Yu-hsiang. 1929, commander, 6th division, national army. 1931, commander, 38th division, 29th army. November, 1935-May, 1936, chairman, Chahar provincial government. Transferred mayor of Tientsin, since June, 1936. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Tzu-p'ing, (張資平)** Prefers own rendering: Djang Tsu-ping. Born 1893; native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. B.Sc. (Tokyo Imperial). Professor of geology, Great China University, Shanghai, since 1930. Author of textbooks on geology and mining, Better known as prolific fiction writer. having produced over sixty novels and short stories. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Wai-jeung** see Chang Hui-chang. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG, Y. Z** see Chang Yuan-ch'ang. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Yi-ch'u, (張翼樞)** Born 1888; native of Honan. Studied at Ecole des Sciences Morales et Politiques, Paris. Sometime counsellor on foreign affairs, Yunnan provincial government. 1927-30, counsellor, ministry of justice. Chief of Chinese section, Havas News Agency in China, since 1931. Elected member, French municipal council, Shanghai, since 1934. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Yuan-ch'ang, (張沅長)** Prefers English rendering: Y. Z. Chang. Born 1905; native of Shanghai. B.A. (Fuhtan). Ph.D. (Johns Hopkins). 1927-31, Chinese instructor, Johns Hopkins Teachers College. 1931-3, professor of English, Wuhan University (Wuchang). 1933-5, professor of English, Central University, Nanking. 1935-6, exchange professor of English at Chapel Hill, North Carolina University. Professor of English, Michigan University, since July, 1936. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHANG Yuan-shan, (章元善)** Courtesy name: Yen-hsün 彥訓 Prefers to sign himself: Y. S. Djang. Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B.A. (Cornell). Executive secretary, China International Famine Relief Commission, Peiping, since 1922. Director of co-operatives, ministry of industries, since January, 1936. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO Ch'ing-hua, (趙慶化)** Better known as the Very Rev. Luke Chao. Born 1874; native of Jehol. Studied at Sungshu-tsui-tzu Seminary (Liaoning). 1899, ordained as priest. Promoted prefect apostolic of Chihfeng (Jehol), since January, 1932. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO, Edward M. P** see Chao Mei-po. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO Hsi-en, (趙錫恩)** Courtesy name: Chin-ch'ing 晉卿 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: S. U. Zau. Born 1882; native of Shanghai. 1928-30, councillor, Shanghai municipal council. 1931, administrative vice-minister of industries. Managing director, Tung Yieh Land and Estate Co., and China Industrial Corporation, since 1928. Director, Wine, Tobacco and Stamp Tax Bureau, Hangchow, since 1934. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO, Very Rev. Luke** see Chao Ch'ing-hua. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO Mei-po, (趙梅伯)** Prefers English rendering; Edward M. P. Chao. Born 1905; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Graduate, Brussels Royal Conservatory of Music. Probably China's most accomplished singer. Has been enthusiastically received by audiences in U.S.A. and Europe, particularly Geneva and Brussels. 1934, crowned singer laureate in special singing exhibition by fifty artistes in Brussels. Composer of “Yellow Bell” and two other song books. July, 1936, returned from U.S.A. to accept music professorship in Shanghai National Conservatory of Music. — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO Min-heng, (趙敏恆)** Prefers English rendering: Thomas Mingheng Chao. Born 1904; native of Nanking. B.A. (Missouri). M.S. (Columbia). 1927, assistant editor, Peking Leader. Nanking correspondent, then manager, Nanking bureau, Reuter's Agency, since 1928. Author, “Foreign Press in China.” — p. 144 ([scan leaf 167](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n167/mode/1up))

**CHAO Mou-hua, Miss, (趙懋華女士)** Courtesy name: P'ei-ch'in 佩琴 Born 1898; native of Szechwan. Ph.D. (Berlin). Sometime special compiler, ministry of education; expert, national defence planning committee, board of general staff. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since 1934. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO Nai-ch'uan, (趙迺傳)** Courtesy name - Shu-t'ing 述庭 Born 1890; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. M.A. (Columbia Teachers' College). Sometime counsellor, ministry of education; lecturer, Nanking University, and Central Political Institute; professor of education, Central University. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1930. Technical member, commission on amendment of administrative laws (attached to Kuomintang central political council), since May, 1931. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO P'ei-lien, (趙丕廉)** Courtesy name: Chih-ch'ing 芷青 Born 1886; native of Wutai, Shansi. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1928, vice-minister of interior. November, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang for abetting Yen Hsi-shan's revolt. December, 1931, reinstated reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Vice-chairman, commission on Mongolian and Tibetan affairs, since January, 1932. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO T. C** see Chao Tzu-ch'eng. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO Tai-wen, (趙戴文)** Courtesy name: Tz'u-lung 次隴 Born 1867; native of Wutai, Shansi. 1928, chairman, Chahar provincial government. 1929-30, minister of interior; president, Control Yuan. November, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang for abetting Yen Hsi-shan's revolt. Reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Reappointed state councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Chairman, Shansi provincial government, since June, 1936. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO, Thomas Mingheng** see Chao Min-heng. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO Tzu-ch'eng, (趙紫宸)** Prefers English rendering: T. C. Chao. born 1888; native of Chekiang. M.A., B.D. (Vanderbilt). D. Litt. (Soochow). Professor of philosophy of Christianity, Yenching University, Peiping, since 1926. Concurrently dean, school of religion, same institution. Author, “Present Day Religious Thought and Life in China,” “Status of Christian Literature.” Co-author, “China Today through Chinese Eyes.” — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO, Y. R** see Chao Yuan-jen. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAO Yuan-jen, (趙元任)** Prefers English rendering: Y. R. Chao. born 1892; native of Changchow, Kiangsu. B.A. (Cornell). Ph.D. (Harvard). 1932-3, director, Chinese educational mission, Washington, D.C. Research fellow, division of history and languages, Academia Sinica, since 1929. Author, “A New Book of Rimes” and “A Phonograph Course in the Chinese National Language.” Composer, “Songs of Contemporary Poems.” Translator, “Alice's Adventures in Wonderland” and “The Camberley Triangle.” — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CHAR, George Y** see Hsieh Yuan-fu. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ch'ang-heng, (陳長蘅)** Courtesy name: Po-hsiu 伯修 Born 1891; native of Szechwan. M.A. (Harvard). Sometime professor of economics in Peiping and Nanking; statistician, salt chief inspectorate; special secretary, Kuomintang central political council; adviser, comptroller-general's office. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1928. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chao-ying, (陳肇英)** Courtesy name: Hsiung-fu 雄夫 Born 1888; native of Puchiang, Chekiang. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1928. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Director of Kuomintang affairs, Fukien province, since April, 1934. Supervisory commissioner for Control Yuan in Fukien and Chekiang provinces, since April, 1935. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chen-hsien, (陳振先)** Courtesy name: Toh-shih 鐸士 Born 1877; native of Fatshan, Kwangtung. B.Sc. (California). Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1912-3, minister of agriculture and commerce. 1918-21, senator. 1927-35, professor of economics, Customs College, Peiping. Expert investigator, national economic council, since September, 1935. Author, “A Historical Sketch of Chinese Astronomy and Astronomical Instruments,” “Chen's Phonetic System,” etc. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ch'eng, (陳誠)** Native of Ch'ingt'ien, Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1929, promoted commander, 11th division. 1932-3, commander, 18th army. 1934, field commander, northern route, Kiangsi-Kwangtung-Fukien-Hunan-Hupeh bandit-suppression forces. Director of military reorganization, Generalissimo's headquarters at Wuchang and Chungking, 1935-6. Now administrative vice-minister of military affairs. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chi-liang, (陳季良)** Native of Fukien. Vice-admiral. Commander, first squadron. Administrative, then political, vice-minister of navy, since 1929. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chi-t'ang, (陳濟棠)** Courtesy name: Po-nan 伯南 Born 1890; native of Fangchen, Kwangtung. Graduate, Kwangtung Military Academy. 1928, commander, Nationalist 4th army. 1929, field commander, 8th route army. April, 1931-July, 1936, dominant military leader in South China. July 20, 1936, dismissed from all military command for insubordination against Central Government. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ch'i-ts'ai, (陳其采)** Courtesy name: Ai-shih 藹士 Born 1879; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1927-30, commissioner of finance, Chekiang and Kiangsu provincial governments; superintendent, Shanghai customs. Comptroller-general, since April, 1931. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chieh, (陳介)** Courtesy name: Cheh-ch'ing 蔗青 Born 1885: native of Sianghsiang, Hunan. Attended Tokyo and Berlin universities. 1918, member of parliament. 1925-6, superintendent of customs and commissioner for foreign affairs, Hankow. 1927-8, manager, Exchange Bank, and Yien Yieh Commercial Bank, Shanghai. Administrative vice-minister for foreign affairs, since December, 1935. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, April-November, 1935. — p. 145 ([scan leaf 168](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n168/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chien-hsiu, (陳劍脩)** Prefers English rendering: Pao-ao Ch'en. Born 1897; native of Suich'uan, Kiangsi. M.A. (London). 1929-30, director of social education, ministry of education. 1932, commissioner of education, Kiangsi provincial government. Dean of education, Central University, Nanking, since January, 1934. Author, “The Correlation between Character and Instinct” and “Children's Dream.” — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chien-ju, (陳劍如)** Born 1895; native of T'aishan. Kwangtung. Graduate, Peking Government University. Sometime acting mayor of Canton; chief secretary, ministry of finance, Canton government; director of general affairs, central Kuomintang headquarters training department; counsellor, National Government headquarters. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Commissioner of social affairs, Nanking city government, since April, 1935. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chih-shih, (陳之碩)** Courtesy name: Chün-yien 君衍 Native of Shensi. 1923, director of public loans, ministry of finance. 1924, commissioner of finance, Honan provincial government. Political vice-minister, and concurrently acting minister, of audit, since December, 1934. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chin-t'ao, (陳錦濤)** Courtesy name: Lan-sheng 瀾生 Born 1870; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. M.S. (Columbia). Ph.D. (Yale). Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1916, again 1926, minister of finance. Chairman, ministry of finance currency reform committee, since January, 1935. Author, “Societary Circulation” (doctoral thesis). — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chueh-sheng, (陳覺生)** Born 1899; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B. Sc. and LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime technical member, Kuomintang central political council's people's food committee; advisor to Peiping political council and Chahar provincial government; vice-director, Institute for the Training of Rural Directors (Hopei.) Managing-Director, Peiping-Liaoning Railway, since December 1935. Chairman, communications committee, Hopei-Chahar political council, since May, 1936. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Chung-fu, (陳中孚)** Born 1882; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Studied in Japan; member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime commissioner of finance, Anhwei provincial government; administrative member, Kiangsu provincial government; administrative member, South-West political council. Member, Hopei-Chahar political council, since December, 1935. Concurrently chairman, foreign relations committee, same council, since March, 1936. Resigned January, 1937. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN, Eugene** see Ch'en Yu-jen. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Heng-cheh, Miss, (陳衡哲女士)** Wife of H.C. Zen 任鴻雋夫人 Prefers English rendering: Sophia H. Ch'en. native of Changchow, Kiangsu. B.A. (Vassar). M.A. (Chicago). 1921-3, professor of history, Peking Government University. Author, “Chinese Women and Other Essays” and (in Chinese) “History of Western Countries,” “Little Raindrop,” etc. Editor, “Symposium of Chinese Culture.” Co-founder, Tu Li P'ing Lun (Independent Critic Weekly), Peiping, since 1932. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Hsing, (陳行)** Courtesy name: Chien-an 健菴 Prefers English rendering: Jian H. Chen. Born 1890; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. B.Sc. (St. John's). M.A. (Ohio). 1926-7, manager, Central Bank, Hankow, and director, Wuchang mint. Deputy governor, reorganized Central Bank, since October, 1928. Concurrently director, Central Mint, since January, 1935. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Hsun-yung, (陳訓泳)** Born 1880; native of Foochow, Fukien. Vice-admiral. 1929-33, commander, training squadron. Administrative vice-minister of navy, since January, 1934. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Hui-teh, (陳輝德)** Courtesy name: Kuang-fu 光甫 Prefers English rendering: K. P. Chen. Born 1880; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. B. Com. (Pennsylvania). General manager, Shanghai Commercial & Savings Bank, since 1915. Chairman, cotton industry regulation committee, national economic council, since September, 1933. Leader of Chinese mission to U.S.A. (1936) to negotiate silver agreement with Washington Government. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Hung, (陳洪)** Prefers English rendering: S. Y. H. Chen. Native of P'inghu, Chekiang. Sometime director, wine and tobacco, then stamp tax, bureaux in Honan. Director-general, national state lottery administration, since March, 1935. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Huo-ch'in, (陳鶴琴)** Born 1881; native of Shangyü, Chekiang. 1918, B.A. (Johns Hopkins). 1919, M.A. (Columbia). Sometime professor of education, Southeastern University. Chinese education officer, Shanghai municipal council, since 1928. Author and translator of books on children's education. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CHEN, Jian H** see Ch'en Hsing. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN K'eh-hui, (陳克恢)** Prefers English rendering: K. K. Chen. Born 1896; native of Tsingpu. Kiangsu. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. Ph.D. (Wisconsin). M.D. (Johns Hopkins). 1926-29, lecturer on pharmacology, Johns Hopkins Medical School. Director of pharmacologic research for Eli Lilly & Co. (U.S.A.), since 1929. Well-known for work in connection with the isolation of ephedrine from Chinese ma-huang plant, now widely used for relief of nasal disorders. Co-author, with Prof. Carl F. Schmidt, “Ephedrine and Its Related Compounds,” and 150 pamphlets on various phases of pharmacology. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CHEN, K. K** see above. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CHEN, K. P** see Chen Hui-teh. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ku-yuan, (陳顧遠)** Courtesy name: Ch'in-kao 晴皐 Born 1894; native of Sanyuan, Shensi. LL.B. (Peking Government). Sometime manager, Kuo Min News Agency (Peiping); secretary, board of audit; special member, central Kuomintang headquarters mass movement directional committee. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1934. Author of numerous volumes on private international law, political philosophy of Mo-tzu and Mencius, history of ancient Chinese weddings, etc. — p. 146 ([scan leaf 169](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n169/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Kung-po, (陳公博)** Born 1890; native of Kwangtung. B.A. (New York). One of the younger leaders of the Kuomintang left wing. Regarded as “a remarkable orator, a powerful writer, and an energetic party organizer.” 1927-8, editor, The Revolutionary Critic weekly. July, 1929, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1932-5 minister of industries. Chairman, committee for direction of people's movement, central Kuomintang headquarters, since April, 1932. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Kuo-fu, (陳果夫)** Courtesy name: Tsu-t'ao 祖燾 Born 1889: native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1926, member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee. 1929-31, vice-president, Control Yuan. 1928-33, state councillor of the National Government. Chairman. Kiangsu provincial government, since September, 1933. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Li-fu, (陳立夫)** Born 1890; brother of Ch'en Kuo-fu. M.A. (Pittsburgh). 1927-8, chief secretary, generalissimo's headquarters; director of political training, directorate-general of military training. 1929-31, secretary general, central Kuomintang headquarters. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Concurrently chairman, organization committee, central Kuomintang headquarters, since May, 1932. State councillor of the National Government, since July, 1933. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Lu, (陳籙)** Courtesy name: Jen-hsien 任先 Prefers French rendering: Tcheng Loh. Born 1878; native of Foochow, Fukien. LL.B. (Paris). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1919, officiating minister for foreign affairs. 1920-8, minister to France. Vice-chairman, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs, since May, 1936. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ming-shu, (陳銘樞)** Courtesy name: Chen-ju 眞如 Born 1889; native of Hoppo, Kwangtung. Educated at Paoting Military Academy, Paoting, Hopei. Member of Tung Men Hui (predecessor of Kuomintang) and took part in first revolution, fighting against Chang Hsun at Hsuchow, Kiangsu. Opposed Yuan Shih-kai's imperial regime and fled to Japan until 1924. On returning to China, he joined Kwangtung 1st army division on general staff. Subsequently appointed commander, 10th division, 4th army, and concurrently commander of south route army. 1926, fought against Wu P'ei-fu and captured Wuchang, Hupeh; appointed defence commander of Wuhan area. Later became chief of general political bureau of the national army headquarters. Chairman, Kwangtung provincial government, 1928-31; Garrison commander of Shanghai-Nanking area; vice-president of Executive Yuan and concurrently minister of communications, 1931-2; leader in Fukien independence movement 1933-4. Now in retirement since collapse of movement. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CHEN, Pao-an** see Ch'en Chien-hsiu. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Pi-chun, Miss, (陳璧君女士)** Wife of Wang Ching-wei 汪精衞夫人 Ardent revolutionary worker; persuaded her father in Straits Settlements to contribute substantially to revolutionary causes. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since November, 1931. Donated (November, 1934) $60,000 towards erection of “model village” for Nanking's hut dwellers. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Pu-lei, (陳布雷)** Courtesy name: Wei-lei 畏[illegible] Born 1890; native of Tz'uhsi, Chekiang. Sometime editor-in-chief, China Times (Shanghai). 1931, vice-minister of education. January, 1932-March, 1934, commissioner of education, Chekiang provincial government. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CHEN, S. K** see Ch'en Shao-K'uan. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CHEN, S. Y. H** see Ch'en Hung. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Shao-k'uan, (陳紹寬)** Prefers English rendering: S. K. Chen. Born 1888; native of Foochow, Fukien. 1920-3, captain, training ship Tung Chi and training cruiser Ying Swei. 1925, promoted vice-admiral. 1926-31, commander, 2nd squadron. April, 1929-December, 1931, political vice-minister of navy. Promoted minister of navy, since January, 1932. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Shih-chen, (陳石珍)** Born 1892; native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. Graduate, Oberlin College, and Columbia University. 1928-30, chief secretary, ministry of education. Transferred counsellor, same ministry, since May, 1930. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Shu-jen, (陳樹人)** Born 1884; native of Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. Well-known painter. 1928-9, member, Kuomintang central executive committee. November, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Chairman, Chinese overseas commission, since May, 1932. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CHEN, Sophia H** see Ch'en Heng-cheh. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ta, (陳達)** Courtesy name: T'ung-fu 通夫 Prefers English rendering: Ta T. Chen. Born 1892: native of Yuhang, Chekiang. B.A. (Reed). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). Professor of sociology, Tsing Hua University, since 1923. Authority on labour problems in China. Author, “Chinese Migrations, with Special Reference to Labour Conditions” (Bulletin No. 340, U.S. Bureau of Labour Statistics). — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ta-ch'i, (陳大齊)** Courtesy name: Pai-nien 百年 1931-2, secretary-general, Examination Yuan. July, 1932-November, 1934, vice-chairman, examinations commission, Examination Yuan. Promoted chairman, same commission, since December, 1934. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CHEN, Ta T** see Ch'en Ta. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN T'iao-yuan, (陳調元)** Courtesy name: Hsueh-hsuan 雪[illegible] Born 1888; native of Hsinan, Hopei. Graduate, Peking Military College. 1928-32, chairman, Anhwei provincial government, and Shantung provincial government. 1934, commander-in-chief, Kiangsi-Kwangtung-Hunan-Hupeh bandit-suppression reserve forces. Chairman, military advisory council, since December, 1934. — p. 147 ([scan leaf 170](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n170/mode/1up))

**CH'EN T'i-Ch'eng, (陳體誠)** Born 1895; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.C.E. (Nanyang). Practical experience in New York and Pittsburgh workshops. 1924-31, chief engineer, Peking-Hankow railway, and Chekiang highway administration. 1932, director, latter administration. 1933, director, seven-province highway construction administration. Commissioner of reconstruction, Fukien provincial government, since February, 1934. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Ts'eh, (陳策)** Courtesy name: Ch'ou-shih 籌碩 Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Chan Chak. Born 1895; native of Chuanshan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Whampoa Military Academy. 1929-31, commander, 4th naval squadron. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since January, 1932. Director of naval matters, national military council, since April, 1934. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Tsung-Ch'eng, (陳宗城)** Courtesy name: Po-p'ing 伯屏 Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: C. S. Chan. Born 1900; native of Canton, Kwangtung. 1925, docteur en droit (Paris). 1925-30, section member, International Labour Office, Geneva. Director, China branch, International Labour Office, since 1930. Author, “Les Concessions en Chine” (doctoral thesis). — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Tu-hsiu, (陳獨秀)** Courtesy name: Chung-fu 仲甫 Born 1879; native of Huaining, Anhwei. Studied in Japan and France. Sometime editor, La Jeunesse magazine (Peiping). 1924, joined Kuomintang, but turned communist. 1925, member, Kuomintang central executive committee, and delegate to Moscow third international. 1928, expelled from Kuomintang. Imprisoned as communist, since October, 1932. Pardoned by government mandate, August 21, 1937. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN, W. C** see below. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Wei-ch'eng, (陳維城)** Courtesy name: Chih-san 秩三 Prefers English rendering: W. C. Chen. Born 1882; native of Wanp'ing, Hopei. Ph.D. (Michigan). Sometime associate editor, China Review (London); secretary, directorate-general of railways; chargé d'affaires in Copenhagen; professor of Chinese, London University. Rose to counsellor of embassy, attached to legation in London, virtually since 1914. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Wen-lin, (陳文麟)** Born 1900; native of Amoy, Fukien. Qualified as an aviator in Europe. 1929, completed leisurely solo flight from Berlin to native city. 1933, accompanied by a mechanic, completed investigation tour on a made-in-China machine along the Yangtze Valley, thence to North China and back to Shanghai. Director, naval aviation bureau, Amoy, since 1931. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Wen-yuan, (陳文淵)** Prefers English rendering: William Y. Chen. Born 1900; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.A., M.A. (Syracuse). 1930, Ph. D. (Duke); student of Prof. William McDougall. Attended Cambridge, Sorbonne and Berlin Universities. Professor of psychology and dean of liberal arts, Fukien Christian University, since 1931. One of the outstanding leaders of the Chinese Christian church, and an eloquent speaker. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CHEN, William Y** see above, — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yao-yuan, (陳耀垣)** Born 1886; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1922, executive secretary, San Francisco branch, Kuomintang headquarters. November, 1930, promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Concurrently vice-chairman, Chinese overseas committee, central Kuomintang headquarters, since April, 1932. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yen-chun, (陳延炯)** Courtesy name: Ti-ch'iu 地球 Born 1895; native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. Sometime director of general affairs, Canton mint and Canton-Kowloon railway; counsellor, ministry of finance; director of general affairs, ministry of railways; president, Tientsin-Pukow railway administration. Managing director, Peiping-Hankow Railway, since 1933. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yi, (陳儀)** Courtesy name: Kung-hsia 公洽 Born 1883; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. November, 1926, civil governor of Chekiang. 1928, director, Shanghai arsenal. 1929, officiating minister of military affairs. 1930-3, administrative, then political vice-minister of military affairs. Chairman, Fukien provincial government, since February, 1934. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yu-jen, (陳友仁)** Born 1878, at Trinidad, S. America, of Cantonese parentage. Educated in England and admitted to bar in London. After practising law in West Indies for some years, returned to China in 1912, and was appointed legal adviser to ministry of communications, Peking. Publisher and editor, Peking Gazette, 1914-6; editor, Shanghai Gazette, 1918-9; founder of Min Pao, Peiping, 1925. Minister of foreign affairs, nationalist government at Canton, 1926 and same post in national government at Wuhan, 1927; after split of Nanking and Wuhan factions, was relieved of post by Nanking government and has since resided in Europe. Joined unsuccessful Fukien independence movement, 1933-4. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yu-kuang, (陳裕光)** Born 1893; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Nanking University. M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia) 1922-5, dean, physics and chemistry department, Peking Higher Normal College. 1926, dean, college of arts and science, Nanking University. President, same university, since September, 1927. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'EN Yuan, (陳垣)** Born 1880; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. 1913, member of parliament. Sometime vice-minister of education; director, sinological institute, Peking National University; dean, history department, Peiping Normal University. President, Fu Jen (Catholic) University, Peiping, since 1932. Well-known historian. Author (in Chinese), “Corrected and supplemented texts of the Code of the Yuan Dynasty” (6 vols.) and “Corresponding Days in the Chinese, European and Mohammedan Almanacs” (5 vols.), as well as numerous monographs. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CHENG, Andrew C. Y** see Ch'eng Chih-yi. — p. 148 ([scan leaf 171](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n171/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Ch'i-pao, (程其保)** Born 1897; native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. A.B., A.M., (Chicago). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1923-5, dean, Southeastern University, Nanking. 1926-8, director, Shanghai College of Commerce. 1929-32, dean, college of education, Central University, Nanking. 1932-3, chairman, educational mission to Europe. Commissioner of education, Hupeh provincial government, since August, 1933. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Ch'ien, (程潛)** Courtesy name: Sung-yun 頌雲 Born 1882; native of Fengling, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1926, commander, nationalist 6th army; garrison commander, Kiukiang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Chief of general staff, since March, 1936. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Chih-yi, (誠質怡)** Prefers English rendering: Andrew C. Y. Cheng. Born 1898; son of Ch'eng Chin-yi. B.A. (Hartford). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). S.T.M. (N. Y. Union Theo. Sem.). 1927-33, assistant professor, school of religion, Yenching University. Professor of religion, Nanking Theological Seminary, since September, 1933. Author, “Hsuntsu's Theory of Human Nature and Its Influence on Chinese Thought” (doctoral thesis), and (in Chinese) “Introduction to the Literature of the New Testament.” — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Chin-yi, (誠靜怡)** Born 1870; native of Hopei. Educated in England. Sometime pastor of the London Mission; general secretary, National Christian Council of China. General secretary, general assembly, Church of Christ in China, since 1928. “During the last twenty years Dr. Cheng's duties have taken him all over the country. Everywhere he has been welcomed, not only because of his sound learning, his thorough understanding of the situation in China and his statesmanlike leadership in the Christian movement, but also because of his unusual ability as a speaker and the beauty of his diction.” — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG, F. T** see Cheng Tien-hsi. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Hsi-keng, (程錫庚)** Courtesy name: Lien-shih 蓮士 Prefers English rendering: S. G. Cheng. Born 1894; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. B.Sc., D.Sc. (London). 1930-4, Nanking manager, Continental Bank. 1935, inspecting commissioner of foreign affairs for Hopei, Shansi and Jehol provinces. Expert member, ministry of foreign affairs, since March, 1936. Author, “Modern China: A Political Study.” — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG Hua, (鄭華)** Courtesy name: Fu-hua 輔華 Prefers English rendering: P. H. Cheng. Born 1888; native of Fukien. M. C. E. (Michigan). Ph.D. (Cornell). 1929-30, assistant chief engineer, Peiping-Liaoning railway; dean, Chiaotung University, Tangshan. Senior engineer and chief, planning division, ministry of railways, since 1931. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG Hung-nien, (鄭洪年)** Courtesy name: Shao-chiue 韶寬 Born 1874; native of Panyu, Kwangtung. Chief secretary, ministry of communications of provisional government at Nanking, 1912; vice-minister of communications, Peking, 1913; vice-minister of finance, nationalist government, Nanking, 1927; political vice-minister of industry, commerce and labour. 1928; president, China University, Chenju, Shanghai. 1927-34. Member of the Legislative Yuan since 1934. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG Lai, (鄭萊)** Prefers own anglicized rendering: Loy Chang. Born 1888; native of Taishan, Kwangtung. M.A. (Harvard). 1928-33, director of public loans, ministry of finance, Transferred director, customs administration. same ministry, since March, 1935. Director, national state lottery administration, June, 1933-March, 1935. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG Lieh, (鄭烈)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-yün 曉雲 Prefers to sign himself: L. Tsen. Born 1887; native of Foochow, Fukien. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1921, officiating president, supreme court, Canton military government. 1922, president, Foochow high court. Chief procurator, supreme court, Nanking, since December, 1928. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG, P. H** see Cheng Hua. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG, Very Rev. Peter** see Ch'eng Yu-t'ang. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG, S. G** see Cheng Hsi-keng. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Shih-k'uei, (程時煃)** Courtesy name: T'ung-lu 桐盧 Born 1889; native of Kiangsi. M. A. (Columbia). 1927, director of elementary and secondary education, ministry of education. 1930-2, commissioner of education, Fukien provincial government. Transferred to similar post, Kiangsi provincial government, since December, 1932. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG T'ien-fang, (程天放)** Born 1899; native of Hsinchien, Kiangsi. B.A. (Fuhtan). M.A. (Illinois). Ph.D. (Toronto). 1928, counsellor, examination yuan. 1929-33, commissioner of education, Anhwei and Hupeh provincial governments. 1932, chancellor, Chekiang University, Hangchow. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Ambassador to Germany since November, 1935. Author, “Oriental Immigration into Canada” (doctoral thesis). — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG T'ien-hsi, (鄭天錫)** Courtesy name: Fu-t'ing [illegible]廷 Prefers English rendering: F. T. Cheng. Born 1884; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. LL.B., LL.D. (London). Barrister-at-law. Middle Temple. 1932-4, administrative, then political vice-minister of justice. Special commissioner to 1935 London international exhibition of Chinese art. June, 1936, nominated successor to Dr. Wang Chung-hui as judge on Permanent Court of International Justice. Author, “Private International Law Relating to the Capacity to Contract” (doctoral thesis), “Supreme Court Decisions” and “Prize Court Judgments.” — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CHENG Tsung-hai, (鄭宗海)** Born 1892; native of Haining, Chekiang. B.A. (Wisconsion). M.A. (Columbia). Sometime professor of education, Southeastern University; dean. college of education, Central University. Professor of education, Chekiang University (Hangchow), since 1929. Translator of several literary and educational works. — p. 149 ([scan leaf 172](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n172/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Yen-ch'iu, (程艶秋)** Born 1898; native of Hopei. Famous female impersonator, regarded as second only to Mei Lan-fang. Visited Europe (1933) for six months, studying dramatic art. Plays usually in Peiping and Shanghai before crowded houses. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Yu-t'ang, (成玉堂)** Better known as the Very Rev. Peter Ch'eng. Born 1876; native of Shansi. Studied at Luan Seminary. 1903-10, ordained as priest; professor of Chinese literature, Luan Seminary. 1911-27, rector of St. Anthony's College, Kiangcheng. 1928-31, rector of St. Peter's College, Hungtung. Promoted prefect apostolic of Hungtung (Shansi), since May, 932. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CH'ENG Yuen-fan, (程遠帆)** Courtesy name: Wan-li 萬里 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: K. V. Dzung. Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B.A. (Washington). Sometime attaché, Chinese legation in Washington; commissioner of finance, Nanking city government. Commissioner of finance, Chekiang provincial government, since January, 1936. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIA Shih-yi, (賈士毅)** Courtesy name: Kuo-po 果伯 Born 1888; native of Ihsing, Kiangsu. Graduate, Meiji University, Tokyo. 1928-32, director of taxation, ministry of finance. Commissioner of finance, Hupeh provincial government, since February, 1933. Author of a financial history of the Chinese Republic, in two volumes. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Chung-cheng, (蔣中正)** Courtesy name: Chieh-shih 介石 Better known as Chiang Kai-shek. Born 1886; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy and Tokyo Military Staff College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1923, chief of staff to Generalissimo Sun Yat-sen, and delegate to Soviet Russia to study its military system. 1924, founder and principal, Whampoa Military Academy. 1926, generalissimo, northern expedition. April, 1927, established Nationalist Government in Nanking, after suppressing the communists in Shanghai area etc. August, 1927, resigned. January, 1928, persuaded to resume command of northern expedition. October, 1928-December, 1931, president, National Government. October, 1928-November 1935, state councillor of the National Government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. Chairman, national military council, since February, 1932. Promoter (March, 1934), New Life Movement. President, Executive Yuan, since December, 1935. Concurrently vice-chairman, Kuomintang central executive committee's standing committee, and vice-chairman, Kuomintang central political council. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Heng-yuan, (江恆源)** Born 1886; native of K'unyuen, Kiangsu. Graduate, Peking Government University. General secretary, National Association of Vocational Education, since 1930. Author of several literary and educational works. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG I-p'ing, (江一平)** Prefers English rendering: Eugene Y. B. Kiang. Born 1898; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1922, B.A. (Fuhtan). 1923, LL.B. (Soochow). 1935, Hon. LL.D. (Fuhtan). Legal practitioner in Shanghai since 1934. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1934. Editor, China Law Review (1923-4). Son-in-law of Yu Ya-ching. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Kai-shek** see Chiang Chung-cheng. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG K'ang-hu, (江亢虎)** Prefers own rendering: Kiang Kang-hu. Born 1883; native of Shangho, Kiangsi. M.A. (Imperial examinations). Honorary D.Litt. (California). 1912-3, founder and leader, Social-Democratic party of China. 1914-20, professor of Chinese subjects, California University. 1930-3, professor of Chinese studies, McGill University (Montreal). Consultant in Chinese, Library of Congress, (Washington, D.C.) since 1930. Confucian scholar. Promoter (1935), Association of Chinese and Foreign Cultural Relations, Shanghai. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Meng-lin, (蔣夢麟)** Courtesy name: Chao-hsien 兆賢 Prefers English rendering: Monlin Chiang. Born 1884; native of Yuyao, Chekiang. B.A. (California). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1927, commissioner of education, Chekiang provincial government. 1928-30, minister of education. Chancellor, National Peking University, since December, 1930. Author, “A Study in Chinese Principles of Education” (doctoral thesis). — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG, Monlin** see above. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Po-ch'eng, (蔣伯誠)** Native of Chekiang. Administrative member, Chekiang provincial government, since 1930. General Chiang Kai-shek's official representative in North China, since 1931. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG T'ing-fu, (蔣廷黻)** Prefers English rendering: T. F. Tsiang. Born 1895; native of Paoch'ing, Hunan. A.B. (Oberlin). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1923-9, professor of history, Nankai University, Tientsin. 1929-35, professor and head, history department, Tsing Hua University. 1930-35, managing editor, Chinese Social and Political Science Review (Peiping). Director of political affairs, Executive Yuan, since December, 1935. Appointed ambassador to U.S.S.R., August 25, 1936. Author, “Labour and Empire” (doctoral thesis) and “Select Documents in Chinese Diplomacy.” — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Ting-ling, Miss, (蔣丁玲女士)** Widow of Hu Yieh-p'ing 胡也頻夫人 Native of Linfeng, Hunan. Writes under pseudonym of “Ting Ling” (丁玲). Recognized as leading lady writer of modern fiction. Co-promoter, defunct “Red and Black Magazine.” Author (in Chinese), “In the Dark” (1928); “A Suicide's Diary” (1929); “Wei Hu” (1930); and “Water” (1932). From sentimentalist has turned progressive revolutionist. 1934, imprisoned as communist; released after six months. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Ting-wen, (蔣鼎文)** Born 1898; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1927, chief of staff, Nationalist 4th army. 1930, commander, 16th division; distinguished himself along Lung-Hai railway in suppression of Feng-Yen revolt. 1931-2, commander, 9th army. 1933, commander, Yangtze defences; commander-in-chief for suppression of Fukien revolt. Pacification commissioner, concurrently Generalissimo Chiang Kai-shek's representative, Fukien province, since February, 1934. — p. 150 ([scan leaf 173](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n173/mode/1up))

**CHIANG Tso-pin, (蔣作賓)** Courtesy name: Yü-yen 雨[illegible] Prefers to sign himself: Tsiang Tso-ping. Born 1884; native of Yingcheng, Hupeh. graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1916, vice-chief of general staff. 1928-31, minister to Germany and Austria. September, 1931-November, 1935, minister, then ambassador, to Japan. Minister of interior, since December, 1935. Translator of Japanese infantry drill manual. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CHIAO I-t'ang, (焦易堂)** Born 1880; native of Wukung, Shensi. Graduate, Shensi Law College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime member of parliament. 1929-35, legislative m mber, Legislative Yuan. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1932. President, supreme court, since November, 1935. Concurrently president, National Institute of Native Physicians, since December, 1935. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CHIEH-TSEH-JE-CHEN-HU-T'U-K'EH-T'U, (結澤熱振呼圖克圖)** Better known as Hutukhtu, or “Living Buddha,” of Outer Tibet, to distinguish him from the Panshen Lama, “Living Buddha” of Inner Tibet. Elected temporal ruler of Tibet, since January, 1934, to govern until a new Dalai Lama is found and enthroned. Endowed with “high spiritual and moral gifts,” and “proved to be the suitable leader for Tibet during the drawing of lots held before the image of the Supreme Buddha.” — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN Ch'ang-chao, (錢昌照)** Courtesy name: I-li 乙藜 Born 1898; native of Ch'angshu, Kiangsu. Educated in Oxford and London Universities. 1927, secretary, ministry of foreign affairs. 1928-30, senior secretary, National Government headquarters. Administrative vice-minister of education, since March, 1931. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN P'ei-yu, (錢沛猷)** Courtesy name: Po-han 伯涵 Born 1896; native of Kiangyin, Kiangsu. Studied at Tsing Hua College; graduate, Customs College, Peking, and University of Missouri, U.S.A. Represented Yi Shih Pao of Tientsin at Second World Press Congress, Honolulu, and Disarmament Conference, Washington, 1921-2; associate director, Yi Shih Pao, Tientsin, 1923; contributing editor, China Weekly Review, Shanghai, 1923-6; assistant editor, North China Star, Tientsin, 1924; secretary and editor, Shun Pao, Shanghai, 1927-35; concurrently professor of journalism at Kwong Hua and Fu Tan Universities; Chinese press officer, Shanghai municipal council, 1935-6; now with Central News Agency, Shanghai. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN Ta-chun, (錢大鈞)** Courtesy name: Mu-yin 慕尹 Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Graduate, Tokyo Military Cadets School. 1925, commander, Nationalist 1st division. 1926-8, commander, 32nd army; defence commissioner, Shanghai and Woosung. Commander, 13th army, since 1929. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN T'ai, (錢泰)** Courtesy name: Chieh-p'ing 楷平 Prefers to sign himself: Tsien Tai. Born 1887; native of Chiashan, Chekiang. Docteur en droit, Paris. 1921-8, director, treaty department, ministry of foreign affairs. 1929-31, counsellor, Judicial Yuan. 1932, counsellor, ministry of justice. Minister to Spain since September, 1933. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN T'ien-huo, (錢天鶴)** Courtesy name: An-t'ao 安濤 Born 1895; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. M.S.A. (Cornell). Sometime principal, Chekiang Provincial College of Agriculture; professor of agriculture, Nanking University; director, metropolitan museum of natural history, Academia Sinica; commissioner of agriculture and mining, Chekiang provincial government. Assistant director, national agricultural research bureau, ministry of industries, since 1933. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN Tsung-tseh, (錢宗澤)** Courtesy name: Mu-lin 慕霖 Born 1890; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1928-30, deputy managing director, Tientsin-Pukow Railway. Managing director, Lung-Hai Railway, since 1931. Concurrently administrative, then political vice-minister of railways (1932-4). — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IEN Yung-ming, (錢永銘)** Courtesy name: Hsin-chih 新之 Born 1885; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Kobe Commercial College. 1927, vice-minister of finance, Nanking. 1927-9, commissioner of finance, Chekiang provincial government. President, Shanghai branch, Joint Savings Society of Kincheng, Salt, Continental, and China and South Seas Banks, since 1929. Councillor, Shanghai French municipal council, since April, 1936. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CH'IN Fen, (秦汾)** Courtesy name: Chin-yang 景陽 Born 1887; native of Chiating, Kiangsu. B.A., M.A. (Harvard). 1928, director of higher education, Central University, Nanking. 1929-35, director, accounting department, ministry of finance, then administrative vice-minister of finance. Secretary-general, national economic council, since August, 1933. Author of textbooks on mathematics and astronomy. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CHIN Pao-shan, (金寶善)** Courtesy name: Ch'u-chen 楚珍 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: P. Z. King. Born 1893; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. M.D. (Chiba, Japan). Director of medical service, national health administration, since October, 1928. Author, “Report on Public Health Administration in the United States”. Chief editor, Chinese section, National Medical Journal of China (1927-8). — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CHIN T'ing-sun, (金廷蓀)** Born 1884; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Managing director of native bank in Ningpo trading under his own name. Vice-president, Jen Chi Hospital, Ningpo. Director of several banks in Shanghai, also of China Merchants Steam Navigation Co. Philanthropist. General agent for national state lottery administration. — p. 151 ([scan leaf 174](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n174/mode/1up))

**CHIN Tsung-ch'eng, (金宗城)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: T. Z. King. Born 1895; native of Chenhai, Chekiang. Sometime chief accountant, Kiangsu Bank. Assistant manager, Shanghai Commercial and Savings Bank, since 1925. Elected member, Shanghai municipal council land committee, since October, 1935. Director of various companies and societies. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHIN Wen-ssu, (金問泗)** Courtesy name: Shun-ju 純儒 Prefers English rendering: Wunsz King. Born 1892; native of Kiangsu. LL.B.. (Peiyang). Hon. LL.D. (Fuhtan). 1930, commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai. 1931, counsellor, ministry of industries. December, 1931, administrative vice-minister of foreign affairs. Minister to Holland (first appointed 1930), since October, 1933. Concurrently delegate, League of Nations assembly, since January, 1934. Author, “Dr. V. K. Wellington Koo's Foreign Policy.” — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CH'IN Yun-ch'ing, (秦潤卿)** Courtesy name: Tsu-tseh 祖澤 Prefers anglicized rendering: Zing Zungching. Born 1878; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. Manager, Foh Yu Native Bank, for nearly 30 years. President, Shanghai native bankers guild. Committee member, Shanghai municipal council, 1928-33. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**“CHINA'S GHANDI,”** see Chu Cheng. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**“CHINESE GRETA GARBO,”** see Hu Tieh. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**“CHINESE LINDBERGH,”** see Chang Hui-ch'ang — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHING Heng-yi, (經亨頤)** Courtesy name: Tzu-yuan 子淵 Born 1879; native of Shangyu, Chekiang. 1929, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. November, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHIU, Ngio** see Ch'ou Ao. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHIU, Teng H** see Chou Ch'eng-yu. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CH'OU Ao, (仇鰲)** Courtesy name: Yi-shan 亦山 Born 1879; native of Siangyin, Hunan. Prefers own anglicized rendering: Chiu Ngio. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime director of civil affairs, Hunan provincial government. 1928-9, counsellor, National Government headquarters. Political vice-minister, ministry of personnel, Examination Yuan, since January, 1930. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Ch'eng-yu, (周澄郁)** Prefers anglicized Amoy rendering: Teng H. Chiu. Born 1900; native of Amoy, Fukien. Studied eleven years in U.S.A. and Europe. Associate, Royal Society of British Artists. “His most amazing achievement took place in London (1931) when he held two individual exhibitions which were commented upon most favourably by the leading critics, who hailed him as the most promising young painter of the generation.” Painter in Shanghai, since 1933. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Ch'i-kang, (周啓剛)** Courtesy name: Chueh-yung 覺庸 Born 1889; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. 1911-27, active Kuomintang worker in Indo-China, Central America and West Indies. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Vice-chairman, Chinese overseas commission, since May, 1932. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Chih-jou, (周至柔)** Born 1898; native of Linhai, Chekiang. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Instructor, Whampoa Military Academy, Canton. Served on general staff, 21st division; chief of staff, 11th division; regimental commander, 21st division, 1927; brigadier-general, 21st division, 1927; transferred brigadier-general, 11th division, 1930; vice-commander and later commander, 4th division; vice-commander, 18th army. Toured world studying aviation, 1933, and after return, 1934, appointed commandant, Central Aviation School, Shien Chiao, Hangchow. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Chi-shih, (周濟世)** Better known as the Right Rev. Joseph Chow. Born 1892; native of Chengting, Hopei. Studied at Chengting Seminary. 1915, became Lazarist. 1919-28, ordained as priest; professor of Latin and Chinese literature, Chengting Seminary. 1929-30, professor of philosophy, Shala Seminary (Peiping). Consecrated titular bishop of Cratia and vicar apostolic of Paoting (Hopei), since March, 1931. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Chun-yien, (周駿彦)** Courtesy name: Chen-ch'in 枕琴 Born 1872; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. Studied in Japan. 1924, commissariat chief, Whampoa Military Academy. 1927, salt commissioner for Chekiang province; administrative member, same provincial government. 1932, administrative member, and concurrently commissioner of finance, same provincial government. Director, commissariat administration, ministry of military affairs, since 1934. 2nd class Paoting decoration. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Fu-hai, (周佛海)** Born 1897; native of Hunan. B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime dean, college of commerce, Wuhan University; director, political training department, Central Military College, and directorate-general of military training. Commissioner of education, Kiangsu provincial government, since 1932. Author (in Chinese) of “Basic Problems of the Three People's Principles” and “Fundamentals of Economics.” — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU, Henry H. C** see Chou Hsueh-chang. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Hsiang-hsien, (周象賢)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Z. Y. Chow. Born 1890; native of Chekiang. B.S. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). 1928, mayor of Hangchow. 1930, counsellor, ministry of finance. 1931-3, chairman, Yangtze River Commission. Reappointed mayor of Hangchow, since February, 1934. Concurrently administrative member, Chekiang provincial government, since December, 1934. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Hsin-t'ang, (周星棠)** Born 1877; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Sometime high adviser to military governor of Hupeh. 1923-8, chairman, Hankow chamber of commerce. Owner of several banks.
