Most influential business leader in Wuhan. — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Hsueh-chang, (周學章)** Prefers English rendering: Henry H. C. Chou. Born 1893; native of Tientsin, Hopei. 1919, B. A. (Oberlin). 1920, M.A.; 1923, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1923, instructor of Chinese, Columbia Teachers' College. 1924, professor of education, Amoy University. 1925, professor and acting president, Hopei University. Professor of education, Yenching University, since 1926. Concurrently dean, college of arts, same institution, since 1932. Author of “Measurement of Composition Ability.” — p. 152 ([scan leaf 175](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n175/mode/1up))

**CHOU Jen, (周仁)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ching 子競 Born 1892; native of Nanking. M.Sc. in mechanical engineering (Cornell). Sometime dean of studies, Nanyang College (now Communications University), Shanghai; dean, engineering college, Central University, Nanking. Director, research institute of engineering, Academia Sinica, since 1930. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOU Keng-sheng, (周鯁生)** Born 1888; native of Changsha, Hunan. M.A. (Edinburgh). Docteur en droit (Paris). Sometime professor and dean, political science department, Peking Government University; legislative member, Legislative Yuan; dean, political science department, Central University. Dean and professor, political science department, Wuhan University (Wuchang), since 1933. Author of several treatises on international law, history of modern Europe, and political science. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOU Tso-jen, (周作人)** Brother of Chou Shu-jen; born 1887. Professor of Chinese, National University of Peking, since 1918. Acknowledged by many as real master of prose essay. Together with his brother, has collected and translated a number of stories from Japan, Russia, Poland and Southern Europe. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOU Tso-min, (周作民)** Prefers to sign himself: Chow Tso-ming. Born 1882; native of Huaian, Kiangsu. Studied in Japan. Managing director, Kincheng Banking Corporation, since 1918. Organizer (1923), powerful combine of Kincheng-Salt-Continental-China and South Sea Banks' Joint Savings Society. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOU Yi-ch'un, (周詒春)** Courtesy name: Chi-mei 寄梅 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Y. T. Tsur. Born 1883; native of Anhwei. B.A. (Yale). M.A. (Wisconsin) Hon. D.Litt. (St. John's and Yenching). Chinshih of imperial examinations. Sometime president, Tsing Hua College; manager, Chung Foo Union Bank, Peiping. 1924-8, executive director, China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture. September, 1933-August, 1934, president, Yenching University. Administrative vice-minister of industries, since December, 1935. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOU Yueh-jan, (周越然)** Better known by his anglicized rendering: Tseu Yih-zan. Born 1884; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B.A. (Nanking). At present vice-principal, Commercial Press correspondence school. Elected committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1936. Best known for his successful series of English Readers, royalties on which are estimated at $20,000 a year. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOW, Right Rev. Joseph** see Chou Chi-shih. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOW Lin** see Tsou Lin. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOW Lu** see Tsou Lu. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOW Tso-ming** see Chou Tso-min. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOW, Z. Y** see Chou Hsiang-hsien. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHOY, J. K** see Ts'ai Tseng-chi. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Ch'ang-nien, (諸昌年)** Born 1898; native of Shanghai. Sometime counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs. 1929-35, minister to Sweden and Norway. 1936, adviser, ministry of foreign affairs. Appointed superintendent of customs, Shanghai, since July 26, 1936. Son-in-law of ex-premier Tang Shao-yi. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Cheng, (居正)** Courtesy name: Chueh-sheng 覺生 Born 1876; native of Kuangchi, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Law College; member, T'ung Meng Hui. Founder, Kwang Hua Daily News (Rangoon). 1912, acting minister of interior. 1913, senator. 1919-22, director of general affairs, reorganized Kuomintang party. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1924. President, Judicial Yuan, since January, 1932. Sometimes referred to, by his admirers, as “China's Ghandi.” — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Chi-ch'ing, (朱霽青)** Born 1880; native of Peichen, Liaoning. Studied in Japan. 1923-4, counsellor, generalissimo's headquarters, Canton. 1928, state councillor of the Nationalist Government. 1933, executive member, commission on Mongolian and Tibetan affairs. 1934-5, managing director, Cheng-Tai railway. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931 (first elected 1925). Active supporter of the volunteers in Manchuria. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Chia-hua, (朱家驊)** Courtesy name: Liu-hsien 騮先 Born 1893; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Ph. D. (Berlin). 1917-26, professor of physics, Peking Government University. 1928-30, commissioner of civil affairs, Chekiang provincial government. 1931, chancellor, Central University, Nanking. 1932, minister of education. 1932-5, minister of communications. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Chairman, British Boxer Indemnity fund administration, since March, 1931. Secretary-general, Academia Sinica, since May, 1936. Chairman, Chekiang provincial government, since December, 1936. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Ch'ing-lan, (朱慶瀾)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'iao 子橋 Born 1875; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Sometime civil governor of Canton; chief civil administrator, Harbin special district. Indefatigable relief worker. Most active in raising subscriptions for the volunteer forces in Manchuria and Jehol. 1935-6, chairman, famine relief commission. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Ching-nung, (朱經農)** Prefers English rendering: King Chu. Born 1887; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. M.A. (George Washington). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1926-7, vice-president, Kwang Hua University; commissioner of education, Shanghai city government. 1927-31, departmental director, then administrative vice-minister of education. 1931-3, president, Cheeloo (Shantung Christian) University. Commissioner of education, Hunan provincial government, since August, 1932. Editor, “Chinese Encyclopædia of Education.” — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Chun-yi, (朱君毅)** Prefers English rendering: Jennings P. Chu. Born 1889; native of Chiangshan, Chekiang. B.A. (Johns Hopkins). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1928-32, professor and head, educational psychology department, Amoy University; director of compilation, Legislative Yuan. Co-director, directorate of statistics, comptroller-general's office, since January, 1934. Concurrently dean and professor of accounting and statistics, Central Political Institute, Nanking. Author and translator. — p. 153 ([scan leaf 176](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n176/mode/1up))

**CHU Hsi-tsu, (朱希祖)** Courtesy name: T'i-hsien 逷先 Born 1879; native of Haiyien, Chekiang. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. 1916-26, dean, Chinese department, Peking Government University. Professor of history, Tsing Hua University, since 1927. Research fellow, Academia Sinica, since 1928. Author of treatises on ancient Chinese history. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Huo-hsiang, (朱鶴翔)** Courtesy name: Feng-ch'ien 鳳千 Prefers French rendering: Louis Ngao-siang Tchou. Born 1888; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. B.A., LL.B., doctor in politics and diplomacy (Louvain). 1916-28, counsellor, then director of political affairs, ministry of foreign affairs. 1929-30, adviser, Peiping-Tientsin garrison commander's headquarters, 1932-5, director, international affairs department, ministry of foreign affairs. Minister to Belgium since September, 1935. Author, “Le Régime des Capitulations et le Régime constitutionnel en Chine” (doctoral thesis). — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU K'ai-min, (朱開敏)** Better known as Right Reverend Simon Tsu. Born 1868; native of Shanghai. Family was converted three hundred years ago. September, 1888, joined the Society of Jesus. 1898-1923, ordained as priest; missionary. 1924, superior of Haimen and Tsungming districts. Consecrated at Rome as titular bishop of Lesbos and vicar apostolic of Haimen (Kiangsu), since August, 1926. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU K'e-chen, (竺可楨)** Courtesy name: Ou-fang 藕舫 Born 1890; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. B.Sc. (Illinois). M.Sc. and Ph.D. (Harvard). 1920-25, lecturer on meteorology and geodesy, Southeastern University. 1926, editor, Commercial Press, Shanghai. 1927, head, geodesy department, Central University. 1928-35, director, research institute of meteorology, Academia Sinica. President, Chekiang University, Hangchow, since March, 1936. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU, Jennings P** see Chu Chun-yi. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU, King** see Chu Ching-nung. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CH'U Min-yi, (褚民誼)** Born 1890; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. M.D., B.Sc. (Strassbourg). Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1928, promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. 1932-5, secretary-general, Executive Yuan. Keen calisthenist. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Pao-yuan, (朱葆元)** Courtesy name: T'ui-yü 退愚 Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: P. N. Tsu. Born 1870; native of Shanghai. 1900, B.D. (St. John's). 1914, M.A. (Columbia). 1923, D.D. (St. John's). Pastor, Church of Our Saviour, Shanghai, since 1904 (being first independent Chinese church, Kiangsu district, since 1916). Author, “Geography of China,” and (in Chinese) “Christ's Sermon on the Mount and Orientalism,” “The Lord's Prayer and Orientalism,” and “New Messages from Old Texts.” — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU, P. K** see Chu Shao-p'ing. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU, Percy** see Chu Po-ch'uan. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Po-ch'uan, (朱博泉)** Prefers to sign himself: Percy Chu. Born 1898; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1928-32, chief auditor, Central Bank, Shanghai. General liquidator, Russo-Asiatic Bank in China, since 1929. Manager, joint reserve board, Shanghai Bankers Association, since November, 1932. President, Shanghai Rotary Club (1934-5). — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Shao-liang, (朱紹良)** Courtesy name: I-min 一民 Commander, 32nd army, since 1929. 1933-5, chairman, Kansu provincial government. Pacification commissioner for Kansu, Ninghsia and Chinghai, since May, 1933. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Shao-p'ing, (朱少屏)** Prefers English rendering: P. K. Chu. Born 1881: native of Shanghai. General secretary, World's Chinese Students' Federation, Shanghai, since 1912. Business manager, China Critic, since 1928. Vice-director, history compilation bureau, city government of Greater Shanghai, since July, 1932. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU, T. C** see Chu T'ing-ch'i. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU T'ing-ch'i, (朱庭祺)** Courtesy name: T'i-jen 體仁 Prefers English rendering: T. C. Chu. Born 1886; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. B.S. (Harvard). 1928-9, director, accounting department, ministry of finance. Director, chief inspectorate of salt revenue, since October, 1929. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Wen-ch'i, (諸文綺)** Born 1884; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Miyaoka polytechnic institute. Sometime senior instructor, Kiangsu provincial industrial school; director, consolidated tobacco tax bureau, Chekiang province; executive member, Shanghai general chamber of commerce. General manager, Ch'i Ming Dyeing Works (Shanghai), since 1920. Principal, Nanyang Commercial Middle School (Shanghai), since 1933. Committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1935. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Yu-ts'ang, (朱育滄)** Courtesy name: Mou-ch'eng 懋澄 Prefers to sign himself: Moutchen Thomas Tchou. Born 1895; native of Tzechow, Szechwan. B.Sc. (Glasgow). 1927-30, director, labour department, ministry of industries. 1929-31, senior delegate, international labour conference, Geneva. General secretary, Association for Economic and Social Advancement of China (Shanghai), since January, 1934. Member, Shanghai municipal council's rickshaw board of three, May, 1934-December, 1936. — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Yu-yu, (朱友漁)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Y. Y. Tsu. Born 1887; native of Shanghai. B.A. (St. John's). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1924-32, secretary, religious and social work department, Peiping Union Medical College. 1933-4, visiting lecturer, Pacific School of Religion (Berkeley); secretary, National Christian Council (Shanghai). Reappointed professor of sociology, concurrently university secretary for the alumni, St. John's University, Shanghai, since October, 1934. Author, “The Spirit of Chinese Philanthropy.” Co-author, “China through Chinese Eyes.” — p. 154 ([scan leaf 177](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n177/mode/1up))

**CHU Yuan-chih, (朱沅芷)** Prefers Cantonese rendering: Chu Yun-gee. Born 1902; native of Canton. 1921, went to California, studied painting under Otis Oldfield, well-known modern painter, and soon was admired as one of the modern painters of San Francisco. 1925, went to Paris, and two years later held his first European exhibition at the Carmine Galleries, where he sold most of his paintings. After three successful years in Europe, he returned to San Francisco, to do research work. “His rise in the art world has been meteoric, and, in spite of his youth, he has already become internationally known as one of the most outstanding painters of to-day.” — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**CHU Yun-gee** see above. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**CHUNG Ao, (鍾鍔)** Born 1890; native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. M.A. in elec. engin. (Wisconsin). Sometime chief consulting engineer in China of U.S. International Telegraph and Telephone Co. 1931-3, director-general of telegraphs, then counsellor, ministry of communications. 1934-6, manager, Bank of Communications, Tientsin branch. Hankow manager, Central Bank, since July, 1936. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**CHUNG Wing-kwong** see below. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**CHUNG Yung-kuang, (鍾榮光)** Prefers Cantonese rendering: Chung Wing-kwong. Born 1880; native of Kwangtung. Sometime commissioner of education, Canton. Promoted president, Lingnan (Canton Christian) College, since 1927. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**“DEFENDER OF JEHOL,”** see Sun K'uei-yuan. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**“DEFENDER OF KUPEIKOW,”** see Hsu T'ing-yao. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**“DEFENDER OF SHANHAIKWAN,”** see Ho Chu-kuo. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**DJANG Tsu-ping** see Chang Tzu-p'ing. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**DJANG, Y. S** see Chang Yuan-shan. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**DOON Dayu** see Tung Ta-yu. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**DZUNG, K. V** see Ch'eng Yuen-fan. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**DOU Yu-seng** see Tu Yung. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**EN-K'E-PA-T'U, (恩克巴圖)** Courtesy name: Tzu-yung 子榮 Born 1889; native of Chahar. Graduate, College of Mongolian and Tibetan Languages. Sometime member of parliament. 1929-31, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. State councillor the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN Heng-an, (樊恆安)** Better known as the Right Rev. Joseph Fan. Born 1887; native of Suiyuan. Consecrated at Rome (by Pope Pius XI) titular bishop of Paphos and vicar apostolic of Tsining (Suiyuan), since June, 1933. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN Hsiang-li, (樊象離)** Courtesy name: Hsü-hsin 虛心 Born 1880; native of Chieh-hsien, Shansi. Graduate, Shansi Law College, Taiyuan. Sometime member, Shansi provincial assembly. 1926, commissioner of police, Shihmen. 1928-9, administrative vice-minister of interior. 1934, director, North Shansi mining bureau. Director of village administration, Shansi provincial government, since March, 1935. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN, Right Rev. Joseph** see Fan Heng-an. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN, Robert** see below. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN Wen-chao, (范文照)** Prefers English rendering: Robert Fan. Born 1893; native of Canton. B.Sc. (St. John's). B. Arch. (Michigan). 1926, third prizeman for designs of Sun Yat-sen's memorial auditorium, Canton. 1928, second prizeman for designs of Sun Yat-sen's mausoleum, Nanking. Architect in Shanghai, since 1928. Founder and first president, Society of Chinese Architects. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FAN Yu-sui, (范予遂)** Born 1894; native of Chu-ch'eng, Shantung. Graduate, Peking Higher Normal College. 1927, editor-in-chief, Min Kuo Jih Pao, Hankow. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Managing director, Taokow-Chinghwa Railway, since April, 1932. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FANG Chi-fan, (方積蕃)** Courtesy name: Chiao-po 椒伯 Born 1884; native of Chenhai, Chekiang. 1920, director, Shanghai mint. 1922, vice-chairman, general chamber of commerce, Shanghai. General manager, Chinese General Insurance Company (Shanghai), since February, 1933. Director of many industrial and shipping concerns in Shanghai and Chenhai. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FANG Chueh-hui, (方覺慧)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'iao 子樵 Born 1888; native of Chinch'un, Hupeh. Graduate, Waseda University, Tokyo. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1929-34, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Supervisory commissioner for Control Yuan in Honan and Shantung provinces, since March, 1935. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FANG Chun-pi, Miss, (方君璧女士)** Wife of Tseng Chung-min 曾仲鳴夫人 Graduate, Paris Institute of Fine Arts. Sometime professor of fine arts, Chungshan University, Canton. Well-known painter, favourably received by art circles in Europe and U.S.A. Regarded as one of most promising women painters in China. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FANG Pen-jen, (方本仁)** Courtesy name: Yao-t'ing 耀廷 Born 1880; native of Huangkang, Hupeh. Graduate, Peking Military College. Sometime military governor of Kiangsi; commander, 11th army, northern expedition. 1929-30, chairman, Hupeh provincial government. Senior counsellor, military advisory council, since 1931. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FENG Chan-hai, (馮占海)** Courtesy name: Shou-shan 壽山 Born 1898; native of P'ohsien, Liaoning. Graduate, Mukden Military Institute. 1928, commander, Kirin military governor's body-guards. 1932-3, active organizer of volunteers' forces in Manchuria. Commander, 91st division, since April, 1933. — p. 155 ([scan leaf 178](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n178/mode/1up))

**FENG Yu-hsiang, (馮玉祥)** Courtesy name: Huan-chang 煥章 Born 1880; native of Ch'ao-hsien, Anhwei. 1922, military governor of Honan. 1924, imprisoned President Ts'ao K'un and installed Tuan Ch'i-jui as provisional chief executive. 1926, retired. 1927, returned from Russia. 1928-9, vice-president, Executive Yuan, concurrently minister for military affairs. June, 1929, expelled from Kuomintang and dismissed from all posts. September, 1929, pardoned. April, 1930, reordered to be arrested for abetting Yen Hsi-shan's revolt. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Reappointed state councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Threatened peace of North China (summer, 1933) when, emerging temporarily from Taishan (Shantung), he endeavoured to rally his former subordinates and “provoke” the Japanese east of Chahar. Vice-chairman, national military council, residing in Nanking, since January, 1936. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FENG Yu-lan, (馮友蘭)** Prefers to sign himself: Fung Yu-lan. Born 1890; native of Honan. Ph.D. (Columbia). Sometime dean of liberal arts, Chung Chow University (Kaifeng)[illegible] 1927-32, professor of philosophy, Tsing Hua University. Dean, college of arts, same institution, since 1933. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**“FLOUR KING” OF CHINA** see Yung Tsung-ching. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FONG Foo Sec** see Kuang Fu-shao. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FOO Ping-sheung** see Fu Ping-ch'ang. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU Ju-lin, (傅汝霖)** Courtesy name: Mu-po 沐波 Born 1895; native of Anta, Heilungkiang. 1931-3, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. 1934, administrative vice-minister of interior. Chairman, Yangtze River commission, since March, 1935. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU Ping-ch'ang, (傅秉常)** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Foo Ping-sheung. Born 1896; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. LL.D. (Hongkong). March, 1929, appointed minister to Belgium. 1931, vice-minister of foreign affairs, Canton government. Legislative member, concurrently chairman, foreign relations committee, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1928. Principal drafter of Chinese civil code. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU Shang-lin, (傅尚霖)** Courtesy name: Ti-li 廸黎 Prefers English rendering: Shang-ling Fu. Born 1902; native of Swatow, Kwangtung. B.A. (Shanghai). Litt.D. (London). 1929-30, dean and professor of sociology, Fukien Christian University. 1930-2, professor of sociology, Tsing Hua University. 1932-3, president, Anglo-Chinese College, Tientsin. Professor of sociology, Chungshan University (Canton), since 1934. Author, “The Chinese Family” and “Some Aspects of the Chinese Family Life,” etc. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU, Shang-ling** see above. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU Ssu-nien, (傅斯年)** Courtesy name: Meng-chen 孟眞 Born 1896; native of Liaoch'eng, Shantung. Graduate, Peking Government, London and Berlin Universities. Sometime professor of literature, Chungshan University, Canton. Director, research institute of history and philology, Academia Sinica, since 1928. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU Tso-yi, (傅作義)** Courtesy name: I-sheng 宜生 Born 1895; native of Shansi. 1911, joined T'ung Meng Hui. 1917, graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1926, promoted division commander. 1928-30, garrison commander, Tientsin. 1931, commander, reorganized 7th army, and concurrently commander, 73rd division. Chairman, Suiyuan provincial government, since August, 1931. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FU T'ung, (傅桐)** Born 1887; native of Lanfeng, Honan. Studied in Tokyo, Birmingham and Oxford universities. Sometime president, Northwestern University, Sian. 1928-33, dean, philosophy department, National University of Peking. President, Anhwei University (Wuhu), since January, 1934. Editor, Philosophical Journal. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**FUNG Yu-lan** see Feng Yu-lan. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**GOCKSON, William** see Kuo Shun. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HAN Fu-chu, (韓復榘)** Courtesy name: Hsiang-fang 向方 Born 1890; native of Pahsien, Hopei. Originally one of Feng Yü-hsiang's ablest generals. 1928, chairman, Honan provincial government. 1929, sided with Nanking to crush Feng Yü-hsiang's revolt. 1930, commander, 1st army. Chairman, Shantung provincial government, since September, 1930. Strict disciplinarian, is a terror to his subordinates. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HAN Li-wu** see below. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HANG Li-wu, (杭立武)** Prefers own rendering: Han Li-wu. Born 1902; native of Ch'uhsien, Anhwei. B.A. (Nanking). M.A. (London). 1930, chief compiler, examinations commission, Examination Yuan. 1931, dean, political science department, Central University; special research fellow, Academia Sinica. General secretary, British Boxer Indemnity fund administration, since April, 1932. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HO Ch'eng-chun, (何成濬)** Courtesy name: Hsueh-chu 雪竹 Born 1882; native of Suihsien, Hupeh. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1927, high adviser, Generalissimo's headquarters. 1928, director, military affairs department, National Government headquarters. 1929-32, chairman, Hupeh provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Pacification commissioner for Hupeh Province, since April, 1932. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HO Chien, (何健)** Courtesy name: Yün-ch'iao 芸樵 Born 1887; native of Liling, Hunan. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1927, commander, Nationalist 35th army. 1928, commander, reorganized 6th division, 4th group army. Chairman, Hunan provincial government, since April, 1929. Concurrently commander, 4th route army. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HO Chu-kuo, (何柱國)** Born 1897; native of Yunghsien, Kwangsi. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy, and Tokyo Military Cadets School. 1927, garrison commander, Kaifeng (Honan); concurrently commander, 23rd division. 1933, “Defender of Shanhaikwan,” as commander, 9th brigade, reorganized north-eastern defence force. Commander, 57th army, since June, 1933. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HO, Franklin L** see Ho Lien. — p. 156 ([scan leaf 179](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n179/mode/1up))

**HO Hao-jo, (何浩若)** Courtesy name: Meng-wu 孟吾 Born 1899; native of Siangtan, Hunan. B.A. (Stanford). M.A., Ph.D. (Wisconsin). Graduate, Norwich Military College. Sometime director of political affairs, West Hunan bandit suppression headquarters. Commissioner of finance, Hunan provincial government, since 1935. Specially commended in a National Government mandate, March, 1936, for his efficient management of Hunan provincial government's finances, — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Kuo-kuang, (賀國光)** Courtesy name: Yuan-ch'ing 元靖 Born 1885; native of P'uch'i, Hupeh. Graduate, Peiping Military College. 1926, garrison commander, Wuhan. 1929-32, deputy director-general of military training. Assistant departmental director, national military council, since April, 1932. Chief aide-de-camp, communist-suppression headquarters (Chungking), since January, 1935. Now acting director, Generalissimo's headquarters at Chnngching, Szechwan. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Lien, (何廉)** Courtesy name: Chün-lien 淬廉 Prefers English rendering: Franklin L. Ho. Born 1897; native of Paoch'ing, Hunan. B.A. (Pomona). Ph.D. (Yale). 1926-30, professor of finance and statistics, Nankai University, Tientsin. Director, institute of economics, same institution, since 1930. Author, “Income Taxation; A Comparative Study of Its Administrative Machinery and Procedure” (doctoral thesis), and “A Weekly Index Number of the Cost of Living in Tientsin, 1926 to Date,” etc. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Ping-sung, (何炳松)** Born 1890; native of Chinhua, Chekiang. B.A. (Wisconsin). M.A. (Princeton). 1917-22, professor of history, Peking Government University and Higher Normal College; principal, first normal school, Hangchow. 1929-35, editor-in-chief, then assistant manager, Commercial Press, Shanghai. President, Chi Nan University (Chenju), since September, 1935. Author, “Outline of Historical Methodology,” etc. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Shih-chen, (何世楨)** Born 1892; native of Chekiang. Studied in Japan. Ph. D. (Michigan). 1929, president, Shanghai provisional court. President, Ch'ih Tze College, Shanghai, since 1930. Jan.-June, 1932, political vice-minister of justice. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Ssu-yuan, (何思源)** Courtesy name: Hsien-ch'a 仙槎 Born 1895; native of Hotze, Shantung. B. A. (Beloit). M.A. (Chicago). 1926, editor-in-chief, Canton Gazette. Commissioner of education, Shantung provincial government, since May, 1929. Author, “International Economic Policy, with Special Reference to China.” — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Sui, (何遂)** Courtesy name: Hsü-fu 敍甫 Born 1887; native of Fukien. Graduate, Peking Army College. Sometime army commander; director, aviation bureau, Canton; acting principal, Whampoa Military Academy. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1934. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Teh-k'uei, (何德奎)** Born 1896; native of Chinhua, Chekiang. B.A. (Wisconsin). M.B.A. (Harvard). Sometime dean of commerce, Kwang Hua University, and head, economics department, Shanghai Law College. Assistant secretary, Shanghai municipal council, April, 1931. Promoted deputy secretary, June, 1937. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO T'ing-jan, (何挺然)** Prefers English rendering: T. J. Holt. Born 1892; native of Canton. Graduate, St. John's University, Shanghai; instructor, Hangchow Christian College, Hangchow, and Y. M. C. A. school, Shanghai. Entered motion picture business in 1919, starting Isis Theatre, Shanghai; promoter, Peking and Nanking Theatres in Shanghai, State Theatre in Nanking and two others in Canton. Now general manager, Shanghai Amusement and Shanghai South Amusement Cos. and vice-president, Shanghai United Amusements, Inc; special delegate, ministry of industries, to America and Europe to study motion picture business. Christian, rotarian, 32nd degree mason and shriner. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Yao-tsu, (賀耀祖)** Courtesy name: Kuei-yen 貴嚴 Born 1889; native of Ninghsiang, Hunan. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1928, commander, 3rd army. 1930-1, director, military affairs department, National Government headquarters. 1932-4, vice-chief of general staff. Minister to Turkey, since April, 1935. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. 1st class Paoting military decoration. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HO Ying-ch'in, (何應欽)** Courtesy name: Ching-chih 敬之 Born 1889; native of Hsingi, Kweichow. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1928, chairman, Chekiang provincial government. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government; concurrently director-general of military training. Minister of military affairs, since March, 1930. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HOH, Gunsun** see below. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HOH Keng-sheng, (郝更生)** Prefers English rendering: Gunsun Hoh. Born 1899; native of Huaian, Kiangsu. 1923, bachelor of physical education (Springfield). 1924-9, professor of physical education, Soochow, Tsing Hua, and National Normal Universities. 1929-31, physical director, Northeastern University. 1932-3, physical director, Shantung University, Tsingtao. Superintendent of physical education, ministry of education, since November, 1933. Author, “Physical Education in China.” Editor-in-chief (1927-31), Physical Education. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HOLT, T. J** see Ho Ting-jan. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HOO, Victor Chi-tsai** see Hu Shih-tseh. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HORN, Jean Y. Z** see Hsiang Chung-yung. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HOW, Bang** see Hsia P'eng. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HSI Ch'ao-chun, (郗朝俊)** Courtesy name: Li-ch'in 勵勤 Born 1882; native of Huayin, Shensi. LL.B. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime commissioner of education, Sian; president, Shensi high court. 1929-31, judge, supreme court. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1932. Author of three volumes on penal laws and legal principles. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HSI, T. C** see Hsi Teh-chun. — p. 157 ([scan leaf 180](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n180/mode/1up))

**HSI Teh-chun, (席德炯)** Courtesy name: Ming-chiu 鳴九 Prefers English rendering: T. C. Hsi. Born 1894; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B. S. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). M. S. (Columbia). 1924-7, chief secretary, then chief of stores division, Hanyehping Co. 1931, chief, engineering and labour division, national flood relief commission. Director, hydraulic engineering bureau, national economic council, since March, 1932. Concurrently director, Kianghan engineering bureau (Hankow), since 1935. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSI Yu-shu, (奚玉書)** Prefers English rendering: Yulin Hsi. Born 1900; native of Shanghai. Chartered accountant. Sometime professor of commerce in Fuhtan, Chi Nan and other universities in Shanghai; director, national association of chartered accountants, and executive member of Chinese ratepayers association. Elected councillor, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1935. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSI, Yulin** see above. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIA, C. L** see below. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIA Chin-lin, (夏晋麟)** Prefers English rendering: C. L. Hsia. Born 1894; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. B.Sc. (Glasgow). M.A., Ph.D. (Edinburgh). 1927-31, president, Medhurst College, Shanghai. 1931-3, first secretary, legations in London and Washington,D.C. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1934. Author, “Treaty Relations between China and Great Britain” (doctoral thesis) and “The Status of Shanghai.” Co-translator, “New Chinese Civil Code.” — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIA P'eng, (夏鵬)** Prefers to sign himself: Bang How. Born 1896; native of Shanghai. B.S. (Pennsylvania). Director and manager, sales department, then supplies department, Commercial Press, Shanghai, since 1926. Director, International Dispensary, Shanghai, etc. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIANG Chung-yung, (項仲雍)** Prefers own anglicized rendering: Jean Y. Z. Horn. Born 1901; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Graduate, Tsing Hua University. Sometime manager, Joint Savings Society (Shanghai) sub-branches in Hongkew and western suburb, and Hankow. Sub-manager, realty department, same savings society, since 1934. Concurrently managing director, International Hotel Co. (Park Hotel), Shanghai. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Cheng-ying, (蕭振瀛)** Courtesy name: Hsien-ke 仙閣 Born 1890; native of Fuyu, Kirin. Graduate, Kirin Provincial Law College. Sometime member of parliament; mayor of Sian; member, Peiping political council; acting chairman of Chahar provincial government. November, 1935-May, 1936, mayor of Tientsin. Member, Hopei-Chahar political council, since November, 1935. Reported to have spent $100,000 in two-day celebration of his parents' 67th birthday (April, 1936) and to have received in return 50,000 presents. As General Sung Cheh-yuan's most trusted aide, has been conspicuous in conducting the Japanese negotiations for his chief. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Chi-san, (蕭吉珊)** Born 1892; native of Chaoyang, Kwangtung. Graduate, Canton Normal College. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since November, 1931. Concurrently chief secretary, same committee, since January, 1934. Appointed administrative member, Kwangtung provincial government, since July 20, 1936. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Chi-yung, (蕭繼榮)** Born 1892; native of Fuch'uan, Kwangsi. Graduate, Berlin University. Sometime chargé d'affaires, Berne; secretary, ministry of foreign affairs. 1932, counsellor, Chinese assessor's office, Lytton commission of inquiry. 1932-3, counsellor, legation in Paris. Transferred counsellor to legation in Angora, since September, 1935. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO En-ch'eng, (蕭恩承)** Prefers English rendering: Theodore E. Hsiao. Born 1898; native of Hunan. 1920, B.A. (Yale-in-China). 1921, M.A. (Columbia). 1924, Ph.D. (New York). 1927, commissioner for foreign affairs, Changsha. Professor of education and dean, department of foreign languages, National University of Peking, since October, 1930. Author, “The History of Modern Education in China” (doctoral thesis). — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Fu-ch'eng, (蕭佛成)** Courtesy name: T'ieh-ch'iao 鐵橋 Born 1862; native of Fukien. Sometime proprietor and editor of a Chinese daily in Siam; chairman, Siam branch, Kuomintang headquarters. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1926. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Feng** see Yeh Ch'u-ts'ang. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO, K. C** see below. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Kung-ch'uan, (蕭公權)** Prefers English rendering: K. C. Hsiao. Born 1897; native of Kiangsi. B.A., M.A. (Missouri). Ph.D. (Cornell). 1927-32, professor at Nankai and Yenching Universities. Professor of political science, Tsing Hua University, since September, 1932. Managing editor, Chinese Social and Political Science Review (Peiping), since September, 1934. Author, “Political Pluralism: A Study in Contemporary Political Theory” (doctoral thesis). — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO, Theodore E** see Hsiao En-ch'eng. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIAO Yu-mei, (蕭友梅)** Born 1884; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). Ph.D. (Leipzig). 1925-7, dean, department of music, Peiping College of Fine Arts. Dean, then principal, National Conservatory of Music (Shanghai), since October, 1927. Author of several volumes on the general theory of music and outline of harmony, etc. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Chien, (謝健)** Courtesy name: Chu-ch'en 鑄陳 Born 1881; native of Yungch'ang, Szechwan. LL.B. (Tokyo). Sometime district magistrate, Huangpi (Hupeh); president, Shanghai district court. 1928-34, senior secretary,National Government headquarters. Administrative vice-minister of justice, since December 1934. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Ch'ih, (謝持)** Native of Kwangtung. Leader, Kuomintang “Western Hills” group. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1926. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIEH, P. C** see Hsieh Pao-ch'iao. — p. 158 ([scan leaf 181](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n181/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Pao-ch'iao, (謝保樵)** Prefers English rendering: P. C. Hsieh. Born 1896; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. 1923, Ph.D. (Johns Hopkins). 1927-8, director, tobacco and wine tax bureau, Kiangsu. 1928-31, senior secretary, ministry of railways; managing director, Canton-Kowloon railway. Director, bureau of legislative research, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1933. Author, “Government of China, 1644-1911” (doctoral thesis). — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Shou-ch'ang, (謝壽昌)** Courtesy name: Kuan-sheng 冠生 Prefers French rendering: Sié Cheou-tchang. Born 1897; native of Chenghsien, Chekiang. Docteur en droit (Paris). 1927-30, secretary, ministry of foreign affairs. Counsellor, concurrently secretary-general, Judicial Yuan, since May, 1930. Founder and editor-in-chief, Chung Hua Fa Hsueh Tsa Chih (China Law Journal), since 1930. Author, “Esquisse d'une Histoire du Droit Chinois” (doctoral thesis). — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Shou-k'ang, (謝壽康)** Prefers French rendering: Cheou-kang Sié. Born 1894; native of Kanchow, Kiangsi. Sc.D. in economics (Paris). 1930-1, chargé d'affaires at Brussels. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Member, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs, since January, 1935. Wrote “The Broken Jade,” which was staged at Brussels Royal Theatre and favourably received. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Sung-kao, (謝頌羔)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Z. K. Zia. Born 1895; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. B.A. (Soochow). M.A. (Boston). Secretary, Christian Literature Society, Shanghai, since 1926. Author, “The Confucian Civilisation”. Translator of numerous foreign authors, including E. Stanley Jones. Editor, The Shining Light and The People's Magazine, Shanghai. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Wan-ying, Miss, (謝婉瑩女士)** Wife of Wu Wen-ts'ao 吳文藻夫人. Pen name: Ping-hsin 冰心. Born 1902; native of Ch'anglo, Fukien. B.A. (Yenching). M.A. (Wellesley). Instructor in Chinese, Yenching University, Peiping, since September, 1926. Lecturer in Chinese, Peiping Women's College and Tsing Hua University. Author (in Chinese), “The Stars,” “Spring Water,” “Superman and Other Stories,” etc., and translator of K. Gibran's “The Prophet.” — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIEH Yuan-fu, (謝元甫)** Prefers English rendering: George Y. Char. Born 1887; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B.S. (Boone). M.D. (St. John's and Harvard Medical School). 1918-9, resident urologist, New York hospitals. May, 1927-April, 1930, medical director, Peiping Central Hospital. Professor of surgery, Peiping Union Medical College, since 1930. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIN Shu-chih, (辛樹幟)** Born 1896; native of Changsha, Hunan. Studied in England. 1929-31, professor of biology, Chungshan University, Canton. Counsellor, Examination Yuan, and concurrently director, national bureau of publications, since 1932. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG Ch'ung-chih, (熊崇志)** Prefers English rendering: Samuel S. Young. Born 1884; native of Kwangtung. B.A. (California). M.A. (Columbia). Chinshih of imperial examinations. June, 1927-June, 1931, consul-general at New York. July, 1931-August, 1933, minister to Mexico. Minister to Brazil, since September, 1933. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG Hsi-ling, (熊希齡)** Courtesy name: Ping-san 秉三 Born 1867; native of Fenghuang, Hunan. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. Sometime premier and minister of finance; director-general, Grand Canal improvement board. Executive member, national famine relief commission, since 1929. Founder, orphanage at Hsiang Shan, Peiping Western Hills. President, International Swastika Society. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG K'e-wu, (熊克武)** Born 1882; native of Szechwan. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. Sometime defence commissioner, Chungking, and civil governor of Szechwan. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG Pin, (熊斌)** Courtesy name: Cheh-ming 哲明 Born 1893; native of Huangan, Hupeh. Graduate, Peking Military College. 1928, vice-minister of war. 1929, director, of aviation, ministry of military affairs. 1932-4, director, general affairs department, board of general staff. Promoted vice-chief of general staff, since May, 1934. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSIUNG Shih-hui, (熊式輝)** Courtesy name: T'ien-yi 天翼 Born 1892; native of Anyi, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1926, commander, 37th division, nationalist 13th army. September, 1928-November, 1931, commander, 5th division; concurrently, garrison commander, Shanghai and Woosung. Chairman, Kiangsi provincial government, since December, 1931. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSU Chi-ch'in, (徐寄[illegible])** Prefers English rendering: C. M. Shu. Born 1880; native of Chekiang. Sometime director, central mint; chairman, Chinese ratepayers association, Shanghai, Feburary, 1932, deputy governor, Central Bank, Managing director, National Commercial Bank, Shanghai, since 1933. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSU Chien-p'ing, (許建屏)** Courtesy name: Yien-fan 彥藩 Prefers English rendering: Jabin Hsu. Born 1889; native of Chekiang. B. A. (Michigan). Sometime chief sub-editor, China Press (Shanghai). 1928-31, senior secretary, ministry of industries. Director of general affairs, ministry of finance, since November, 1933. Concurrently general secretary, National Child Welfare Association. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSU Ch'ung-chih, (許崇智)** Native of Kwangtung. Sometime commissioner of civil affairs, Kwangtung provincial government. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1926. Vice-president, Contro Yuan, since December, 1935. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSU Ch'ung-ch'ing, (許崇清)** Courtesy name: Chih-ch'eng 志澄 Native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). April, 1935-July, 1936, vice-chairman, examinations commission, Examination Yuan. Reappointed commissioner of education, Kwangtung provincial government, since July 20, 1936. — p. 159 ([scan leaf 182](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n182/mode/1up))

**HSU Ch'ung-hao, (許崇灝)** Courtesy name: Kung-wu 公武 Native of P'anyü, Kwangtung. Graduate, Nanking Military Institute. 1928-31, secretary-general, Examination Yuan. Reappointed to same post, since January, 1933. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Chung-nien, (徐仲年)** Prefers to sign himself: Sung-nien Hsu, Born 1904; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. 1930, Litt.D. (Lyons). 1930-1, publications director, Labor University, Shanghai. Professor of literature, Central University, since 1932. Editor, literary supplement of Min Sheng Pao (Nanking). Author of several volumes in French and Chinese discussing literary topics. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Fu, (徐桴)** Courtesy name: Sheng-shan 聖禪 Native of Chenhai, Chekiang. 1916, chief statistician, Canton civil governor's office. 1926, chief auditor, northern expedition commander-in-chief's headquarters. 1928-9, again 1934-5, commissioner of finance, Fukien provincial goverment. Commissioner of finance, city government of Greater Shanghai, since May, 1936. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Hsiao-ch'u, (許曉初)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-ch'u 孝雛 Born 1900; native of Shouhsien, Anhwei. 1922, B. Econ. (Fuhtan). Director of various companies, schools and associations; executive member, Chinese ratepayers association. Elected committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1936. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Hsin-liu, (徐新六)** Courtesy name: Cheng-fei 振飛 Prefers English rendering: Singloh Hsu. Born 1890; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. B.Sc. (Birmingham). B.Com. (Manchester). General manager, National Commercial Bank, since 1926. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1929. Chairman, Taishan Insurance Co. Ltd., since August, 1932. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, I-min, (徐逸民)** Prefers English rendering: W. Imin Hsu. Born 1890; native of Canton. B.A. (St. John's); M.D., same university, 1919; M.M.Sc. (Pennsylvania) 1922. Associate professor of urology, St. John's University, Shanghai, 1922-30; genito-urinary surgeon, St. Luke's Hospital, Shanghai, 1922-30; visiting urologist, Red Cross Hospital, Shanghai, 1922-5; consulting urologist, Orthopedic Hospital, Shanghai, 1928-30; professor of urology, St. John's University, 1934. Member, Shanghai Medical Society, Chinese Medical Association, rotarian, mason and shriner. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Jabin** see Hsu Chien-p'ing. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU K'an, (徐堪)** Courtesy name: K'e-t'ing 可亭 Born 1887; native of Sant'ai, Szechwan. Sometime director, museum of commercial exhibits, Peiping. 1928-35, director, currency department, ministry of finance. Promoted administrative vice-minister of finance, since May, 1935. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Leonard S** see Hsu Shih-lien. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Mo, (徐謨)** Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. LL.B. (Peiyang). M.A. (George Washington); 1922-6, professor of law and dean of arts, Nankai University, Tientsin. 1927, judge, Shanghai provisional court; chief judge, Sungkiang district court. 1928-31, director, Euro-American affairs and Asiatic affairs departments, ministry of foreign affairs; part time concurrently commissioner for foreign affairs, Shanghai. Administrative, then political vice-minister for foreign affairs, since February, 1932. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Paul H** see below. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU P'ei-huang, (徐佩璜)** Courtesy name: Chün-t'ao 君陶 Prefers English rendering: Paul H. Hsu. Born 1888: native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B.S. (Mass. In. Tech.). 1923-7, professor ank dean, middle school, Nanyang University. 1928-31, counsellor, then commissioner of education, city government of Greater Shanghai. Commissioner of public utilities, same city government, since December, 1932. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Pei-hung, (徐悲鴻)** Prefers French rendering: Ju-Péon. Born 1894; native of Ihsing, Kiangsu. Celebrated at age of 20 as modern artist. 1918, returned from Japan; professor of fine arts, Peking National University. 1919-26, studied painting in Europe; invited to exhibit his works at various salons in Paris. Professor of fine arts, Central University, Nanking, since 1928. “Exponent of realism and opposed to futurist as well as mercenary art; critic of conventional landscapes and advocate of Hellenistic art.” Most of his works are collected in book form. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Shan-hsiang, (徐善祥)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Z. Z. Zee. Born 1882; native of Shanghai. Ph.B. (Yale). Ph.D. (Columbia). Director, central industrial laboratory, ministry of industries, since 1929. Editor of scientific textbooks. Author of “1-2-3 System” adopted for standardizing weights and measures. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Shih-lien, (許仕廉)** Prefers English rendering: Leonard S. Hsü. Born 1901; native of Siangtan, Hunan. B.A. (Stanford). M.A., Ph.D. (Iowa). 1926-31, professor and dean, department of sociology and social work, Yenching University. Counsellor, ministry of industries, since 1933. Concurrently vice-director, agricultural division, national economic council, since 1934. Author, “Political Philosophy of Confucianism” (doctoral thesis) and “Political Principles of Sun Yat-sen.” — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Shih-ying, (許世英)** Courtesy name: Chin-jen 靜仁 Born 1872; native of Chiupu, Anhwei. 1910-2, chief justice, supreme court; minister of justice. 1916, minister of interior and communications. 1921, civil governor of Anhwei. 1925-6, premier; acting minister of finance. 1928-35, chairman, national famine relief commission. Ambassador to Japan, since February, 1936. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Showin Wetsen** see Hsu Wei-chen. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Shuhsi** see below. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Shu-hsi, (徐淑希)** Prefers English rendering: Shuhsi Hsu. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. Ph.D. (Columbia). Professor and dean, political science department, Yenching University, since 1925. Author, “China and Her Political Entity” (doctoral thesis) and brochures entitled “Questions relating to Manchuria” and “The Manchurian Dilemma—Force or Pacific Settlement,” etc. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU, Singloh** see Hsu Hsin-liu. — p. 160 ([scan leaf 183](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n183/mode/1up))

**HSU Sung-ming, (徐誦明)** Born 1890; native of Chekiang. Graduate of Tokyo Imperial University. 1919-26, professor of medicine, College of Medicine,Peking. 1927, section chief, army medical service, northern expedition. 1928-32, dean, college of medicine, National Peiping University, Acting president, same university, since December, 1932. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU, Sung-nien** see Hsu Chung-nien. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU Ti-shan, (徐狄善)** Born 1888: native of Canton. One of the first graduates of Yenching University, took up postgraduate work in Columbia and Oxford Universities. 1917-35, professor of history and philosophy, Yenching, Tsing Hua, and National Peking Universities. Chairman of Chinese department, Hongkong University, since January, 1936. Author (in Chinese) of “A History of Taoism” and several short stories concerning social life among small-town citizens. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU T'ing-hu, (徐廷瑚)** Courtesy name: Hai-fan 海帆 Born 1889; native of Yihsien, Hopei. M.Sc. (Paris). 1925, vice-director, Peiping Agricultural College. 1928, acting president, Hopei University. Director, department of agriculture, ministry of industries, since 1928. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU T'ing-yao, (徐庭瑤)** Courtesy name: Yueh-hsiang 月祥 Born 1890; native of Wuwei, Anhwei. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Probably one of ablest generals in Chinese army; “never met defeat once in his years of active service in the field.” “Defender of Kupeikow,” refused to yield this strategic gateway along the Great Wall in 1933, until driven out by superior Japanese armament. 1934-5, toured Europe and U.S.A. to study military conditions. Director of military transport, directorate-general of military training, since May, 1935. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU, W. Imin** see Hsu I-min. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU Wei-chen, (徐維震)** Courtesy name: Hsü-ying 旭瀛 Prefers English rendering: Showin Wetsen Hsu. Born 1881; native of Tunghsing, Chekiang. LL.B. (Indiana). Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1927-31, president, Shanghai provisional court. 1933-5, legal adviser, Central Bank. Reappointed president, Kiangsu high court, second branch (Shanghai), since January, 1936. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU Yung-ch'ang, (徐永昌)** Born 1892; native of Shansi. One of Yen Hsi-shan's trusted commanders. Commander, 33rd army, since 1929. 1931-6, chairman, Shansi provincial government. Pacification commissioner, Shansi-Honan-Shensi borders, since May, 1936. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSU Yung-tso, (徐永祚)** Courtesy name: Yü-shu 玉書 Born 1890; native of Haining, Chekiang, Graduate, Shen Chow College, Shanghai. Sometime chief accountant, Shanghai Stock Exchange. Chartered accountant, since 1928. Member, Shanghai city government provisional assembly. Author of treatises on accountancy principles and methods. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSUAN T'ung** see P'u Yi. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HSUEH Tu-pi, (薛篤弼)** Courtesy name: Tzu-liang 子良 Born 1892; native of Chienhsien, Shansi. 1924-6, officiating minister of justice; civil governor of Kansu. 1928-9, minister of interior and public health. 1932, state councillor of the National Government. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since July, 1936. Legal practitioner in Shanghai, since January, 1933. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Chia-shao, (胡嘉詔)** Courtesy name: Chien-ju 檢汝 Born 1882; native of Hsingkuo, Kiangsi. C. E. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime engineer, Ssupingkai-Taonan railway (Kirin), and Yu Hua Afforestation Co. (Kiangsu); chief engineer, then director, Kiangsi provincial bureau of highway administration. 1925, inspecting supervisor of road construction, Generalissimo's provisional headquarters in Chungking. Transferred commissioner of reconstruction, Kweichow provincial government, since May, 1936. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Chun, (胡筠)** Courtesy name: Pi-chiang 筆江 Born 1880; native of Yangchow, Kiangsu. Educated in England. General manager and managing director, China and South Sea Bank, Shanghai, since 1926. Concurrently chairman, board of directors, Bank of Communications. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU, Very Rev. Gaspar** see below. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Hsiu-shen, (胡修身)** Better known as the Very Rev. Gaspar Hu. Born 1876; native of Shantung. Studied at Tsinanfu Seminary. 1902, ordained as priest. Promoted prefect apostolic of Lintsing (Shantung) since March, 1931. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Jo-shan, (胡若山)** Better known as the Right Rev. Joseph Hu. Born 1881; native of Tinghai, Chekiang. November, 1906, became Lazarist. June, 1909, ordained as priest. 1909-15, missionary in Chekiang. 1916-25, professor of philosophy and dogmatic theology, Ningpo Theological Seminary. Consecrated at Rome as titular bishop of Theodosiopolis and vicar apostolic of Taichow (Chekiang), since June, 1926. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU, Right Rev. Joseph** see above. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Lin, (胡林)** Courtesy name: Cheng-chih 政之 Born 1893; native of Szechwan. LL.B., (Tokyo Imperial). 1915-7, counsellor, ministry of interior; editor, Ta Kung Pao (L'Impartial), Tientsin. Founder and general manager, Kuo Wen News Agency, Shanghai and Peiping, since 1921. Concurrently, general manager, Ta Kung Pao, Shanghai. — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Shih, (胡適)** Courtesy name: Shih-chih 適之 Born 1891; native of Shanghai. B.A. (Cornell). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1935, Hon. LL.D. (Hongkong). Founder, “Literary Revolution” movement. 1917-26, professor of philosophy, then dean of English literature, Peking National University. 1927-31, professor of philosophy, Kwang Hua University, and president, China Institute (Woosung). Dean, school of literature, National Peking University, and chairman, translating and editing committee, China Foundation for the Promotion of Education and Culture (Peiping), since September, 1931. Elected corresponding member, Prussian Academy of Learning, since July, 1932. Chairman, Shanghai conference, Institute of Pacific Relations (November, 1931). Author “Development of the Logical Method in Ancient China” (doctoral thesis); also in Chinese, “Outline of Chinese Philosophy” and “Ancient History of China.” — p. 161 ([scan leaf 184](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n184/mode/1up))

**HU Shih-tseh, (胡世澤)** Courtesy name: Tzu-tseh 子澤 Prefers English rendering: Victor Chitsai Hoo. Born 1894; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Docteur en droit (Paris). Sometime chargé d'affaires, Berlin. 1930-1, director of Asiatic affairs, ministry of foreign affairs. Chargé d'affaires, then minister to Switzerland, since December, 1931. Author, “The Basic Principles of Sino-Russian Relations” (doctoral thesis in French). — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HU Shu-hua, (胡庶華)** Prefers to sign himself: Schuhua Hu. Born 1886; native of Yuhsien, Hunan. Graduate, Technische Hochschule, Berlin-Charlottenburg, Germany. University professor for many years; commissioner of education of Kiangsu; director of Hanyang arsenal; director of Shanghai Steel Works of the ministry of war; president of National Tung Chi University; member of Legislative Yuan; president of Hunan University; now president of Chungking University, Szechwan. Author of several books on metallurgical engineering. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HU Tieh, Miss, (胡蝶女土)** Wife of Eugene Penn 潘有仁夫人 Better known as Butterfly Wu, or “Chinese Greta Garbo.” Native of Kwangtung. Acknowledged Chinese movie queen, is probably highest paid artiste. Starred in numerous pictures, including “Two Sisters,” a talkie which was shewn for two months in one Shanghai picture-house, and which has received premier honours in Moscow international cinema exhibition. 1935, toured Europe, part time as guest of Soviet movie industry people. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HO Wen-hu, (胡文虎)** Better known as Aw Boon-haw. Born at Yungting, Fukien. Medical expert, druggist and philanthropist. Accumulated vast wealth through his many useful medicines and donated considerable sums of money to government and educational institutions. Also a newspaper man, having established newspapers in Rangoon and Singapore. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HU Wen-yu, (胡韞玉)** Courtesy name: P'u-an 樸安 Born 1877; native of Chinghsien, Anhwei. 1929, technical expert, examinations commission, Examination Yuan. 1930-1, commissioner of civil affairs, Kiangsu provincial government. 1932-4, professor of Chinese literature, Chi Nan University; managing director, Min Pao. Dean, department of literature, China Institute (Woosung), since March, 1935. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Chin-yung, (黃錦鏞)** Courtesy name: Chin-yung 金榮 Born 1868; native of Shanghai. Philanthropist and business magnate. Proprietor, Great World (amusement centre), Chinese Grand Theatre, Crystal Theatre and several other commercial establishments. Decorated by Chinese Government for munificent contributions to flood relief and purchase of aeroplanes. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Hsu-ch'u, (黃旭初)** Born 1893; native of Yunghsien, Kwangsi. Graduate, Peking Military College. Rose to become army commander. 1930, garrison commander, Nanning. Chairman, Kwangsi provinciai government, since 1931. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Pe-tsiau** see below. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Po-ch'iao, (黃伯樵)** Prefers to sign himself: Huang Pe-tsiau. Born 1889; native of Taitsang, Kiangsu. Graduate, Berlin Institute of Technology. 1927, again 1929-32, commissioner of public utilities, Shanghai city government. Managing director, Nanking-Shanghai and Shanghai-Hanchow-Ningpo railways, since December, 1932. Author of a Sino-German dictionary. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Shao-hsiung, (黃紹雄)** Courtesy name: Chi-k'uan 季寬 Born 1895; native of Yunghsien, Kwangsi. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1925, commissioner of civil affairs, Kwangsi province. 1926, chairman, Kwangsi provincial government. 1927-31, commander, 15th army. 1932-4, minister of interior. December, 1934-July, 1936, chairman, Chekiang provincial government. Transferred chairman, Hupeh provincial government, December, 1936. Re-elected reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG, Tsefang F** see below. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Tzu-fang, (黃子方)** Prefers English rendering: Tsefang F. Huang. Born 1899; native of Amoy, Fukien. S.B., S.M., M.D. (Chicago). 1927-9, commissioner of health, Peiping; counsellor, ministry of public health. 1930-2, expert, League of Nations health organization, Geneva. Health adviser, Shanghai-Nanking and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo railways, since April, 1933. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Yen-p'ei, (黃炎培)** Courtesy name: Jen chih 任之 Born 1879; native of Shanghai. Graduate, Nanyang College; studied in Japan. December, 1921, again June, 1922, appointed minister of education, but declined. Chairman, National Association for Vocational Education, since 1918. Editor-in-chief, A Statistical Survey of Shanghai (bilingual), 1933. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUANG Yun-su, (黃芸蘇)** Prefers own rendering: Yuen-su Wong. Born 1888; native of Taishan, Kwangtung. B.A. (Washington). M.A. (Columbia). Organizer, American branch, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime counsellor, Canton nationalist government. 1932, consul-general in San Francisco. Minister to Mexico, since November, 1933. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUNG K'uei, (洪逵)** Courtesy name: Wen-ling [illegible]舲 Born 1885; native of Huaining, Anhwei. B.Sc. (London). Sometime professor and dean of liberal arts, Central University; vice-president and dean of law, Chinan University (Chenju); secretary, city government of Greater Shanghai. Director, Greater Shanghai municipal library, since May, 1936. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUNG Lu-tung, (洪陸東)** Born 1892; native of Huangyen, Chekiang. Graduate, Shansi University (Taiyuan). Active Party worker. Sometime secretary, central Kuomintang headquarters organization committee. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. Political vice-minister of justice, since November, 1934. — p. 162 ([scan leaf 185](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n185/mode/1up))

**HUNG, William** see below. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**HUNG Yeh, (洪業)** Courtesy name: Wei-lien 煨蓮 Prefers English rendering: William Hung. Born 1893; native of Fukien. B.A. (Ohio Wesleyan). M.A. (Columbia). B.D. (N.Y. Theo. Sem.). Hon. D.D. (Ohio Wesleyan). 1922-7, head, history department, Yenching University. Professor of history and theology, same institution, since July, 1927. Editor, Sinological Index Series. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**I-K'AN-JEN** see Kuo Mo-jo. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**ING, Z. T** see Ying Shang-teh. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**JEN Hung-chun, (任鴻雋)** Courtesy name: Shu-yung 叔永 Prefers to sign himself: Zen Hung-chiun. Born 1886; native of Szechwan. B. A. (Cornell). M.A. (Columbia). 1924-7, vice-president, Southeastern University, Nanking. Trustee and executive director, China Foundation for the Promotion of Education and Culture, since January, 1929. President, Szechwan University, Chengtu, since January, 1935. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**JU-PEON** see Hsu Pei-hung. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAH, T. K** see Keh Tsu-k'uang. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAN Chi-yung, (甘績鏞)** Courtesy name: Tien-k'uei 典夔 Native of Szechwan. Graduate, Chengtu Polytechnic Institute. Sometime taoyin of Eastern Szechwan; principal, Eastern Szechwan Normal School; director of political affairs, 21st army. Commissioner of civil affairs, Szechwan provincial government, since December, 1934. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAN Nai-kuang, (甘乃光)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ming 自明 Born 1896; native of Tsench'i, Kwangsi. Graduate, Canton Christian College. Postgraduate work in U.S.A. and Europe. 1927, chairman, Canton municipal government. 1931, re-elected member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1932-4, political vice-minister of interior. Director of Party affairs, Generalissimo's headquarters, Wuchang, since February, 1935. Author of treatises on the history of economic thought before the Ch'in dynasty, and some fundamental problems of the Kuomintang Party, etc. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAO I-han, (高一涵)** Born 1883; native of Liuan, Anhwei. B.A. (Meiji). Sometime dean and professor, political science department, Chungkuo University (Peiping); dean of social sciences, China Institute (Woosung). 1932-3, director, bureau of compilation and translation, national military council. Supervisory member, Control Yuan, since 1933. Concurrently supervisory commissioner for Control Yuan in Hunan and Hupeh provinces, since April, 1935. Author (in Chinese) of a history of European political thought, outline of political science, history of Chinese imperial censorship system, and history of Chinese cabinet system. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAO Lu, (高魯)** Courtesy name: Shu-ch'ing 曙青 Born 1878; native of Ch'anglo, Fukien. Graduate, Brussels University. Sometime director, Peiping and Nanking observatories. October, 1928-November, 1930, minister to France. Supervisory member, Control Yuan, since July, 1931. Author of treatises on astronomy. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KAO T'ing-tzu, (高廷梓)** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Tingtsz Ko. Born 1896; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. B.A. (Peiping). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1927-32, dean, political science department, Chungshan University (Canton); founder and editor-in-chief, ChenPao (Canton). Director, navigation department, Ministry of Communications, since November, 1932.
