Author, “Governmental Methods of Adjusting Labour Disputes in North America and Australasia” (doctoral thesis). — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KEH Ching-en, (葛敬恩)** Courtesy name: Chan-hou 湛侯 Born 1889; native of Kashing, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1928-30, vice-chief of general staff. Vice-director, first department, national military council, since March, 1932. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**K'EH Hsing-o, (克興額)** Courtesy name: Chih-nan 指南 Born 1894: native of Inner Mongolia. Graduate, Ch'engteh (Jehol) Normal College. Sometime member of parliament. Executive member, commission on Mongolian and Tibetan affairs, since February, 1929. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Editor-in-chief, Mongolian and Tibetan Weekly. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KEH Tsu-k'uang, (葛祖爌)** Courtesy name: Tz'u-sun 慈孫 Prefers own anglicized rendering: T. K. Kah. Born 1894; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. B.A. (New York). Sometime vice-consul in Ottawa; chief of personnel section, ministry of foreign affairs. Consul-general in Chicago, since March, 1933. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KIANG, Kang-hu** see Chiang K'ang-hu. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KING, P. Z** see Chin Pao-shan. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KING, T. Z** see Chin Tsung-ch'eng. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KING, Wunsz** see Chin Wen-ssu. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KO, Ting-tsz** see Kao T'ing-tzu. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KOO, S. S** see Ku Shu-sen. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KOO, T. Z** see Ku Tzu-jen. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KOO, V. K. Wellington** see Ku Wei-chun. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KU Cheng-lun, (谷正倫)** Courtesy name: Chi-ch'ang 紀常 Born 1890; native of Anshun, Kweichow. Graduate, Tokyo Military Cadets School. 1927, commander, 1st division, nationalist 40th army. 1928-31, garrison commander, Nanking. February-June, 1932, acting mayor of Nanking. Reappointed garrison commander, Nanking, since February, 1932. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KU Chi-kang, (顧頡剛)** Prefers own rendering: Ku Chieh-kang. Born 1893; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Graduate, National Peking University. Sometime editor, Commercial Press, Shanghai; research professor, Amoy University; professor of history, Chungshan University (Canton). Professor of history, Yenching University, since 1927. Author, “Symposium on Ancient Chinese History (Vols. 1-5). Editor, Chinese Historical Geography (Chinese fortnightly), since 1933. — p. 163 ([scan leaf 186](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n186/mode/1up))

**KU Chieh-kang** see above. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KU Chu-t'ung, (顧祝同)** Courtesy name: Mo-san 墨三 Native of Lienshui, Kiangsu. 1928-9, commander, 1st division. 1929-31, commander, 2nd division. December, 1931-December, 1933, chairman, Kiangsu provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Political vice-minister of military affairs, since January, 1934. Appointed director of Generalissimo's headquarters at Sian, January, 1937. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KU Meng-yu, (顧孟餘)** Born 1888; native of Hopei. Graduate, Berlin University. 1922-5, dean of economics, Peking National University. 1927, director of publicity, central Kuomintang headquarters, Hankow nationalist government. 1929, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1932-5, minister of railways. Transferred minister of communications, since December, 1936. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KU Shu-sen, (顧樹森)** Courtesy name: Yin-t'ing [illegible]亭 Prefers English rendering: S. S. Koo. Born 1886; native of Chiating, Kiangsu. Studied in London University. November, 1928-May, 1930, commissioner of education, Nanking city government. Director of elementary and secondary education, ministry of education, since June, 1930. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KU Tzu-jen, (顧子仁)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: T. Z. Koo. Born 1888; native of Shanghai. Graduate, St. John's College. Hon. Litt. D. (Colgate). Sometime associate general secretary, Y.M.C.A. national committee. Popular lecturer in U.S.A. and England. Special secretary, World Student Christian Federation, since 1930. Author, “Chinese Folk Songs.” — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KU Wei-chun, (顧維鈞)** Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan 少川 Prefers English rendering: V. K. Wellington Koo. Born 1887; native of Chiating, Kiangsu. B.A. (Yale). M.A., Ph. D. (Columbia). Hon. LL.D. (Yale). 1922-4, minister for foreign affairs and acting premier. 1926-7, minister of finance; transferred to foreign portfolio and premier of regency cabinet. December, 1931, minister for foreign affairs. January-September, 1932, Chinese assessor, Lytton commission of inquiry. Minister, then ambassador, to France, since October, 1932: concurrently representative, League of Nations council. Author, “The Status of Aliens in China” (doctoral dissertation). — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUANG Fu-shao, (鄺富灼)** Courtesy name: Yao-si [illegible]西 Better known as Dr. Fong Foo Sec. Born 1869; native of Sunning, Kwangtung; son of a farmer, he went to America at age of 13 as railway labourer. Studied at night school and became a Salvation Army worker. Entered college. B.L. (California); M.A. (Columbia). Professor of English, Canton College of Foreign Languages, 1906; editor-in-chief, English department, Commercial Press, 1908-29. Author of many textbooks in English. Received honorary degree of LL.D. from Pomona College, 1922. For many years, chairman of national committee of Y.M.C.A. of China; connected with many educational and philanthropical institutions; once president of the Shanghai Rotary Club. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUEI Chung-ch'u, (桂中樞)** Prefers English rendering: C. S. Kwei. Born 1897; native of Kaihsien, Szechwan. B.A. (Lawrence). B.J. (Wisconsin). 1925-31, Shanghai sales manager, Paramount Films Inc. Head, surety department, American Asiatic Underwriters (Shanghai) since July, 1932. Managing editor, China Critic (Shanghai), since 1927. Contributing editor, China Press, since July, 1935. Editor, Chinese Year Book (premier issue 1935). — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUNG, A. C** see below. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUNG An-ch'ing, (龔安慶)** Prefers English rendering: A. C. Kung. Born 1891; native of Hofei, Anhwei. B.A. (Cantab.). Sometime secretary, ministry of foreign affairs; consul-general inSan Francisco. Consul-general in Irkutsk (Siberia), since March, 1933. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUNG, H. H** see K'ung Hsiang-hsi. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**K'UNG Hsiang-hsi, (孔祥熙)** Courtesy name: Yung-chih 庸之 Prefers English rendering: H. H. Kung. Born 1881; native of T'aiku, Shansi. 75th descendant of Confucius. B.A. (Oberlin). M.A. (Yale). Hon. LL.D. (Oberlin). 1928-31, minister of industries. 1932-3, state councillor of the National Government; special industrial commissioner to U.S.A. and Europe. Minister of finance, concurrently vice-president, Executive Yuan, since November, 1933. Governor, Central Bank, since June, 1933. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Special envoy to coronation of King George VI in May, 1937. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**K'UNG Hsiang-yung, (孔祥榕)** Born 1888: native of Ch'ufou, Shantung. Graduate, Peking College of Languages. Sometime chief accountant, China Merchants Steam Navigation Company; administrative director, Hankow bureau for inspection and testing of commercial commodities. Chairman, Yellow River flood relief commission, since 1933. Chairman, Yellow River improvement commission, since December, 1935. Author of two volumes on methods for conservancy of Yungting River. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUO Hsin-sung, (郭心[illegible])** Courtesy name: Chung-yueh 仲岳 Native of P'ingyang, Chekiang. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. Sometime professor of law, Chungshan University, Canton. 1930-1, counsellor, Examination Yuan. 1932-3, counsellor, ministry of communications. Director-general of posts, since May, 1934. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUO Mo-jo, (郭沫若)** Original name: K'ai-chen 開貞 Pen name: Tu-yien 杜衍 and I-k'an-jen 易坎人 Born 1893; native of Chiating, Szechwan. Studied medicine in Japan, but did not complete the course. 1924, co-founder, Ts'ang Tsao Sheh (“Creative Society”) and its monthly Ts'ang Tsao Yueh K'an. 1925, dean of arts, Chungshan University, Canton. 1927, resigned from Northern Expedition to devote himself to literary career. Has written ten novels, half a dozen plays, five volumes of poems, six volumes of essays and one dozen translations of German and Russian authors. Editor, The Flood. Known as the fighting poet and novelist, is regarded as premier modern scholar. — p. 164 ([scan leaf 187](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n187/mode/1up))

**KUO, P. W** see below. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO Ping-wen, (郭秉文)** Courtesy name: Hung-sheng 鴻聲 Prefers English rendering: P. W. Kuo. Born 1880; native of Shanghai. Ph.B. (Wooster). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1918-25, president, Nanking Southeastern University. 1925-30, director, China Institute, New York. 1932-3, general secretary, China Institute of Foreign Relations (Shanghai). Director, bureau of foreign trade, ministry of industries, since December, 1935. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO Shun, (郭順)** Prefers English rendering: William Gockson. Born 1882; native of Kwangtung. General manager, Wing On Textile Manufacturing Co., Shanghai, since 1924. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1935. Committee member, same council (1934-5). — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO T'ai-chen, (郭泰楨)** Prefers own rendering: Quo Tai-tsing. Born 1899; younger brother of Quo Tai-chi. B.S. (Pennsylvania). M.B.A. (New York). Sometime commissioner of foreign affairs, Anhwei province; superintendent of customs, Wuhu; advisor on foreign affairs, Shantung provincial government. Director, No. 3 special administrative district (ex-British concession), Hankow, since 1932. Concurrently managing director, Hankow Herald, since 1935. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO T'ai-ch'i, (郭泰祺)** Courtesy name: Fu-ch'u 復初 Prefers to sign himself: Quo Tai-chi. Born 1888; native of Hupeh. B.A. (Pennsylvania). 1929-30, legislative member, Legislative Yuan: 1932, political vice-minister for foreign affairs. Minister to Great Britain, since July, 1932. Promoted ambassador to Great Britain, May, 1935. Concurrently delegate to League of Nations assembly. Deputy envoy to coronation of King George VI in May, 1937. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO Teh-hua, (郭德華)** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: T. W. Kwok. Born 1900; native of Panyü, Kwangtung. B.Sc. (Washington); M.Sc. (Harvard). 1931-2, secretary, ministry of finance; civilian commissioner for retrocession of Japanese evacuated areas. Chinese secretary, Whangpoo conservancy board, Shanghai, since 1930. Secretary, national economic council, since 1934. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO Wei-pai, (郭威白)** Born 1900; native of Lich'uan, Kiangsi. M.A. (Columbia). J.S.D. (New York). Sometime editor-in-chief, Young China Morning Post, San Francisco; professor of law, Chungshan University, Canton. Assistant director, bureau of foreign trade, ministry of industries, since 1934. Author: “A Comparative Study of Constitutions,” “War-time International Law,” and “The Diplomacies of World Powers,” etc. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KUO Yun-kuan, (郭雲觀)** Courtesy name: Min-ch'ou 閔疇 Born 1889; native of Wenchow, Chekiang. LL.B. (Peiyang). Hon. LL. D. (Fuhtan). Postgraduate studies at Columbia University. 1922-5, judge, supreme court. 1926-32, professor of law, and part-time acting vice-president, Yenching University. President, Shanghai first special area district court, since November, 1932. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KWEI, C. S** see Kuei Chung-ch'u. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**KWOK, T. W** see Kuo Teh-hua. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LAMB, Jefferson D. H** see Lin Tung-hai. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LAO Nien-tsu, ([illegible]念祖)** Courtesy name: Ching-hsiu 敬修 Born 1863; native of Huoshan, Kwangtung. Resident of Shanghai for over half a century, is one of leading lights in Cantonese community. Achieved success in banking, insurance, mining and shipping enterprises, also silk and piece goods trades, etc. At present supervisor, general chamber of commerce; director, Nanyang Brothers Tobacco Co., etc. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LAU Tong Sun** see Lou T'ung-sun. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LAW, Yau-tsit** see Lo Yu-chieh. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEAN Yick Chung** see Lin Yi-chung. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, Chinglun Frank W** see Li Ching-lun. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, Edward Bing-shuey** see Li Ping-jui. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, Handel** see Li Han-toh. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, J. S** see Li Ssu-kuang. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, Pin-heng** see Li Ping-heng. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, S. K** see Li Shih-chia. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE Shao-chang** see Li Shao-ch'ang. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE, T. H** see Li Teng-hui. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEE Tso-yu** see Li Tsu-yü. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEI Hai-tsung, (雷海宗)** Courtesy name: Po-lun 伯倫 Born 1902: native of Yungch'ing, Hopei. Ph.B., Ph.D. (Chicago). 1929-31, dean, history department, Central University, Nanking. 1931-2, professor of history, Ginling College for Women, Nanking: then Wuhan University, Wuchang. Professor of history, Tsing Hua University, since September, 1932. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEI P'ei-hung, (雷沛鴻)** Courtesy name: Ping-nan 賓南 Prefers English rendering: Binnan P. Louis. Born 1888; native of Nanning, Kwangsi. B.A. (Oberlin). M.A. (Harvard). Active promoter of “Work and Study” movement among Chinese students abroad. Commissioner of education, Kwangsi provincial government, since 1932 (first appointed, 1926). — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEONG Yew-kok** see Liang Yu-kao. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEUNG, S. C** see Liang Ch'ang-shu. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LEW, Timothy Tingfang** see Liu T'ing-[illegible]ang. — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LI Chao-huan, (黎照寰)** Courtesy name: Yao-sheng [illegible]生 Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: J. Usang Ly. Born 1888; native of Canton. B.S. (Haverford). B.C.S. (New York). M.A. (Columbia). 1928-30, counsellor, then administrative vice-minister of railways. President, Chiaotung University, Shanghai, since September, 1930 — p. 165 ([scan leaf 188](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n188/mode/1up))

**LI Cheng-nan, (李振南)** Born 1898; native of Paoking, Hunan. 1926, M.A.; 1927, Ph.D. (Yale). 1930-4, director, division of economic studies, Statistics Company (New York). Associate director, research department, Bank of China head office, since December, 1934. Author of two text-books on English teaching. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Cheng, (李蒸)** Courtesy name: Yun-t'ing 雲亭 Prefers English rendering: Yuntin Cheng Li. Born 1904; native of Luanchow, Hopei. M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1931, director of social education, ministry of education. Reappointed president, National Teachers College, Peiping, since July, 1932. Author, “A Study of the Organization of the American One-Teacher School” (doctoral dissertation). — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Chi, (李濟)** Courtesy name: Chi-chih 濟之 Born 1896; native of Chunghsiang, Hupeh. Ph.D. in anthropology (Harvard). Sometime professor of anthropolgy, Tsing Hua University. Vice-director, research institute of history and philology, Academia Sinica, since 1929. Well-known anthropologist, his excavations at Anyang (Honan) revealing neolithic villages and towns of the Shang or early Chou period, have persuaded the Académie des Inscriptions et Belles-Lettres of the Institut de France to award the coveted Julean Prize to the Academia Sinica. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Chi-shen, (李濟深)** Courtesy name: Jen-chao 任潮 Born 1886; native of Changwu, Kwangsi. Graduate, Military College of Peking. Chief-of-staff, 22nd division, revolutionary forces, 1911; chief-of-staff, 1st division of Kwangtung army, 1921; defence commissioner of Wuchow, Kwangsi, 1924; commander of 4th revolutionary army, 1924; commander, 8th route revolutionary army and chief-of-staff, headquarters of nationalist revolutionary forces, 1928; member, central executive committee of Kuomintang and of central political council at Nanking, 1927-8; state councillor and chief of general staff of National Government, 1928; relieved of all posts on account of implication in Kwangsi faction revolt at Wuhan, 1928; pardoned, 1931; inspector general of military training, 1932-3; again involved in Fukien independence movement, 1933-4, relieved of all posts by National Government and expelled from Kuomintang. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Ching-lun, (李錦綸)** Prefers English rendering: Chinglun Frank W. Lee. Born 1884; native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. M.A. (New York). LL.B. (Chicago). October, 1928-November, 1929, minister to Mexico. 1930-1, political vice-minister, then officiating minister for foreign affairs. October, 1933-May, 1934, minister to Poland. Minister to Portugal, since June, 1934. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Chun, (李駿)** Courtesy name: Hsien-chang 顯章 Prefers French rendering: Li Tchuin. Born 1891; native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. 1917, supervisor of Chinese war labourers in France. 1922, second secretary, legation in Paris. 1927, consul-general in Singapore. 1929, consul-general in Ottawa. 1933, consul-general in Paris. Minister to Peru, since March, 1934. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Chung-kung, (李仲公)** Born 1889, native of Kweichow. Graduate, Peking Law College. Studied in Japan. 1927-9, commissioner of civil affairs, Kweichow provincial government. 1928-30, political vice-minister of communications. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Commissioner of finance, Kweichow provincial government, since April, 1935. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI, H** see Li Hsieh. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Han-toh, (李漢鐸)** Prefers English rendering: Handel Lee. Born 1886; native of Kiangning, Kiangsu. B.Th. and D.D. (Boston). M.Th. and Ph.D. (Drew). 1916-21, pastor, Methodist church, Wuhu. 1923-31, pastor, same church, Nanking. President, Nanking Theological Seminary, since May, 1931. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Hsieh, (李協)** Courtesy name: Yi-chih 儀祉 Prefers English rendering: H. Li. Born 1882; native of P'uch'eng, Shensi. Graduate, Charlottenburg and Dantzig polytechnic institutes. Engineer-in-chief, Hwai River conservancy commission, since 1929. Concurrently chairman (1929-35), Yellow River improvement commission. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Lieh-chun, (李烈鈞)** Courtesy name: Hsieh-ho 協和 Born 1883; native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1920, civil governor of Kiangsi. 1926, chairman, Kiangsi provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1924. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI, Right Rev. Matthew** see Li Yung-chao. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Meng-keng, (李夢庚)** Born 1878; native of Nungan, Kirin. 1912, again 1918, senator. 1922, editor, Shanghai Hsu Pao. 1929-31, adviser, Heilungkiang provincial government. Supervisory member, Control Yuan, since February, 1931. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Ming, (李銘)** Courtesy name: Fu-sun 馥蓀 Born 1886; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Yamaguchi Commercial College. Sometime chairman, board of directors, Bank of China. General manager, Chekiang Industrial Bank, Shanghai, since 1925. Chairman, supervisory committee, Central Bank. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI P'ing-heng, (李平衡)** Prefers to sign himself: Lee Pin-heng. Born 1897; native of Huaining, Anhwei. LL.B. (Paris). Sometime executive member, Kuomintang district headquarters in France. 1932-4, director, labour department, ministry of industries; delegate, international labour conference (Geneva). Chinese member, international labour office governing board (Geneva), since January, 1935. — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI Ping-jui, (李炳瑞)** Prefers English rendering: Edward Bing-shuey Lee. Born 1903; native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. B.A. (McGill). 1931-2, editor, Peiping Leader, until suppressed upon Japanese pressure. Editor, Chinese Republic, weekly, Shanghai, since May, 1932. Author, “Two Years of Japan-China Undeclared War.” — p. 166 ([scan leaf 189](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n189/mode/1up))

**LI P'ing-sien, (李品先)** Courtesy name: He-ling 鶴靈 Born 1893; native of Changwu, Kwangsi. Received his training at Paoting Military Academy. Commander, 3rd division of 8th revolutionary army and played prominent part in northern punitive expedition, 1926; commander, 8th revolutionary army and concurrently garrison commander of Wuhan cities, member of the Hupeh provincial government and member of the military affairs commission, 1927; commander-in-chief, 12th route revolutionary army, 1928; retired owing to father's death, 1929; chief-of-staff of 4th group army, member of the national military affairs commission and commander of 8th army; border defence commissioner of Kwangsi, 1931-5; now chief-of-staff of Kwangsi army. Nanning, Kwangsi. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shao-ch'ang, (李紹昌)** Better known as Shao-chang Lee. Born 1891; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. 1917, B.A. (Yale). 1918, M.A. (Columbia). Professor of Chinese history and literature, University of Hawaii, since 1922. Concurrently head, department of Oriental studies, same university, since 1930. Author, “The Development of Chinese Culture” (1926), and “A Brief Interpretative Outline for the Study of Chinese Culture” (1927) etc. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shih-chia, (李世甲)** Courtesy name: K'ai-t'ao 凱濤 Prefers English rendering: S. K. Lee. Born 1894; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Chefoo Naval School. 1928, captain of training ship Tung-chi. 1929-1933, director of general affairs, ministry of navy. Administrative member, Fukien provincial government, concurrently commander, Mawei Forts, since January, 1934. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shih-tseng** see Li Yu-ying. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shou-houa** see Li Shu-hua. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shu-ch'un, (李樹春)** Courtesy name: Yin-hsien 蔭軒 Native of Ch'ingyuan, Hopei. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1928, commissioner of civil affairs, Honan provincial government. 1929, vice-chief of general staff. Commissioner of civil affairs, Shantung provincial government, since September, 1930. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shu-hua, (李書華)** Courtesy name: Jun-chang 潤章 Prefers French rendering: Li Shou Houa. Born 1889; native of Ch'angli, Hopei. D.Sc. (Paris). 1922-7, professor and dean of physics, Peking National University. 1931, political vice-minister, then acting minister of education. Acting director, Peiping Research Academy, since 1933. Author, “La Permabilité Sélective des Membranes Polarisées” (doctoral thesis). — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Shu-t'ien, (李書田)** Courtesy name: Keng-yien 耕硯 Born 1900; native of Ch'angli, Hopei. 1923, B.S. (Peiyang). 1926, Ph.D. (Cornell). 1927-8, professor of civil engineering, Peiyang University, Tientsin. 1930-2, president, Tangshan Engineering College. President, Peiyang University, since September, 1932. Author, “Engineering Economics of Railroad Operation” (doctoral thesis), and “Irrigation of Korea,” etc. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Ssu-kuang, (李四光)** Courtesy name: Chung-k'uai 仲揆 Prefers English rendering: J. S. Lee. Native of Hupeh. M.Sc., D. Sc. (Birmingham). 1930-3, director, research institute of geology, Academia Sinica. Professor and head, geology department, National Peking University, since September, 1933. 1935, lectured in Great Britain, under auspices of Universities' China Committee in London. Author, “The Earth's Age,” etc. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI, T. T** see Li Ti-chun. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Tchuin** see Li Chun. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Teng-hui, (李登輝)** Prefers English rendering: T. H. Lee. Born 1872; native of Tungan, Fukien. B.A. (Yale). Hon. Litt. D. (St. John's). 1912-3, chief editor, Republican Advocate. President, Fuhtan University, Shanghai, since 1919. Founder (1907), World's Chinese Students' Federation, Shanghai. Appointed member of Legislative Yuan, September, 1936. Author of several textbooks. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Ti-chun, (李迪俊)** Courtesy name: Ti-ching 滌鏡 Prefers English rendering: T. T. Li. Born 1901; native of Huangmei, Hupeh. B.A., Ph.D. (Wisconsin). 1929-31, professor of political science, Central Political Institute, Nanking. Promoted director of publicity, ministry of foreign affairs, since February, 1933. Co-founder and editor, Current Events monthly (in Chinese), since 1929. Author, “The Political and Economic Theories of Dr. Sun Yat-sen” (doctoral thesis). — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI T'ien-lu, (李天祿)** Courtesy name: Fu-t'ien 福田 Born 1884; native of T'aian, Shantung. B.A. (Peking Methodist). M.A., Ph.D. (Vanderbilt). 1927-9, vice-president, then acting president, Cheeloo (Shantung Christian) University, Tsinan. Dean, Nanking Theological Seminary, since 1930. Author, “Congressional Policy on Chinese Immigration” (doctoral thesis). — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI T'ing-an, (李廷安)** Prefers English rendering: Tingan Li. Born 1899; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. M.D. (Peiping Union Medical College). D.Ph. (Harvard). 1931, assistant professor of public health, Peiping Union Medical College. Commissioner of public health, city government of Greater Shanghai, since November, 1932. Author, “School Health Syllabus.” — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Tseh-i, (李擇一)** Native of Fukien. Educated in Japan. Diplomatic adviser, Fukien provincial government, since June, 1934. Assisted in Japanese negotiations for liquidation of Tangku truce agreement — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Tsu-yu, (李祖虞)** Courtesy name: Meng-tsou 夢騶 Prefers own rendering: Lee Tso-yu. Born 1884; native of Wuchin, Kiangsu. Graduate, Waseda University. Sometime judge, supreme court. Commissioner of civil affairs, Fukien provincial government, since April, 1934. Author of two volumes on general principles of obligations in civil law, etc. — p. 167 ([scan leaf 190](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n190/mode/1up))

**LI Tsung-jen, (李宗仁)** Courtesy name: Teh-lin 德鄰 Born 1890; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. 1927, commander, nationalist 3rd route army. 1928, chairman, Wuhan branch, Kuomintang central political council; concurrently commander-in-chief, 4th group army. October, 1928, appointed state councillor of the National Government. March, 1929, deprived of all posts for launching defection of Kwangsi generals. December, 1931, pardoned. April, 1932-July, 1936, director of peace preservation, Nanning (Kwangsi). Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. Transferred member, standing committee, national military council, since July 20, 1936. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Tu, (李杜)** Courtesy name: Chih-ch'u 植初 Born 1880; native of Ihsien, Liaoning. 1929, field commander, Sungari defence force: promoted lieutenant-general. Elected commander-in-chief, northeastern self-defence army, October, 1931. One of most intrepid leaders of the volunteers in Manchuria, until compelled to retreat into Soviet territory by superior Japanese forces. June, 1933, returned to China via Europe. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Wen-fan, (李文範)** Courtesy name: Chün-p'ei 君培 Born 1885; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1929-31, secretary-general, Legislative Yuan. State councillor of the National Government, since December, 1933. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. Concurrently chairman, pensions committee, central Kuomintang headquarters, since January, 1936. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Yen-nien, (李延年)** Courtesy name: Chi-fu 吉甫 Born 1902; native of Kuangjao, Shantung. Graduate, Whampoa Military Academy. 1931-2, deputy commander, 88th division, 5th army. Promoted commander, 9th division, with headquarters at Hsuchow, since June, 1932, in recognition of meritorious defence of Shanghai area. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Yu-ying, (李煜瀛)** Courtesy name: Shih-tseng 石曾 Born 1882; native of Kaoyang, Hopei. One of Kuomintang's “elder statemen.” Studied in France and established first bean-curd factory at Paris. 1928, chairman, Peiping branch, Kuomintang central political council. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1924. Director, Peiping Research Academy, since 1929. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Yung-chao, (李容兆)** Courtesy name: Hsieh-pang 協邦 Better known as the Right Rev. Matthew Li. Born 1877; native of Singwen, Szechwan. 1910-28, ordained as priest: missionary. 1929, appointed prefect apostolic of Yachow (Szechwan). Consecrated at Rome (by Pope Pius XI) titular bishop of Tlos and vicar apostolic of Yachow (Szechwan), since June, 1933. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI Yung-fang, (李榮芳)** Born 1887; native of Lanhsien, Hopei. B.A., M.A. (Peking Methodist). Promoted professor of biblical literature. concurrently head, church administration and worship department, school of religion, Yenching University, since 1929. Author, “Apocrypha and Pseudepigrapha” and “An Introduction to the Literature of the Old Testament.” — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LI, Yuntin Cheng** see Li Cheng. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Ch'ang-shu, (梁長樹)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-ch'u 小初 Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: S. C. Leung. Born 1889; native of Fatshan, Kwangtung. M.A. (Vanderbilt). Connected with Y.M.C.A. work in China for quarter of a century, first as secretary, then as general secretary, Canton Y.M.C.A. 1931-4, acting general secretary, Y.M.C.A. national committee. Promoted general secretary, same committee (in succession to late David Z. T. Yui), since February, 1935. Author, “Emergence of the Labor Problem in China” and “Psychology of Jesus.” — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG, H. C** see below. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Han-ts'ao, (梁寒操)** Courtesy name: Chün-mo 均默 Prefers English rendering: H. C. Liang. Born 1901; native of Kaoyao, Kwangtung. 1929-31, senior secretary, then counsellor, ministry of railways. Legislative member, concurrently secretary-general, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1933. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG, Lone** see Liang Yun-sung. — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Shu-min, (梁漱溟)** Original name: Huan-ting 煥鼎 Born 1894; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. 1919-24, professor of philosophy, Peking National University. 1925, founder, Tsaochow Middle School, Shantung. Director, Tsowping rural training centre, since 1930. Famous Buddhist scholar; author of treatises on Indian philosophy, Oriental and Occidental philosophies, also (in Chinese) “The Chinese People Work Out Their Own Salvation.” Eulogizing his present devotion to the welfare of rural folk, one commentator observes: “As Schweitzer has made Lambarene in tropical Africa famous, so has Liang Shu-min, in a measure, Tsowping in rural Shantung.” — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Yu-kao, (梁宇[illegible])** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: Yew-koh Leong. Born 1888; native of Nanhai, Kwangtung. LL.B., B.Sc. (London); barrister-at-law (Inner Temple). 1925, secretary, Canton Government. Legal adviser, Executive Yuan, concurrently counsellor, ministry of railways, since July, 1932. Senior Chinese commissioner of Sino-British Yunnan-Burma boundary commission, 1936. Co-author, “Village and Town Life in China.” — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Yuen-li, (梁鋆立)** Born 1903; native of Singchang, Chekiang. 1926, LL.B. (Shanghai Comparative Law School). 1930, S.J.D. (Harvard). 1928, judge, Shanghai provisional court. 1929-30, second secretary, legation in Washington, D.C. Senior secretary, ministry of foreign affairs, since October, 1933. Professor of law, Shanghai Comparative Law School. Author, “The Sino-Japanese Dispute: Studies in International Law and International Relations.” — p. 168 ([scan leaf 191](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n191/mode/1up))

**LIANG Yun-sung, (梁雲松)** Courtesy name: Lung 龍 Prefers to sign as: Lone Liang. Born 1894; native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. B.A. (London). LL.M. (Cambridge). 1923-5, presiding judge, Canton supreme court. 1927-8, judge, Shanghai provisional court. 1929-33, counsellor, legation in Berlin. Chargé d'affaires, legation at Prague, since October, 1933. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIAO Shih-ch'eng, (廖世承)** Born 1893; native of Kiating, Kiangsu. Graduate, Tsing Hua College. Ph.D. (Brown). Sometime professor of education, and principal, middle school, Southeastern University. Professor and principal, middle school, Kwang Hwa University, since 1927. Author of books on educational psychology, tests and measurements. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIEU, D. K** see Liu Ta-chun. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIEU, O. S** see Liu Hung-sheng. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Chi-ch'ing, (林濟青)** Prefers English rendering: T. I. Linn. Born 1891; native of Laiyang, Shantung. 1911, B.A. (Peking Methodist). 1917, M.E. (Lehigh). 1918-24, dean, mining engineering department, Shantung Provincial College. 1925-9, dean, Tsingtao University. Dean, colleges of arts and science, Cheeloo (Shantung Christian) University, Tsinan, since 1930. Concurrently acting president (1932-4). — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Ching-yun, (林景潤)** Born 1898: native of Hinghwa, Fukien. B.A. (Fukien Christian). M.A. (Oberlin and Harvard). 1927, chairman, administration commission, Fukien Christian University (Foochow). President, same university, since 1928. President, China Christian Educational Association (1930-4). — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Ch'un-hsien, (林椿賢)** Courtesy name: Tung-hai 東海 Prefers English rendering: Jefferson D. H. Lamb. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. B.A. (Phillips). M.A. (Vanderbilt). Ph.D. (Yale). D.Jur. (Lanier). 1924-32, professor in Nanking, Wuchang and Peiping. Counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs, since 1933. Author, “The Influence of Roman Law on the Development of International Private Law” (doctoral thesis), also “The Labour Movement and Labour Legislation in China,” “The Agrarian Movement and Agrarian Legislation in China,” etc. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN, D. Y** see Lin Tao-yang. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Feng-mien, (林風眠)** Born 1898; native of Meihsien, Kwangtung. Graduate, Ecole des Beaux Arts (Paris). 1926-7, principal, College of Fine Arts, Peiping. Principal, National College of Fine Arts, Hangchow, since March, 1928. Well-known painter. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Hung-hsun, (凌鴻勛)** Courtesy name: Tso-ming 作銘 Prefers English rendering: H. H. Ling. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. Graduate, Nanyang College; practical training in U.S.A. 1923-7, professor, and dean, Communications University, Shanghai. 1931-2, chief engineer, Tungkwan-Sian section, Lung-Hai railway. Engineer-in-chief, Chuchow-Shiuchow section, Canton-Hankow railway, since October, 1932. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN K'ang-hou, (林康[illegible])** Prefers English rendering: K. H. Ling. Born 1881; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Sometime general manager, Sin Wah Banking Corporation, Shanghai. January-February, 1932, administrative vice-minister of finance. General secretary, Shanghai Bankers Association, since 1930. President, associated chambers of commerce. Committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1932. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN P'eng-hsia, Miss, (林鵬俠女士)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-ch'ung 霄冲 Born 1904; native of Hinghwa, Fukien. B. Com. (Columbia). Qualified as aviatrix in London. Of adventurous disposition, has been investigating on own initiative, since the autumn of 1932, the cultural and political conditions in Shensi, Kansu, Ninghsia and Chinghai provinces. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Ping, (凌冰)** Courtesy name: Chi-tung 濟東 Prefers to sign himself: P. Ling. Born 1894; native of Kushih, Honan. B.A. (Stanford). M.A. (Columbia). Ph.D. (Clark). 1922, again 1928, commissioner of education, Kaifeng (Honan). 1930-4, minister to Cuba. Assistant counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs, since March, 1935. Leader of Chinese “goodwill” mission to Siam, May-July, 1936. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Sen, (林森)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'ao 子超 Born 1862; native of Foochow, Fukien. Resided in California. 1921-3, senator. 1924, member, Kuomintang central executive committee. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government; concurrently vice-president, then acting president, Legislative Yuan. President of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Tao-yang, (凌道揚)** Prefers English rendering: D. Y. Lin. Born 1888; native of Paoan, Kwangtung. B.Sc. (Massachussetts). M.F. (Yale). 1923-8, director of agriculture and forestry, Tsingtao. Director, central forestry bureau, Nanking, since May 1930. Concurrently chairman, Ningshu agricultural relief association, Nanking environs, since 1931. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN T'ung-shih, (林桐實)** Courtesy name: Tun-min 敦民 Prefers own rendering: Lynn Dun-min. Born 1882; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Paris University. Sometime chargé d'affaires in Paris, Portugal, Cuba and Brazil; assistant director of political affairs, ministry of foreign affairs; chief secretary, National Labor University. Assistant director of general affairs and chief of protocol section, ministry of foreign affairs, since 1932. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Yi-chung, (林翼中)** Prefers own rendering: Yick Chung Lean. Born 1892; native of Hop'u, Kwangtung. Graduate, Canton Teachers' College. 1927, senior political instructor, Whampoa Military Academy. August, 1929-July, 1936, commissioner of civil affairs, Kwangtung provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Yu-t'ang, (林語堂)** Born 1895; native of Changchow, Fukien. B.A. (St. John's). M.A. (Harvard). Ph.D. (Leipzig). 1923-6, professor of English philology, Peking National University; dean of arts, Amoy University. Research fellow in philology and English editor, Academia Sinica, since May, 1930. Founder and editor, Lun Yu (Analects fortnightly), since September, 1932, and Jen Chien Shih fortnightly, Shanghai, since March, 1934. Editor, “Little Critic” section, The China Critic (Shanghai), since 1930. Author of “My Country and My People,” New York (a good seller), and several volumes of Kaiming English readers and essays. — p. 169 ([scan leaf 192](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n192/mode/1up))

**LIN Yun-k'ai, (林雲陔)** Born 1884; native of Singi, Kwangtung. M.A. (Syracuse). 1929-31, administrative member, Kwangtung provincial government. May, 1931-July, 1936, chairman, same provincial government. Transferred chairman, commission on Mongolian and Tibetan affairs, since July 20, 1936. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LING, H. H** see Lin Hung-hsun. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LING, K. H** see Lin K'ang-hou. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LING, P** see Lin Ping. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LINN, T. I** see Lin Chi-ch'ing. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Chan-en, (劉湛恩)** Prefers English rendering: Herman Chanen Liu. Born 1896; native of Hanyang, Hupeh. B.Sc. (Soochow). M.A. (Chicago). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1924-7, secretary, educational department, Y.M.C.A. national committee, Shanghai. President, Shanghai University (formerly Baptist College), since July, 1928. Author, “Non-Verbal Tests for Use in China” (doctoral thesis) and “Co-Education.” — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Cheh, (劉哲)** Courtesy name: Ching-yü 敬輿 Born 1888; native of Kirin. Graduate, Peking National University. Sometime civil governor of Kirin; senator. 1927-8, minister of education. 1930-1, president, Harbin Industrial College. Member, Peiping political council, and then (its successor) Hopei-Chahar political council, since July, 1933. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Chen-hua, (劉鎮華)** Courtesy name: Hsueh-va 雪亞 Born 1883; native of Kunghsien, Honan. Graduate, Peking Law College. Sometime civil and military governor of Shensi. 1928-9, commander-in-chief, 8th route army. Commander, 64th division, since 1930. Chairman, Anhwei provincial government, since September, 1933. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Chi-wen, (劉紀文)** Born 1890; native of Tungkuan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Tokyo Law College. April, 1928-March, 1930, mayor of Nanking. April, 1930-March, 1931, customs superintendent, Shanghai. March, 1932-July, 1936, mayor of Canton. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Transferred administrative member, Kwangtung provincial government, since July 20, 1936. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Chin-wen, (劉錦文)** Better known as the Right Rev. Francis Liu. Born 1872; native of Lucheng, Shansi. Educated at Taiyuanfu and Luan. 1903-29, ordained as priest; rector, St. Anthony's College, and professor of Chinese literature, Luan Seminary. Consecrated titular bishop of Lampsacus and promoted vicar apostolic of Fenyang (Shansi), since October, 1930. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Ch'ung-chieh, (劉崇傑)** Courtesy name: Tzu-k'ai 子楷 Born 1880; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Waseda University. 1917-8, counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs. 1920-8, minister to Spain and Portugal. 1932-3, administrative vice-minister for foreign affairs. 1933-5, minister to Germany. Transferred minister to Austria, since October, 1935. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Hai-sou** see below. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Hai-su, (劉海粟)** Prefers to sign himself: Liu Hai-sou. Born 1895; native of Wuchin, Kiangsu. 1929, commissioned by ministry of education to study art in Europe. 1932, returned to China. Called the “great master of the renaissance of Chinese art,” by M. Louis Laley, eminent French literary critic. One of his paintings, “Snow of Luxembourg,” was purchased by the French Government and displayed at the Musée du Jeu de Paume. 200 of his studies are collected in book form. Principal, National College of Fine Arts, Shanghai, since 1929. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU, H. C** see Liu Hsing-chi. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU, Herman Chanen** see Liu Chan-en. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU, Right Rev. Francis** see Liu Chin-wen. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Hsiang, (劉湘)** Courtesy name: Fu-ch'en 甫臣 Born 1890; native of Szechwan; uncle of Liu Wen-hui. Commander, 21st army, since 1929. Chairman, Szechwan provincial government, since December, 1934. Concurrently rehabilitation commissioner for same province. Commander, Szechwan provincial peace preservation units, since February, 1935. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Hsing-chi, (劉行驥)** Prefers English rendering: H. C. Liu. Born 1902; native of Huangp'i, Hupeh. B.S.A. (Wisconsin). M.S. (Cornell). 1928-9, director, Hupeh and Anhwei agricultural and forestry experimental stations. Senior technical ex pert, ministry of industries, since 1930. Concurrently director of fishery and animal husbandry, same ministry (1931-3). — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Hung-sheng, (劉鴻生)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: O. S. Lieu. Born 1892; native of Shanghai. Hon. D.Litt. (St. John's). One of Shanghai's rising business men. 1930-3, councillor, Shanghai municipal council. Shanghai agent, Kailan Mining Administration, since 1920. November, 1932-March, 1936, general manager, China Merchants Steam Navigation Company. Director of many concerns; philanthropist. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU, J. Heng** see Liu Jui-heng. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Ju-ming, (劉汝明)** Courtesy name: Tzu-liang 子[illegible] Born 1898; native of Hsien-hsien, Hopei. Started as soldier under Gen. Feng Yu-hsiang. 1926, commander, Kuominchun 10th division. 1930, commander, Honan-Shensi bandit suppression forces. 1933, distinguished himself in defence of Great Wall as commander, 2nd division. Commander, reorganized 143rd division, since March, 1936. One of Gen. Sung Cheh-yuan's trusted aides. Chairman, Chahar provincial government, in succession to Gen. Chang Tzu-chung, since June, 1936. — p. 170 ([scan leaf 193](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n193/mode/1up))

**LIU Jui-heng, (劉瑞恆)** Courtesy name: Yueh-ju 月如 Prefers English rendering: J. Heng Liu. Born 1890; native of Tientsin, Hopei. B.S., M.D. (Harvard). 1923-9, rose to medical superintendent, then director, Peiping Union Medical College. 1928-30, vice-minister, then minister of health. Director, national health administration, since December, 1930. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Lu-yin, (劉蘆隱)** Born 1895; native of Yungfeng, Kiangsi. B.Sc. (California). 1928-31, director, compilation bureau, Legislative Yuan: concurrently director of publicity, central Kuomintang headquarters. January, 1932, appointed vice-president, Examination Yuan, but declined. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Reappointed director of publicity, central Kuomintang headquarters, since January, 1936. Arrested in Shanghai, February 24, 1937, and sent to Hupeh for trial in connection with assassination of Yang Yung-t'ai, late chairman of Hupeh provincial government. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Shang-ch'ing, (劉尙清)** Courtesy name: Hai-ch'uan 海泉 Born 1880; native of Haich'eng, Liaoning. Graduate, Mukden Law College. 1919, commissioner of finance, Heilungkiang. 1927, minister of agriculture and commerce; civil governor of Mukden. 1931, minister of interior. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Appointed chairman of Anhwei provincial government, April, 1937. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Shih, (劉峙)** Courtesy name: Ching-fu 經扶 Born 1893; native of Chian, Kiangsi. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1927-30, commander, Nationalist 1st division. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Chairman, Honan provincial government, since November, 1930. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Shih-shun, (劉師舜)** Courtesy name: Ch'in-wu 琴五 Born 1900; native of Ifeng, Kiangsi. A.B. (Johns Hopkins). A.M. (Harvard). Ph.D. (Columbia). 1925-7, professor of international relations, Tsing Hua University. 1930, counsellor, ministry of interior. 1931, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Director, European and American affairs department, ministry of foreign affairs, since February, 1932. Author, “Extraterritoriality: Its Rise and Decline” (doctoral thesis). — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Shou-chung, (劉守中)** Courtesy name: Yun-ch'en 允丞 Born 1881; native of Shensi. March, 1929, elected reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Shun-i, Miss, (劉純一女士)** Native of Shantung; late husband, P'eng Shih-ming, being revolutionary leader in Shensi. Studied in Japan. 1916-21, principal, Women's Normal School, Sian. Founder, Women's Star Weekly (Tientsin); publisher and editor, Chinese Women's Daily News (Shanghai). 1927, director, women's division, Shanghai Kuomintang headquarters. May, 1931, delegate to national people's convention, Nanking; elected member of its presidium, in recognition of her services for women's emancipation. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Ta-chun, (劉大鈞)** Courtesy name: Chi-t'ao 季陶 Prefers to sign himself: D. K. Lieu. Born 1891; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. B.A. (Michigan). F.R.A.S. 1920-8, senior investigator, bureau of economic information. 1928-31, director, bureau of statistics, Legislative Yuan and comptroller-general's office. Chartered accountant in Shanghai,since July, 1932. Advisory editor, Social Abstracts (New York) and The China Critic (Shanghai). Author, “China's Industries and Finance” and “Foreign Investments in China.” — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU T'ing-fang, (劉廷芳)** Prefers English rendering: Timothy Tingfang Lew. Born 1891; native of Wenchow, Chekiang. B.D. (Yale). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). Professor of theology and psychology, Yenching University, since 1926. Concurrently vice-president, same institution, since October, 1934. Author, “Psychology of Learning Chinese” (doctoral thesis) and “China in American School Textbooks .” Joint-author, “China To-Day through Chinese Eyes” and “Education in China.” Editor, The Amethyst (Peiping). — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Wei-chih, (劉維熾)** Born 1892; native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime commissioner of finance, Canton city government. 1929-31, managing director, Peiping-Hankow, Nanking-Shanghai and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo railways. 1932, administrative vice-minister of railways. July, 1933-July, 1936, administrative, then political vice-minister of industries. Transferred commissioner of reconstruction, Kwangtung provincial government, since July 20, 1936. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Wen-hui, (劉文輝)** Courtesy name: Tzu-ch'ien 自乾 Born 1894; native of Tayi, Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Sometime commander-in-chief for defence of Western Szechwan. 1928-34, chairman, Szechwan provincial government. Commander, 24th army, since 1927. Chairman, Hsikang provincial government organization commission, since January, 1935. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Wen-tao, (劉文島)** Courtesy name: Ch'en-su 塵蘇 Born 1896; native of Kuangchi, Hupeh. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy, and Paris Law College. 1929-31, mayor of Hankow. August, 1931-October, 1933, minister to Germany and Austria. November, 1933-October, 1934, minister to Italy. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Promoted ambassador to Italy, since November, 1934. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Ya-tzu, (柳亞子)** Born 1887; native of Wuchiang, Kiangsu. Noted poet, having produced over five thousand poems. 1926, and since December, 1931, member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee. Director, history compilation bureau, city government of Greater Shanghai, since July, 1932. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**LIU Yi-yen, (劉貽燕)** Born 1887; native of Huaining, Anhwei. B.Sc. (Glasgow). Sometime principal, Anhwei Engineering College; associate director, Hangchow-Kiangshan Railway; director of planning, central machine works, ministry of industries. Commissioner of reconstruction, Anhwei provincial government, since 1934. — p. 171 ([scan leaf 194](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n194/mode/1up))

**“LIVING BUDDHA” OF INNER TIBET** see Lo-pu-ts'ang-t'u-pa-tan, etc., etc. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**“LIVING BUDDHA” OF MONGOLIA** see Chang-chia hu-t'u-k'eh-t'u. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**“LIVING BUDDHA” OF OUTER TIBET** see Chi-tseh-je-chen-hu-t'u-k'eh-tu. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Chia-lun, (羅家倫)** Born 1896; native of Chekiang. Graduate, Peking Government University. 1928, president, Tsing Hua University. 1930-3, dean of studies, Central Political Institute, Nanking. Chancellor, Central University, since August, 1932. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Concurrently vice-chairman, publicity committee, central Kuomintang headquarters, since 1933. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Ch'uan-hua, (駱傳華)** Prefers own rendering: Lowe Chuan-hua. Born 1902; native of Nanchang, Kiangsu. 1923, Ph.B. (Chicago). 1927-8, professor of economics, Chih Tze College, Shanghai. 1928-34, industrial secretary, Chinese Y.M.C.A. national committee. Publicity director, China international famine relief commission, since June, 1934. Author, “China's Labour Movement,” “Facing Labour Issues in China” and “Symposium on Japan's Undeclared War in Shanghai”; also “Rural Reconstruction in China” (in preparation). — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Chung-yi, (羅忠詒)** Courtesy name: Yi-yuan 儀元 Prefers to sign himself: T. Y. Lo. Born 1886; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.A. (Cantab.). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1914-23, first secretary, legation in London; chargé d'affaires, Lima. Minister to Denmark, since 1926. Concurrently delegate, League of Nations assembly (1934-5). — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO-pu-ts'ang-t'u-pa-tan-ch'u-chieh-i-ma-keh-leh-k'eh-la-mu-chieh, (羅布藏吐巴丹曲結宜瑪格勒克拉木結)** Better known as Panshen Lama, or Tashi Lama, or “Living Buddha” (15th holder) of Inner Tibet 班禪額爾德尼 Born 1883; native of Cha-shih-lun-pu, Inner Tibet. 1923, fled to China upon disagreement with late Dalai Lama, the temporal head of Tibet. Administrative member, Chinghai provincial government, since January, 1929. State councillor of National Government, since February, 1934. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO, R. Y** see Lo Yun-yen. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO, T. Y** see Lo Chung-yi. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Wen-kan, (羅文幹)** Courtesy name: Chün-jen 鈞任 Born 1888; native of P'anyu, Kwangtung. M.A. (Oxon.); barrister-at-law (Inner Temple). 1921, again 1926, minister of justice. 1922, chief justice, supreme court; minister of finance. 1928, minister for foreign affairs. 1932-4, minister of justice; concurrently minister for foreign affairs. Industrialist in Kwangtung. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Yu-chieh, Miss, (羅有節女士)** Prefers Cantonese rendering: Yau-tsit Law. Born 1888; native of K'aip'ing, Kwangtung. 1906, one of first four women admitted into Canton Christian College (now Lingnan University) as an experiment in co-education. 1912, one of six founders of Canton Y.W.C.A. 1916-7, B.A. (Mount Holyoke), M.A. (Columbia). 1926-8, dean of women, Lingnan University. Principal, True Light Elementary School, since 1928. General secretary, Canton Y.W.C.A. (1923-35). Delegate to first biennial conference, Institute of Pacific Relations (1925). — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LO Yun-yen, (羅運炎)** Courtesy name: Yao-tung 耀東 Prefers English rendering: R. Y. Lo. Born 1890; native of Kiukiang, Kiangsi. B.A. (Baldwin). M.A., Ph.D. (Syracuse). Editor-in-chief, Chinese Christian Advocate, since 1921. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1933. Author, “The Social Teachings of Confucius” (doctoral thesis), “The Chinese Revolution from the Inside” and “The Opium Problem in the Far East,” etc. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LOH Pak-oong** see Lu Po-hung. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LOU T'ung-sun, (樓桐蓀)** Courtesy name: P'ei-lan 佩蘭 Prefers own rendering: Lau Tong Sun. Born 1896; native of Yungk'ang, Chekiang. M.A. (Paris). 1925-6, dean, Shanghai Law College. 1927, president, Chekiang Law College. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1928. Author of two volumes on England's modern political history, and principles of the civil code, etc. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LOWE Chuan-hua** see Lo Ch'uan-hua. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LOUIS, Binnan P** see Lei Pei-hung. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LÜ Ch'ao, (呂超)** Courtesy name: Han-ch'ün 漢羣 Born 1891; native of Iping, Szechwan. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1927, pacification commissioner, Szechwan province. Director, military affairs department, National Government headquarters, since March, 1931. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LU Chih-wei, (陸志韋)** Prefers English rendering: C. W. Luh. Born 1894; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Ph.D. (Chicago). Sometime professor of educational psychology, Southeastern University. Professor of psychology, Yenching University, since 1927. Concurrently president, same institution, since September, 1934. Author and translator of books on psychology. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LU Ch'ing, (盧靖)** Courtesy name: Mu-chai 木齋 Born 1855; native of Hupeh. Mathematics instructor in Peiyang Military Academy under Li Hung-chang. 1911, retired. March, 1927, donated $100,000 to Nankai University for construction of new library; also his own collection of 200,000 volumes. Regarded as best scholar in Tientsin. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LÜ Hsien, (咸呂)** Courtesy name: Chu-ch'ing 箸青 Born 1888; native of Chochow, Hopei. Graduate, Peiyang University. Sometime counsellor, ministry of agriculture and commerce; finance commissioner, Hopei provincial government; civil commissioner, Kiangsi provincial government. Appointed successor to late T. D. Woo as manager, national treasury department, Central Bank of China, since July, 1936. — p. 172 ([scan leaf 195](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n195/mode/1up))

**LU Po-hung, (陸伯鴻)** Better known by Shanghai rendering: Loh Pak-oong. Philanthropist and Catholic leader. Born 1872; native of Shanghai. Managing director, Shanghai Tramways Company, since 1926. Director of several enterprises. Founder (1935) of new “model lunatic asylum” in Shanghai, constructed at cost of $500,000. Councillor, French municipal council, Shanghai, since 1934. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**LU Tso-fu, (盧作孚)** Native of Szechwan. Founder and general manager, Min Sheng Industrial Company, Chungking, since 1926. Starting with capital of eight thousand dollars, his concern is now capitalized at over $2,000,000, including reserves accumulated through yearly profits. It maintains 28 river steamers totalling 7,000 tons, plying between Shanghai and Chungking, a machinery plant in Chungking, an electric plant at Ho-ch'uan, several coal mines, 16 to 50 kilometres of light railway for coal transportation, the Western China Science Research Institute devoted to researches in biology, physics, chemistry, geology, agriculture and forestry, and maintains an armed militia of five hundred men to protect its mines, railroad and other properties. Commissioner of reconstruction, Szechwan provincial government, since December, 1935. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**LUH, C. W** see Lu Chih-wei. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**LUNG Yun, (龍雲)** Courtesy name: Chih-chou 志舟 Born 1888; native of Ch'aotung, Yunnan. Graduate, Yunnan Military Institute. 1926, defence commissioner, Kwenming. Chairman, Yunnan provincial government, since August, 1928. Concurrently commander, 38th army, since March, 1927. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**LY, J. Usang** see Li Chao-huan. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Chan-shan, (馬占山)** Courtesy name: Hsiu-feng 秀峯 Born 1887; native of Huaiteh, Liaoning. Acting, then substantive chairman, Heilungkiang provincial government, since November, 1931. Has become national hero in present Manchurian crisis for epic defence of Tsitsihar, and then rallying the loyalists for unremitting resistance against the Japanese two months after feigning fealty to “Manchoukuo” Government. December, 1932, retreated into Soviet territory in face of superior Japanese armament. June, 1933, returned to China, via Europe. Member, national military council, since 1934. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Ch'ao-chun, (馬超俊)** Courtesy name: Hsin-ch'iao 星樵 Born 1886; native of Taishan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Kyoto School of Aviation. 1929-33, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931. State councillor of the National Government, since November, 1934. Mayor of Nanking, since April, 1935. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Ho, (馬和)** Courtesy name: Chün-wu 君武 Born 1887; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. Graduate, Kyoto Imperial University. Doktor ingenieur (Berlin). 1913, again 1915, senator. 1921-2, civil governor of Kwangsi. 1925-6, minister of justice, Canton Government. Chancellor, Kwangsi National University, since 1927. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Hsiang-po** see Ma Liang. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Hsin-yi, Miss, (馬心儀女士)** Prefers English rendering; Roberta M. Ma. Born 1902; native of Tsinan, Shantung. 1926, B.A.; 1927, M.A.; 1930, Ph.D. (Texas). 1924-6, assistant in the department of botany, University of Texas. 1930-3, le turer in botany, Yenching University; research fellow, department of bacteriology, Peiping Union Medical College. Professor of botany, Kwangsi University, Wuchow, since September, 1933. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Hung-k'uei, (馬鴻逵)** Courtesy name: Shao-yün 少雲 Born 1892; native of Kansu. Graduate, Kansu Military Academy. 1915, bandit-suppression commissioner, Kansu-Shensi-Mongolia borders. 1929, commander, reorganized 64th division. Commander, reorganized 7th division, since 1930. Concurrently chairman, Ninghsia provincial government, since 1931. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Liang, (馬良)** Courtesy name: Hsiang-po 相伯 Born 1839; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. Probably the oldest living official of late Manchu dynasty. Appointed state councillor of National Government, January, 1937. Despite advanced age, is in sound health and active in public service and authorship. Founder of Aurora and Fuhtan Universities (Shanghai). Well-known Chinese and Latin scholar. Best of his works is “History of Ling Hsin” (History of Spiritualism). — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Lin, (馬麟)** Courtesy name: Hsün-ch'en 勳臣 Born 1880; native of Linhsia, Kansu. 1929, commissioner of reconstruction, Ching-hai (Kokonor) provincial government. 1930, bandit - suppression commissioner, Kansu province. Chairman, Chinghai provincial government, since January, 1933. Concurrently commissioner of civil affairs (1933-4). — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA, Roberta M** see Ma Hsin-yi. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MA Yin-ch'u, (馬寅初)** Born 1884; native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. B.A. (Yale). M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). Hon. LL.D. (Soochow). 1920-7, departmental chief, Bank of China, Peiping head office. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since October, 1928. Noted economist. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MAO I-sheng, (茅以昇)** Courtesy name: T'ang-ch'en 唐臣 Prefers English rendering: T. E. Mao. Born 1896; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. M.C.E. (Cornell). D. Eng. (Carnegie In. Tech.). 1924-6, president, Tangshan Engineering College. 1928-32, president, Peiyang University. Director, hydraulic engineering division, national economic council, since January, 1934. Author, “Secondary Stresses in Bridge Trusses” (doctoral thesis). — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MAO, T. E** see above. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MAO Tsu-Ch'uan, (茅祖權)** Courtesy name: Yung-hsün 詠薰 Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Sometime commissioner of civil affairs, Kiangsu provincial government. President, administrative court, since November, 1933. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MAO-t'un** see Shen Yien-ping. — p. 173 ([scan leaf 196](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n196/mode/1up))

**MEI Ching-chou, (梅景周)** Prefers Cantonese anglicized rendering: K. C. Mui. Born 1895 native of Taishan, Kwangtung. M. A. (Chicago). 1929-30, member, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs. 1931-2, vice-consul in Havana. Consul, then promoted consul-general, in Honolulu, since, January, 1933. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEI Ju-ao, (梅汝敖)** Born 1904; native of Nanchang, Kiangsi. 1924, graduate, Tsing Hua University. 1926, B.Sc. (Stanford). 1928, doctor of jurisprudence (Chicago). Sometime professor of political science in Nankai (Tientsin) and Wuhan (Wuchang) universities. 1933-4, counsellor, ministry of interior. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since January, 1935. Author of “Modern Jurisprudence” and “Outlines of Legal Philosophy.” — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEI Lan-fang, (梅蘭芳)** Born 1893; native of Kiangsu. China's most noted female impersonator. Hon. D.Litt. (California). Aged 19, won 27,000 votes in popularity contest as “King of Actors.” Acts in over 400 plays, one-third being own productions. 1930, visited U.S.A. for six months; as royally received as previously in Tokyo and Hongkong. Painter and philanthropist. 1935, visited Soviet Russia, as guest of Soviet theatrical interests, and other parts of Europe. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEI, Y. C** see below. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEI Yi-ch'i, (梅貽琦)** Prefers English rendering: Y. C. Mei. Born 1889; native of Tientsin, Hopei. B.A., M.A. C.E. (Cornell). 1915-29, professor of physics, Tsing Hua University. 1929-31, director, Chinese educational mission in U.S.A. President, Tsing Hua University, since November, 1931. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEI Yi-lin, (梅貽琳)** Born 1893; younger brother of Y. C. Mei. 1921, B.Sc. (Chicago). 1925, M.D. (Rush Med. Coll.). 1927, D.Ph. (Johns Hopkins). 1929-31, technical expert, ministry of health; commissioner of public health, Nanking city government. Director, army medical administration, ministry of military affairs, then army medical service, national military council, since 1933. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MEN Chih-chung, (門致中)** Born 1888; native of Kirin. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Formerly one of Feng Yü-hsiang's Kuominchun commanders. 1928-30, chairman, Ninghsia provincial government. Chairman, reconstruction committee, Hopei-Chahar political council, since January, 1936. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MENG, H. C** see below. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MENG Hsien-ch'eng, (孟憲承)** Prefers English rendering: H. C. Meng. Born 1894; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. B.A. (St. John's). 1923-6, dean, Chinese department, St. John's University. 1927-9, dean of education,Central University. 1930-3, director, Hangchow Rural Research Institute.
