Professor of education, Central Political Institute, and lecturer on education, Central University, Nanking, since September, 1933. Author (in Chinese), “Principles of Education,” “History of Education,” “Adult Education,” etc. Translator, “Pragmatism,” “Modern Educational Theories,” “Foundations of Method,” etc. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MIAO P'ei-ch'eng, (苗培成)** Courtesy name: Kao-pao 告寶 Born 1895; native of Chingcheng, Shansi. Graduate, Peking National University. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Supervisory commissioner for Control Yuan in Anhwei and Kiangsi provinces, since March, 1935. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MOH, H. Y** see Mu Hsiang-yueh. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MU Hsiang-yueh, (穆湘玥)** Courtesy name: Ou-ch'u 藕初 Prefers English rendering: H. Y. Moh. Born 1877; native of Shanghai. B.S. (Wisconsin). M.S. (Illinois). 1920-6, president, Chinese cotton goods exchange; chairman, general chamber of commerce. October, 1928-June, 1931, administrative vice-minister of industries. Managing director, Teh Dah Cotton Spinning Co. (Shanghai), since 1920. Member, rural rehabilitation commission and cotton industry regulating commission, since 1933. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**MUI, K. C** see Mei Ching-chou. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NA Yien-t'u, (那彦圖)** Courtesy name: Chü-fu 鉅甫 Prince of 1st order and jassak of Khalka Mongols. 1903, grand chamberlain. 1922, pacification commissioner of Mongolia. High adviser to Executive Yuan, since January, 1934. Member, Mongolian local autonomy political council, since April, 1934. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NEW, W. L** see Niu Hui-lin. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NIEH, C. C** see below.. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NIEH Ch'i-chieh, (聶其杰)** Courtesy name: Yün-t'ai 雲臺 Prefers English rendering: C. C. Nieh. Born 1880; native of Changsha (Hunan). 1918, organizer and president, Chinese Cotton Mill Owners' Association. General manager, Great China Cotton Mill (Shanghai), since 1919. Co-founder, National Association for Vocational Education. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NIU Hui-lin, (牛惠霖)** Prefers English rendering: W. L. New. Born 1887; native of Shanghai. M.A., Ch.B. (Cantab.). M.R.C.S. and L.R.C.P. (London). 1917-27, medical superintendent, Chinese Red Cross General Hospital: associate professor of operative surgery, St. John's University Medical School. Private practice in Shanghai, since 1920. Past president, National Medical Association. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NIU Yung-chien, (鈕永建)** Courtesy name: T'i-sheng 惕生 Born 1870; native of Shanghai. 1923, civil governor of Kiangsu. 1928-32, chairman, Kiangsu provincial government; acting minister of interior; chairman, board of personnel, Examination Yuan; vice-chairman, national opium suppression commission; legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Vice-president, Examination Yuan, since July, 1932. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**NY Tsi ze** see Yen Chi-tz'u. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**OUYANG Chü, (歐陽駒)** Also known as Auyang Kui. Native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Chief-of-staff, 1st route army of northern punitive expedition; vice-commander, 1st independent division (commander: Wu Te-chen); commander, 17th division, 6th army; commander of Bocca Tigris forts, Canton, 1924; member, national military advisory council. Now secretary-general, Kwangtung provincial government. — p. 174 ([scan leaf 197](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n197/mode/1up))

**PAI Ch'ung-hsi, (白崇禧)** Courtesy name: Chien-sheng 健生 Born 1893; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Credited as the real brain of the Kwangsi generals. 1927, defence commissioner, Shanghai and Woosung. 1928, second in command, 4th group army. March, 1929, dismissed from all posts for abetting Li Tsung-jen's defection. March, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, pardoned. April, 1932-July, 1936, vice-director of peace preservation, Nanning (Kwangsi). Transferred chairman of Chekiang provincial government, since July 20, 1936. Reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. Appointed member of military council, September, 1936. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PAI P'eng-fei, (白鵬飛)** Courtesy name: Ching-t'ien 經天 Prefers own rendering: Peh Pong-fee. Born 1889; native of Kweilin, Kwangsi. 1919, LL.M. (Tokyo Imperial). Sometime dean and acting president, Kwangsi University, Kweilin. Dean, college of law, Peking National University, since 1931. Author of treatises on administrative law, comparative labour laws, etc. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PAI Yun-t'i, (白雲梯)** Courtesy name: Chü-ch'uan 巨川 Native of Kalasin, Inner Mongolia. 1918, member, Canton extraordinary parliament. November, 1930, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member (first elected, 1924), Kuomintang central executive committee. Member, Mongolian local autonomy political council, since April, 1934. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PAN, Francis K** see below. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'AN Kuang-chun, (潘光迥)** Prefers English rendering: Francis K. Pan. Born 1904; native of Paoshan, Kiangsu. 1926, B.A. (Dartmouth). 1927, M.C.S.: 1929, D.C.S. (New York). Sometime research fellow, Institute of International Finance, New York. Assistant manager, Commercial Press, Shanghai, since 1930. Chief secretary, ministry of railways, since December, 1935. Author, “International Financial Intervention” (doctoral thesis). — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'AN Kung-chan, (潘公展)** Prefers to sign himself: Y. Y. Phen. Born 1895; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's University. 1919, leader, “student movement.” 1920, editor, Shanghai Tribune. 1924, editor, Shun Pao (Shanghai). 1929-31, chief secretary, then commissioner of social affairs, city government of Greater Shanghai. Commissioner of education, same city government, since July, 1932. Translator, “New Village Administration.” — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'AN Kung-pi, (潘公弼)** Born 1895; native of Chiating, Kiangsu. Graduate, Nanyang College. Studied in Tokyo Commercial College. 1918, Japan correspondent, Shun Pao. 1919, editor, Ching Pao (Peiping). 1920, rejoined Shih Shih Hsin Pao (China Times), Shanghai. Editor-in-chief, same journal, since 1928. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'AN Shu-lun, (潘序倫)** Born 1895; native of Ishing, Kiangsu. Graduate, National Naval Officers' College, Nanking. Joined St. John's University; graduated, 1921; attended Harvard Graduate School of Business Administration, specializing in accounting and received M. A. degree, 1923; Ph.D. (Columbia), 1924; professor and then dean, Shanghai College of Commerce, 1924; later, dean, Government Chinan University, Chenju, Shanghai. Now chartered accountant practising in Shanghai, also serving as accounting adviser to many government institutions. Author, “Trade of U.S. with China”; “Bookkeeping and Accounting”; “Corporate Finance” (in English); “Advanced Bookkeeping” and “Chinese Corporate Accounting” (in Chinese). — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PEH Pong-fee** see Pai P'eng-fei. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PEI, Tsuyee** see below. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PEI Tsu-yi, (貝祖詒)** Courtesy name: Sung-sun 淞孫 Prefers English rendering: Tsuyee Pei. Born 1893; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Connected with Bank of China, since 1914. Shanghai manager, same institution, since 1927. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, 1932-4. Director, Central Bank, since March, 1935. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PEI, W. C** see P'ei Wen-chung. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'EI Wen-chung, (裴文中)** Prefers English rendering: W. C. Pei. Born 1898; native of Hopei. 1927, graduate, Peking National University. Geologist and palæontologist, Cenozoic laboratory,Geological Survey of China, since 1930. Is celebrated as discoverer (1931) of the “Peking Man” in the vicinity of Peiping, “the most impressive discovery in the whole history of human palæontology.” 1933, discoverer of palæolithic implements in Choukoutien, Hopei. Author, “On the Mammalian Remains from Locality 5 of Choukoutien,” etc. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**P'ENG Hsueh-p'ei, (彭學沛)** Courtesy name: Hao-hsü 浩徐 Born 1897; native of Anfu, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo and Brussels Universities. 1927, editor, Central Daily News, Nanking. 1932, political vice-minister of interior. 1932-5, director, political affairs department, Executive Yuan. Administrative vice-minister of communications, since January, 1936. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PHEN, Y. Y** see P'an Kung-chan. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PIEN Shou-ch'ing, (邊守靖)** Courtesy name: Chi-ch'ing 潔清 Born 1884; native of Chinghai, Hopei. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1912, speaker, Chihli provincial assembly. Director-general, Ling Ching Mining Corporation, and proprietor, Ho Pei Jih Pao, Tientsin, since 1916. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PIEN Shou-sun, (卞壽孫)** Courtesy name: Pai-mei 白眉 Prefers English rendering: Z. S. Bien. Born 1883; native of Icheng, Kiangsu. Ph.B. (Brown). 1914, chief auditor, Bank of China. 1922-4, Peiping manager, same bank. Tientsin manager, same institution, since 1925. Councillor, British municipal council, Tientsin, since April, 1935. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PING Chih, (秉志)** Courtesy name: Nung-shan 農山 Born 1889; native of Kaifeng, Honan. B.S.A., Ph.D. (Cornell). 1921-7, professor of zoology, Southeastern University. Director, biological laboratory, Science Society of China, since 1922. Director, Fan Memorial Institute of Biology, since 1928. Author, “The Fossil Insects of China” and “The Cerebrum of Felis Tigris,” etc. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PING-HSIN** see Hsiêh Wan-ying. — p. 175 ([scan leaf 198](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n198/mode/1up))

**PO Wen-wei, (柏文蔚)** Courtesy name: Lieh-wu 烈武 Born 1875; native of Fengyang, Anhwei. 1912, military governor of native province. November, 1929, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**P'U Yi, (溥儀)** Manchu ex-Emperor Hsuan Tung. Better known as Henry Pu Yi. Born 1906; nephew of Emperor Kuang Hsu. November, 1908, succeeded to the throne. February, 1912, abdicated. July, 1917, restored to the throne for a fortnight by Chang Hsun. 1924-31, resided in Japanese Concession, Tientsin. November, 1931, left Tientsin for Manchuria. March, 1932, assumed office as Regent of “Manchoukuo Government” under Japanese protection. March, 1934, enthroned as “Emperor Kang-tê of Manchoutikuo.” — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**QUO Tai-chi** see Kuo T'ai-ch'i. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**QUO Tai-tsing** see Kuo T'ai-chen. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SAH Fu-chun, (薩福均)** Courtesy name: Shao-ming 少銘 Prefers English rendering: F. K. Sah. Born 1885; son of Admiral Sah Chen-ping, C.E. (Purdue). 1923-9, chief engineer, Kiaochow-Tsinan railway. Director, engieeering department, ministry of railways, since 1929. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SAH, F. K** see above. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SEN, K. T** see Sun Kuei-ting. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHA-K'EH-TU-ERH-CHA-PU, (沙克都爾札布)** Courtesy name: Chan-k'uei 占魁 President of Ikh Chao League, Inner Mongolia. Administrative member, Suiyuan provincial government, since 1932. Second vice-chairman, Mongolian local autonomy political council, since March, 1934. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHANG Chen, (商震)** Courtesy name: Ch'i-yü 啓輿 Born 1884; native of Paoting, Hopei. Graduate, Peiyang Military College. 1925, tutung of Suiyuan. 1929, chairman, Hopei provincial government. 1931, chairman, Shansi provincial government. Commander, 32nd army, since 1931. 1935, chairman,Hopei provincial government. Transferred chairman, Honan provincial government, sinceDecember, 1935. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHAO Li-tzu, (邵力子)** Courtesy name: Chung-hui 仲輝 Native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime editor-in-chief, Min Kuo Jih Pao (Shanghai). 1927-31, chief secretary, generalissimo's headquarters, Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1926. 1932, chairman, Kansu provincial government. Chairman, Shensi provincial government, since July, 1933. Appointed director of central publicity committee, central Kuomintang headquarters, February, 1937. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHAW, Kinn-wei** see Shou Ching-wei. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN, Bazin D. Z** see Shen Pai-hsien. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Ch'ang, (沈昌)** Born 1905; native of T'unghsiang, Chekiang. M.S. (Mass. In. Tech.). Sometime chairman, purchasing commission, ministry of railways. Managing director, Peiping-Suiyuan railway, since 1933. Investigating railway matters in Europe and America, since July, 1935. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Chin-ting, (沈覲鼎)** Courtesy name: Yueh-hsin 瀹新 Prefers English rendering: G. D. Shen. Born 1894; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Tokyo Imperial University. 1929-31, counsellor, Examination Yuan. 1932-4, director of Asiatic affairs, ministry of foreign affairs. Minister to Panama, since February, 1935. Author, Japanese translation of Sun Yat-sen's “Three Principles of the People.” — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN, Eugene** see Shen Yu-ch'ien. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN, G. D** see Shen Chin-ting. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Hung-lieh, (沈鴻烈)** Native of Hopei. 1928-32, commander, Northeastern squadron; promoted rear admiral. Mayor of Tsingtao, since 1930. Member, Peiping political council, and (its successor) Hopei-Chahar political council, since July, 1933. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Pai-hsien, (沈百先)** Prefers English rendering: Bazin D. Z. Shen. Born 1896; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. M.S. (Iowa). 1927-9, chief engineer, Taihu basin waterways commission. Commissioner of reconstruction, Kiangsu provincial government, since July, 1933. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN P'eng-fei, (沈鵬飛)** Born 1898; native of Kwangtung. B.F. (Oregon). M.F. (Yale). 1929-32, dean of agriculture, then dean of studies and acting president, Chungshan University, Canton. 1933, director of higher education, ministry of education. 1934-5, president, Chinan University, Chenju (Shanghai). Counsellor, ministry of education, since September, 1935. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN, S. K** see below. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Shu-yu, (沈叔玉)** Prefers English rendering: S. K. Shen. Born 1885; native of Foochow, Fukien. Studied in London and Vienna. 1924-6, managing director, Nanking-Shanghai and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo railways. 1932-4, director, customs administration, ministry of finance. Reappointed deputy director-general (first appointed, 1930-2), postal remittances and savings banks, since January, 1935. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Tsung-han, (沈宗瀚)** Born 1895; native of Yuyao, Chekiang. M.S.A. (Georgia). Ph.D. in agriculture (Cornell). Professor of plant breeding, University of Nanking, since 1927. Chief technician, national agricultural research bureau, ministry of industries, since 1934. Delegate to Pan-Pacific Science Congresses (1926, 1929, 1933). Author of over 30 technical papers published in research journals of U.S.A., Canada, and China. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Tz'u-liang, (沈嗣良)** Prefers English rendering: William Z. L. Sung. Born 1897; son of Shen Tsai-shen. B.A. (St. John's); M.A. (Columbia). Vice-president and dean, St. John's University, since 1928. Hon. general secretary China National Amateur Athletic Federation, since 1926. — p. 176 ([scan leaf 199](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n199/mode/1up))

**SHEN Yien-ping, (沈雁冰)** Pen name: Mao-t'un 茅盾 Native of T'unghsiang, Chekiang. 1921-4, editor, Short Story Monthly in Chinese. 1924-6, resigned to do revolutionary work in Shanghai. 1928-30, sojourned in Japan. One of the leading fiction writers of Young China. Author of numerous volumes of essays on Chinese and foreign mythologies, modern literature etc. One of these, entitled “From Kuling to Tokyo,” announcing his resolution thereafter to portray the life of the Chinese masses and encourage their uplift, attracted universal attention among literary circles. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHEN Yu-ch'ien, (沈有乾)** Prefers English rendering: Eugene Shen. Born 1900; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Ph.D. (Stanford). Professor of psychology. Chekiang University, since 1931. Well-known psychologist. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHENG Cheng-wei, (盛振爲)** Born 1900; native of Shanghai. B.A., LL.B. (Soochow). Dr. Juris (Northwestern). Sometime dean, Comparative Law School (Shanghai); editor-in-chief, Law Review (Chinese); professor of law, Chi Nan University, Chenju. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1934. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHIH Chao-chi, (施肇基)** Courtesy name: Chih-chih 植之 Prefers English rendering: Sao-ke Alfred Sze. Born 1876; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. B.A.; M.A. (Cornell). Hon. LL.D. (St. John's and Syracuse). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1914-20, minister to Great Britain; delegate, Paris peace conference. 1921-8, minister to U.S.A.; chief delegate, Washington and Geneva opium conferences. 1929-32, reappointed minister to Great Britain; delegate, League of Nations assembly and council. January-February, 1923, minister for foreign affairs. Retransferred minister, then ambassador, to U.S.A., since January, 1933. Now retired. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHIH Mei-yu, Miss, (石美玉女士)** Prefers English rendering: Mary Stone. One of earliest Chinese lady doctors educated in U.S.A. Father was first Christian pastor in Central China, and mother the first Bible-woman teacher. 1895-1920, conducted medical work in Kiukiang (Kiangsi), her native city. Founder, with Miss Jessie V. Hughes, and superintendent, Bethel Mission and Hospital, Shanghai, since 1920. This mission combines a hospital with the largest nurses' training school in China, an orphange, and school of evangelism, etc. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHIH Ying, (石瑛)** Born 1888; native of Hupeh. Graduate, Toulouse University. 1929-31, commissioner of reconstruction, Chekiang provincial government. March, 1932-March, 1935, mayor of Nanking. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since April, 1932. Minister of personnel, since October, 1935. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHOU Ching-wei, (壽景偉)** Courtesy name: Yi-ch'eng 毅成 Prefers English rendering: Kinn-wei Shaw. Born 1890; native of Chuchi, Chekiang, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1929-31, co-director and chief editor, bureau of foreign trade, ministry of industries. Sub-manager, Bank of China, Hangchow, since June, 1931. Author, “Democracy and Finance in China” (doctoral thesis), and “Elements of Fiscal Science” (in Chinese). — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SHU, C. M** see Hsü Chi-ch'in. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SIE Cheou-kang** see Hsieh Shou-k'ang. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SIE Cheou-tchang** see Hsieh Shou-ch'ang. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SOONG, Ching-ling** see Sung Ch'ing-ling. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SOONG, Mayling** see Sung Mei-ling. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SOONG, T. L** see Sung Tzu-liang. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SOONG, T. V** see Sung Tzu-wen. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**STONE, Mary** see Shih Mei-yü. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SU Hsueh-lin, Miss, (蘇雪林女士)** Courtesy name: Mei 梅 Pen Name: Lu-yi 綠漪 Wife of Chang Pao-ling 張寶齡夫人 Native of T'aip'ing, Anhwei. Graduate, Women's Higher Normal College, Peiping. 1921-5, studied in Ecole des Beaux Arts, Lyons. 1926-31. taught in Laura Hayward School, (Soochow), Shanghai and Anhwei Universities. Professor of Chinese literature, Wuhan University (Wuchang), since 1932. Prominent author, having published many novels in Chinese including “Li Yi-shan's Romance,” “Blue Sky,” “Bitter Heart,” “Life of a Poor Fish,” etc. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SU Ping-wen, (蘇炳文)** Courtesy name: Han-Chang 翰章 Born 1891; native of Hsinmin, Liaoning. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1930, commander, Chinese Eastern Railway defence force, Harbin-Manchouli section. Acclaimed as national hero for intrepid leadership of the volunteers along the western section of the C.E.R. and spurning all Japanese overtures to rejoin “Manchoukuo Government.” December, 1932, retreated into Soviet territory in face of superior enemy forces. June, 1933, returned to China, via Europe. Member, national military council, since 1934. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN, Clarence** see Sun To-yü. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN Fo** see Sun K'e. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN Fu-hsi, (孫福熙)** Courtesy name: Ch'un-t'ai 春苔 Born 1898; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Graduate, Peking National University and Lyons Fine Arts College. 1925, editor, Peiping Morning Post special supplement. Co-founder and professor, National College of Fine Arts, Hangchow, since 1928. Well-known writer; has published several volumes of essays since 1924. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN K'e, (孫科)** Courtesy name: Cheh-sheng 哲生 Prefers Cantonese rendering: Sun Fo. Born 1891; son of Dr. Sun Yat-sen. B.A. (California). M.S. (Columbia). Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government concurrently vice-president, Examination Yuan, and minister of railways. January-February, 1932, president, Executive Yuan. President, Legislative Yuan, since March, 1932. First president (1930), China National Aviation Corporation. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN Kuei-ting, (孫貴定)** Prefers to sign himself: K. T. Sen. Born 1892; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. 1922, Ph.D. (Edinburgh). Dean, college of education, Amoy University, since August, 1924. Author, “Group Consciousness with Special Reference to Educational Applications” (doctoral thesis), “Chinese Fables,” “Ethics,” etc. — p. 177 ([scan leaf 200](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n200/mode/1up))

**SUN, Right Rev. Melchior** see below. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN Teh-chen, (孫德禎)** Better known as the Right Rev. Melchior Sun. Born 1869; native of Peiping. January, 1897, ordained as priest. January, 1899, became Lazarist. 1900-12, professor of Latin, Catholic Seminary, Peiping. 1913-24, missionary at Niufang, near Peiping. April, 1924, elected prefect apostolic of Lihsien (Hopei). Consecrated at Rome as titular bishop of Hesebon and vicar apostolic of Ankuo (Hopei), since June, 1926. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN To-yu, (孫多鈺)** Courtesy name: Chang-fu 章甫 Prefers English rendering: Clarence Sun. Born 1876; native of Shouhsien, Anhwei. C.E. (Cornell). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1911-21, managing director of various railways. 1923-4, vice-minister of communications. President, Chung Foo Union Bank, Tientsin, since 1924. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN T'ung-kang, (孫桐崗)** Born 1910; native of Shantung. German-trained aviator. 1933, completed leisurely solo flight from Berlin to China. 1934, completed round-the-country flight on Junker plane, presented by his admirers, to instil air-mindedness. April, 1934, sailed for Europe and U.S.A. to study aviation at Government expense. May, 1936, returned to China. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN, Y. Ralph** see below. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN Yuan-fang, (孫元方)** Courtesy name: Ching-hsi 景西 Prefers English rendering: Y. Ralph Sun. Born 1883; cousin of Sun To-yü. Ph.B. (Brown). Manager, Fou Feng Flour Mill, and managing director, T'ung Feng Flour Mill, Shanghai, since 1916. Shanghai manager, Chung Foo Union Bank, since 1917. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUN Yuan-liang, (孫元良)** Born 1904; native of Huayang, Szechwan. Graduate, Whampoa Military Academy. Studied at Tokyo Military Staff College, 1931, commander, 1st brigade, 259th brigade, 87th division. Promoted deputy commander, 88th division, since May, 1932, in recognition of meritorious defence of Shanghai area. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Ai-ling, Miss, (宋[illegible]齡女士)** Wife of K'ung Hsiang-hsi 孔祥熙夫人 Eldest sister of Mesdames Sun Yat-sen and Chiang Kai-shek. Born 1888; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B.A., M.A. (Wellesley). Recognized as the ablest of the three distinguished sisters. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Cheh-yuan, (宋哲元)** Courtesy name: Ming-hsien 明軒 Born 1885; native of Hopei. 1925, commander, 11th division; military governor of Jehol. 1926-7, commander, Kuominchun 4th army. 1927-8, chairman, Shensi provincial government. Commander, 29th army, since 1929. 1932-5, chairman, Chahar provincial government. Chairman, Hopei-Chahar political council, concurrently chairman, Hopei provincial government, and pacification commissioner for Hopei and Chahar provinces, since December, 1935. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Ch'ing-ling, Miss, (宋慶齡女士)** Widow of Dr. Sun Yat-sen 孫中山夫人 Prefers Shanghai rendering: Soong Ching-ling. Born 1890; younger sister of Madame K'ung Hsiang-hsi. B.A. (Wesleyan). Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. Most outspoken critic of Kuomintang leaders who depart from labouring for what she calls “a revolution built on mass support and for the masses.” — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Fah-hsiang, (宋發祥)** Courtesy name: Chih-ch'ang 致長 Prefers English rendering: Fartsan T. Sung. Born 1883; native of Hinghwa, Fukien. B. Sc. (Michigan). Sometime director, Soochow and Nanking mints. 1925-8, managing director, Sino-Scandinavian Bank (Peiping). 1928-30, consul-general to Australia. Transferred consul-general to Batavia, since September, 1931. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG, Fartsan T** see above. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Han-chang, (宋漢章)** Born 1872; native of Yuyao, Chekiang. 1911-27, manager, Bank of China, Shanghai. 1925-8, chairman, Shanghai general chamber of commerce and Bankers' Association. General manager, Bank of China, since April, 1935. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Mei-ling, Miss, (宋美齡女士)** Wife of Chiang Kai-shek 蔣中正夫人 Prefers Shanghai rendering: Soong May-ling. Born 1892; youngest sister of Madame K'ung Hsiang-hsi. B.A. (Wellesley). 1929-32, legislative member, Legislative Yuan, Principal, School for the Orphans of the Revolution, Nanking, since 1929. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Tzu-liang, (宋子良)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: T. L. Soong. Born 1893; brother of T. V. Soong. Educated at St. John's University and in U.S.A. 1930-1, director of general affairs, ministry of foreign affairs. 1932-3, director, Whangpoo conservancy board. Managing director, Manufacturers Bank, Shanghai, since 1932. General manager, China Development Finance Corporation, Shanghai, since 1934. Commissioner of finance, Kwangtung provincial government, since July 20, 1936. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG Tsu-wen, (宋子文)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: T. V. Soong. Born 1891; brother of Mesdames K'ung Hsiang-hsi, Sun Yat-sen and Chiang Kai-shek. A.B. (Harvard). 1924, president Central Bank, Canton. 1925-7, minister of finance, Canton government. State councillor of the National Government, since 1928. Concurrently minister of finance (1928-33); vice-president, then acting president, Executive Yuan, and governor, Central Bank, Shanghai (1930-3). Delegate, world economic conference, London (1933). Executive member, national economic council, since 1932. Chairman, board of directors, Bank of China, since April, 1935. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SUNG, William Z. L** see Shen Tz'u-liang. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**SZE, Sao-ke Alfred** see Shih Chao-chi. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**TAI Chi, (戟戴)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-k'un 孝[illegible] Born 1894; native of Chinteh, Anhwei. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. Battalion commander and later commander, 4th regiment, 1st division, 1916; commander, 30th regiment, 10th division, 4th revolutionary army, during northern punitive expedition, 1926; commander, 24th division, 11th revolutionary army under Ch'en Ming-shu; transferred commander 26th division; commander, 61st division, 19th route army, 1930; garrison commander of Shanghai and Woosung, 1931, and took prominent part in Shanghai war of 1932; China's chief delegate to the Shanghai truce conference; chief-of-staff, 19th route army, 1933-4. Now living in retirement at Soochow. — p. 178 ([scan leaf 201](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n201/mode/1up))

**TAI Chi-tao** see below. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TAI Ch'uan-hsien, (戴傳賢)** Courtesy name: Chi-t'ao 季陶 Born 1890; native of Wuhsing, Chekiang. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government. President, Examination Yuan, since October, 1928. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1924. Chairman, board of trustees, Chungshan University, Canton. A Japanese scholar and Buddhist devotee. Author of “The Philosophical Foundations of Sunyatsenism,” “National Revolution and Chinese Kuomintang,” etc. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TAI En-sai, (戴恩賽)** Born 1892; native of Wuhua, Kwangtung. 1915, M.A.; 1918, Ph.D. (Columbia). 1921-2, mayor of Wuchow, Kwangsi. 1925-9, director-general of conservancy, Canton. 1929-33, minister to Brazil. February, 1935-July, 1936, superintendent of customs, Amoy. Transferred customs superintendent, Canton, since July 20, 1936. Author, “Treaty Ports in China: A Study in Diplomacy” (doctoral dissertation). — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'AI Hsu, Monk, (太虛法師)** Born 1889; native of Chungteh, Chekiang; Lu being his family name before entering Buddhist monastery. One of the most learned and enlightened Buddhist monks in China, who has achieved world renown. Has travelled in Europe, Japan, America, and South Sea Islands, and spoken in more than 50 high educational institutions. Author of (in Chinese): “Life as a Science,” “History of Liberty,” “T'ai Hsu's Essays,” “T'ai Hsu's Travels,” “An Introduction to the Study of Buddhism,” and “A.B.C.s of Buddhism,” etc. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TAINE, S. H** see T'an Hsi-hung. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'AN Chen, (覃振)** Courtesy name: Li-min 理鳴 Born 1883; native of T'aoyuan, Hunan. Graduate, Waseda University. 1912, senator. 1913, member of parliament. Vice-president, Judicial Yuan, since January, 1932. Concurrently chairman, commission for disciplinary punishment of public functionaries, since January, 1933. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'AN Hsi-hung, (譚熙鴻)** Courtesy name: Chung-k'uei 仲逵 Prefers English rendering: S. H. Taine. Born 1891; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Graduate, Paris and Toulouse Universities. 1919-27, dean of biology, Peking Government University. 1927-31, principal, Chekiang Agricultural College. Director, forestry and reclamation administration, ministry of industries, since January, 1932. Author of several volumes on the world's cotton supply, China's silk trade, etc. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TAN, S. H** see T'an Shao-hua. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'AN Shao-hua, (譚紹華)** Prefers English rendering: S. H. Tan. Born 1897; native of Taishan, Kwangtung. B.A. (Shanghai). Ph.D. (Chicago). 1926-8, professor of political science, Shanghai University. 1928-33, expert member, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs. Senior secretary, then counsellor, same ministry, since June, 1934. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TAN Teh-hsing** see T'ang Teh-hsin. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Chien-fei, (唐健飛)** Born 1902: native of Ninghsien, Hunan. Graduate, Hunan provincial law school, and Sun-yat-sen University (Moscow). Sometime instructor, Central Military Academy, Nanking; publicity director, political training department, Generalissimo's headquarters (Nanchang). Director of labour, ministry of industries, since January, 1935. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TANG, Chindon Yui** see Yü Ch'ing-t'ang. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Ch'u-wu, (唐聚五)** Born 1897: native of Shuangchen, Kirin. Graduate, Mukden Military Academy. Was regimental commander when Japanese invasion of Manchuria occurred. Distinguished himself as volunteers' leader in the eastern section of Liaoning province. July, 1932, elected commander-in-chief, 3rd volunteer army corps. Remembered by his men for having cut off one of his fingers and adjured them in writing, with the dripping blood, to recover their native soil. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Hai-an, (唐海安)** Prefers English rendering: H. O. Tong. Born 1894; native of Kwangtung. B.Sc. (London). Sometime director of wine and tobacco administration, Canton. 1929, customs superintendent, Chinkiang. April, 1931-July, 1936, superintendent of customs, Shanghai. Salt commissioner for Kwangtung province, since July, 1936. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**TANG Leang-li** see below. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Liang-li, (湯良禮)** Prefers own rendering: Tang Leang-li. Born 1901; native of Fukien. B.Sc. (London). 1929, principal correspondent in Europe of Kuomintang central executive committee. Managing editor, The People's Tribune (Shanghai), since 1930. Political associate and English secretary to Wang Ching-wei. Author, “China in Revolt,” “Inner History of the Chinese Revolution,” “Wang Ching-wei: A Political Biography,” etc. Editor, “Japanese Invasion and China's Defence” and “China To-Day” series. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Pao-shu, (唐寶書)** Courtesy name: Tsung-lin 宗麟 Prefers own rendering: Tong Pao-shu. Born 1891; native of Kwangtung. Graduate, Berlin University. 1921-32, organizer and general manager, Shanghai branch, National Commercial and Savings Bank of Hongkong. 1933-4, director-general of postal remittances and savings banks. Chairman, purchasing commission, ministry of communications, since February, 1935. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Shao-yi, (唐紹儀)** Courtesy name: Shao-ch'uan 少川 Born 1860; native of Hsiangshan, Kwangtung. 1912, first premier of the Republic. 1922, minister of finance, Canton Government. 1928-34, magistrate of native district. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 179 ([scan leaf 202](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n202/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Sheng-chih, (唐生智)** Born 1885; native of Hunan. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy. 1929, commander, 5th route army. December, 1929, revolted. December 1931, pardoned. 1932-4, chairman, military advisory council. Director-general of military training, since November, 1934. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Shou-min, (唐壽民)** Born 1891; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. Sometime assistant manager, Shanghai Commercial and Savings Bank; director, central mint; general manager, banking department, Central Bank; general manager, Manufacturers Bank. General manager, Bank of Communications, since October, 1933. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Teh-hsin, (唐德炘)** Courtesy name: Hsueh-ch'iao 雪樵 Prefers own rendering: Tan Teh-hsing. Born 1875; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Mawei Naval College. Served in the navy, since 1901. Director of naval operations, ministry of navy, since June,1929. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TANG, W. K** see below. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'ANG Wen-k'ai, (唐文愷)** Courtesy name: Po-yuan 伯原 Prefers English rendering: W. K. Tang. Born 1893; native of K'unshan, Kiangsu. B.A. (Carleton). M.A. (Chicago). 1924-8, dean, school of commerce, Nankai University. Secretary-general, Chinese Bankers' Institute, Shanghai, since 1932. Concurrently director, Institute of Social and Economic Research, Shanghai, since 1934. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO Chih-hsing, (陶知行)** Prefers English rendering: W. T. Tao. Born 1892; native of Hsihsien, Anhwei. M.A. (Columbia). Sometime dean, school of education, Southeastern University. Recognized educational leader. Author of numerous books and essays. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO, L. K** see T'ao Lu-kung. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO Lu-ch'ien, (陶履謙)** Courtesy name: Yi-sheng 益生 Prefers English rendering: Yeesheng T. L. Tao. Born 1889; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. 1927-8, director of general affairs, ministry of foreign affairs. 1929, commissioner for foreign affairs, Canton. 1930-1, inspecting commissioner of foreign affairs for Chekiang, Fukien, Kwangtung, Kwangsi and Kweichow provinces. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Political vice-minister of interior, since March, 1935. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO Lu-kung, (陶履恭)** Courtesy name: Meng-he 孟和 Prefers English rendering: L. K. Tao. Born 1887; native of Tientsin, Hopei. B.Sc. (London). 1914-25, professor of sociology, Peking Government University; dean of faculties, Teachers College, Peiping. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Director, Social Research Institute, Peiping, since 1928. Co-author, “Village and Town Life in China.” — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO Shan-min, Miss, (陶善敏女士)** Prefers English rendering: S. M. Tao. Born 1896; native of Chekiang. 1922, B.A. (Ginling). B.S. (Michigan). 1927, Sc.D. (Johns Hopkins). 1927-8, senior bacteriologist, Michigan State department of health. 1929-31, senior bacteriologist, national epidemic prevention bureau. 1931-3, professor of bacteriology and director of clinical laboratories, Margaret Williamson Hospital (Shanghai). Chief of vaccine division, national epidemic prevention bureau, Peiping, since 1933. Delegate of national health administration to 2nd international congress for microbiology, London (June, 1936). Author of many papers on bacteriological, parasitological and public health subjects. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TAO, S. M** see above. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'AO Wei-sun, Miss, (陶慰孫女士)** Born 1898; native of Shanghai. 1918, graduate, Tokyo High Normal School. 1921, B.Sc. (Columbia). 1922, M.A. (Cornell). 1922-7, professor of chemistry, Tatung University, Shanghai. 1927-31, research student, Kyoto Imperial University. July, 1933, granted degree of D.Sc. by the Japanese minister of education (being first Chinese woman to receive doctor's degree from Japan). Author, “Scientific Study of the Digestibility of Rice Starch” (doctoral thesis). — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TAO, W. T** see T'ao Chih-hsing. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TAO, Yeeshing T. L** see T'ao Lü-ch'ien. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TEH-MU-CH'U-K'EH-TUNG-LU-PU, (德穆楚克棟魯布)** Native of Inner Mongolia. Better known as Prince Teh. President, Silingol League, Inner Mongolia. Administrative member, Chahar provincial government, since 1932. Moving spirit in recent movement for Mongolian local autonomy. Secretary-general, then concurrently vice-chairman, Mongolian local autonomy political council, since March, 1934. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TCHENG Loh** see Ch'en Lu. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TCHOU, Louis Ngaosiang** see Chu Huo-hsiang — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TCHOU, Moutchen Thomas** see ChuYü-ts'ang. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TENG Cheh-hsi, (鄧哲熙)** Courtesy name: Chung-chih 仲知 Born 1894; native of Hopei. Graduate, Hopei Provincial Law College, and Meiji University. Sometime president, Kaifeng high court; commissioner of civil affairs, Honan provincial government. 1932-3, vice-chairman, national opium suppression commission. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since September, 1933. President, Hopei high court, since May, 1936. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TENG Chia-yien, (鄧家彥)** Courtesy name: Meng-shih 孟碩 Native of Kwangsi. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. State councillor of the National Government since January, 1934. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan (1933). — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**T'ENG Kuei, (滕圭)** Courtesy name: Po-yeh 白也 Born 1900; native of Hunan. B. A. (Washington). M.A. (Harvard). Sometime professor of art, Yenching University. At present sculptor, and professor of art in Shanghai University. Leading Chinese exponent of fingernail painting (ancient style). Has works permanently on exhibit in various museums in U.S.A. Awarded first prize (May, 1936) for bust of Dr. Sun Yat-sen to be erected in bronze in “Banking Circle,” Chungshan Avenue, Nanking. — p. 180 ([scan leaf 203](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n203/mode/1up))

**TIAO Hsin-teh, (刁信德)** Prefers English rendering: Edward Sintuk Tyau. Born 1879; native of Hsingning, Kwangtung. M.D. (St. John's). D.Ph. (Pennsylvania). Senior medical officer, St. Luke's Hospital (Shanghai), since 1908. 1928-31, dean, St. John's University Medical School; superintendent, Chinese Red Cross General Hospital. Past president, National Medical Association. Vice-president, Chinese mission to lepers, since 1932. Leader of Chinese Oxford Group movement in Shanghai. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TIAO Min-ch'ien, (刁敏謙)** Courtesy name: Teh-jen 德仁 Prefers English rendering: M. T. Z. Tyau. Born 1888; cousin of Tiao Hsin-teh. LL.B., LL.D. (London). 1917-20, founder and editor, Peking Leader. 1929-31, director of publicity, then adviser, ministry of foreign affairs. Editor, China Press Weekly, since August, 1935. Author, “Legal Obligations Arising Out of Treaty Relations between China and Other States” (doctoral thesis), “London through Chinese Eyes,” “China Awakened,” “Junior Brighter English Conversations,” etc. Editor, “Two Years of Nationalist China”; and managing editor (1923-30), Chinese Social and Political Science Review (Peiping). — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TIAO Tso-ch'ien, (刁作謙)** Courtesy name: Ch'eng-chang 成章 Prefers English rendering: Philip K. C. Tyau. Born 1880; elder brother of Tiao Min-ch'ien. M.A., LL.B. (Cantab.); barrister-at-law (Middle Temple). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1921-6, minister to Cuba and Panama. 1928-33, managing director, Peiping Leader. Consul-general at Singapore, since December, 1933. Golf champion of North China (1929-33). — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TIEN, C. C** see below. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**T'IEN Chun-chin, (田[illegible]錦)** Courtesy name: Yün-ch'ing 雲青 Prefers English rendering: C. C. Tien. Born 1899; native of Ch'ingyang, Kansu. Ph.D. (Illinois). 1930-1, professor of political science, Northeastern University, Shenyang. Supervisory member, Control Yuan, since 1931. Author, “State Supervision over Municipal Finance in England and U.S.A” (doctoral thesis). — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TING Ch'ao-wu, (丁超五)** Courtesy name: Li-fu 立夫 Born 1884; native of Shaowu, Fukien. 1912, member of parliament. 1927, commissioner of reconstruction, Fukien provincial government. 1933-4, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1930. Supervisory commissioner for Control Yuan in Kiangsu province, since April, 1935. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TING Hsieh-lin, (丁燮林)** Courtesy name: Hsuan-fu 巽甫 Born 1893; native of T'aihsing, Kiangsu. M.Sc. (Birmingham). Sometime professor of physics, Peking Government University. Director, research institute of physics, Academia Sinica, since 1928. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TING Ling** see Chiang Ting-ling. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TING Kuei-t'ang, (丁貴堂)** Courtesy name: Yung-chieh 榮階 Prefers English rendering: K. T. Ting. Born 1891; native of Haicheng, Liaoning. Graduate, Customs College, Peiping. Appointed assistant, 1917; promoted deputy commissioner, 1927, and commissioner, 1929. Chief secretary to inspector-general of customs 1934-5. Special delegate of ministry of finance to study customs administration in Europe and America, 1935. Now Chinese secretary, inspectorate-general of customs, Shanghai. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TING Wei-fen, (丁惟汾)** Courtesy name: Ting-ch'en 鼎臣 Born 1874; native of Jihchao, Shantung. Graduate, Meiji University, Tokyo. 1912-3, member of parliament. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1924. Concurrently director of training (1928-9) and secretary general (1931), central Kuomintang headquarters. 1932-5, vice-president, Control Yuan. Dean of studies, Central Political Institute, since 1934. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TONG, H. O** see T'ang Hai-an. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TONG, Hollington K** see TungHsien-kuang. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TONG Pao-shu** see T'ang Pao-shu. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TS'AI Ching-chun, (蔡勁軍)** Born 1892; native of Wanning, Kwangtung. Graduate, Whampoa Military Academy. 1932-4, director of general affairs, Generalissimo's headquarters (Nanchang). Commissioner of public safety, city government of Greater Shanghai, since March, 1935. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TS'AI T'ing-k'ai, (蔡廷鍇)** Courtesy name: Hsien-ch'u 賢初 Born 1890; native of Lotien, Kwangtung. Joined army at 16; noted for courage and gallantry at early age; worked way up from common soldier; before Sino-Japanese hostilities in spring of 1932, was commander of 19th route army transferred to Kiangsu garrisoning Shanghai-Nanking area with headquarters at Shanghai; fought Japanese and won title of heroic defender of Shanghai: after Shanghai war, promoted commander-in-chief, 19th army and concurrently pacification commissioner of Fukien; played leading part in Fukien independence movement, 1933-4. Now living in retirement. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TS'AI Tseng-chi, (蔡增基)** Prefers anglicized Cantonese rendering: J. K. Choy. Born 1891; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B.S. (Columbia). 1929-30, managing director, Nanking-Shanghai and Shanghai-Hangchow-Ningpo railways. 1932-5, commissioner of finance, city government of GreaterShanghai. General manager, C. M. S. N. Co., since March, 1936. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TSAI, V** see below. — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TS'AI Wu-chi, (蔡無忌)** Prefers own rendering: V. Tsai. Born 1898; native of Shaohsing, Chekiang. Ingénieur agricole and médecin vétérinaire (Grignon and Alfort). 1926, veterinarian, Shanghai French municipal council. 19[illegible]7, dean, college of agriculture, Central University, Nanking. Rose to become director, Shanghai bureau of inspection and testing of commercial products, since 1930. Concurrently principal, Veterinary College (Shanghai). — p. 181 ([scan leaf 204](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n204/mode/1up))

**TS'AI Yuan-p'ei, (蔡元培)** Courtesy name: Chieh-min 子民 Born 1867; native of Shanying, Chekiang. One of Kuomintang's “Elder Statesmen”. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1912, again 1927, minister of education, Nanking. 1917-23, chancellor, Peking Government University. State councillor of the National Government, concurrently president, Academia Sinica, since October, 1928. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1924. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TS'AO Ching-yuan, (曹經沅)** Courtesy name: Hsiang-heng 纕蘅 Born 1891; native of Mienchu, Szechwan. Graduate, Chunghua University, Peiping. 1927-8, counsellor, ministry of interior. 1930-1, secretary-general, Anhwei provincial government. 1932-4, counsellor, Executive Yuan. Commissioner of civil affairs, Kweichow provincial government, since April, 1935. Well-known poet. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TS'AO Hao-sen, (曹浩森)** Born 1888; native of Tuchang, Kiangsi. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1928-9, administrative member, Kiangsi provincial government. Administrative vice-minister of military affairs, since July, 1931. Concurrently director, army administration, same ministry, since January, 1929. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TS'AO Po-wen, (曹伯聞)** Born 1893; native of Changsha, Hunan. Graduate, Peiping Military College. 1926, salt commissioner, Changsha. 1927, commissioner of reconstruction, Hunan provincial government. Commissioner of civil affairs, same provincial government, since January, 1929. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSAO, Y. H** see below. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TS'AO Yen-shen, (曹炎申)** Prefers English rendering: Y. H. Tsao. Native of Kwangtung. B.A. (Yale). D.Litt. (California). 1926-34, general secretary, Chinese Y.M.C.A., Hongkong. Principal, Shanghai Y.M.C.A. schools, since October, 1934. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSEN, L** see Cheng Lieh. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Hsu-pai, (曾虛白)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Tseng Hyui-puh. Born 1895; native of Kiangsu. B.A. (St. John's). 1929-31, proprietor and chief editor, Vrai Beau Bien monthly. 1930-1, professor of Chinese, Nanking University. Managing editor, Ta Wan Pao (China Evening News), Shanghai, since March, 1932. Author of “Sister Teh,” “Devil's Den,” “Combustible Heart,” and “Three Prickles”—all written in spoken language. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Hyui-puh** see above. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Pao-sun, Miss, (曾寶蓀女士)** Courtesy name: Hao-ju 浩如 Born 1894; native of Sianghsiang, Hunan. B.Sc. (London). Founder and principal, I Fang Girls' Collegiate School, Changsha, since 1918. Concurrently (1919) principal, Girls Normal School, same city. 1928, delegate, International Missionary Council conference, Jerusalem. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG, T. K** see Tseng Tsung-chien. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Tsung-chien, (曾宗鑒)** Courtesy name: Yung p'u 鎔圃 Prefers English rendering: T. K. Tseng. Born 1882; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.A. (Cantab.). 1926-8, minister to Sweden and Norway. Secretary-general, commission for readjustment of domestic and foreign loans, since January, 1929. Trustee and treasurer, British Boxer Indemnity fund administration, since 1930. Administrative vice-minister of railways, since December, 1935. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Yang-feng, (曾仰豐)** Courtesy name: Ching-nan 景南 Born 1887; native of Fukien. B.Sc. (Peiyang). B.Sc. (Illinois). Sometime engineer to Hankow-Szechwan railway, Hulutao harbour administration, and Yellow River conservancy board; salt commissioner for Chekiang and Kiangsu provinces. 1933, chief inspector, salt chief inspectorate. Salt commissioner for Changlu district (Hopei), since 1934. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSENG Yang-fu, (曾養甫)** Born 1892; native of P'ingyuen, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peiyang and Pittsburgh universities. 1928-32, vice-chairman, national reconstruction commission. 1932-5, commissioner of reconstruction, Chekiang provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. December, 1935-July, 1936, political vice-minister of railways. Transferred mayor of Canton, since July, 1936. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSEU Yi-zan** see Chou Yuen-jan. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSIANG, T. F** see Chiang T'ing-fu. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSIANG Tso Ping** see Chiang Tso-pin. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSIEN Tai** see Ch'ien T'ai. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSOU Lin, (鄒琳)** Courtesy name: Yü-lin 玉林 Prefers own rendering: Chow Lin. Native of T'aip'u, Kwangtung. Graduate, Peking Law College. 1928-32, director, central salt administration. Political vice-minister of finance, since August, 1932. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSOU Lu, (鄒魯)** Courtesy name: Hai-ping 海濱 Prefers own rendering: Chow Lou. Born 1886; elder brother of Tsou Lin. Graduate, Waseda University. Sometime member of parliament. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. Chancellor, Chungshan University, Canton, since 1931. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSOU Ping-wen, (鄒秉文)** Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. M.S. (Cornell). 1917-27, dean, school of agriculture, Southeastern University. 1928-32, director, Shanghai bureau for testing and inspection of commercial commodities. Assistant manager, Shanghai Commercial and Savings Bank, since May, 1932. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSU, P. N** see Chu Pao-yuan. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSU, Y. Y** see Chu Yu-yü. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSUI, Right Rev. Joseph** see below. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TS'UI Shou-hsun, (崔守恂)** Better known as the Right Rev. Joseph Tsui. Born 1877; native of Hochien, Hopei. Studied at Sienhsien. 1904-28, ordained as priest; missionary. 1929, appointed prefect apostolic. Consecrated at Rome and promoted titular bishop of Tona and vicar apostolic of Yungnien (Hopei), since June, 1933. — p. 182 ([scan leaf 205](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n205/mode/1up))

**TSUR, Y. T** see Chou Yi-ch'un. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TU-YIEN** see Kuo Mo-jo. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TU Yueh-sen** see Tu Yung. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TU Yung, (杜鏞)** Courtesy name: Yueh-sheng 月笙 Better known as: Dou Yu-seng, or Tu Yueh-sen. Born 1887; native of Shanghai. Most influential resident in Shanghai French Concession; well known public welfare worker. President, Chung Wai Bank and Tung Wai Bank, Shanghai. Managing director, Hua Feng Paper Mill, Hangchow. President, Shanghai Emergency Hospital and Jen Chi Hospital, Ningpo. Founder and chairman, board of directors, Cheng Shih Middle School, Shanghai. Councillor, French municipal council, 1931-5. Decorated by the National Government for munificent contributions towards flood relief and purchase of aeroplanes. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUAN Cheng-yuan, (段正元)** Better known as Tuan Ssu-tsun, or Tuanfucius, among his numerous followers. Born 1863; native of Weiyuen, Szechwan. Having mastered Buddhism at age of eighteen, for more than fifty years has been preaching the principles of peace and those taught by Confucius. Claiming to have seen a spiritual vision, while meditating on top of Mount Omei, he wrote “The Origin of the Universe” at age of forty. Acclaimed by his followers as “the only sage after Confucius and Mencius,” it is said that his prophecies always come true and “his sincerity is that of the gods.” The immediate answer to his prayers for rain, during the summer drought of 1934, in Chekiang province and his warning concerning another world war are instrumental in organizing the present preparatory committee for the World Peace Prayer Meeting. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUANFUCIUS** see above. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUAN Hsi-p'eng, (段錫朋)** Courtesy name: Shu-yi 書貽 Born 1896; native of Yunghsien, Kiangsi. B.A. (Peking Government). M.A. (Columbia). Political vice-minister of education, since January, 1932. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUAN Ssu-tsun** see Tuan Cheng-yuan. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUNG Hsien-kuang, (董顯光)** Prefers English rendering: Hollington K. Tong. Born 1887; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. B.A. (Missouri). 1913-7, editor, Peking Daily News. 1926-31, editor, Yung Pao, Tientsin. June, 1931-December, 1934, managing editor, then managing director, China Press, Shanghai. Managing director, China Times (Shanghai), since 1935. Author, “Men and Events in the Far East.” — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TUNG Ta-yu, (董大酉)** Prefers English rendering: Dayu Doon. Born 1900; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. B.S., M.S. (Minnesota). 1928-30, joined E. Suensen & Co., Shanghai architects. Chief architect in charge of Greater Shanghai new civic centre development, since 1931. Secretary, Society of Chinese Architects, and editor, architectural section, Journal of Chinese Institute of Engineers, Shanghai. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TYAU, Edward Sintuk** see Tiao Hsin-teh. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TYAU, M. T. Z** see Tiao Min-ch'ien. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**TYAU, Philip K. C** see Tiao Tso-ch'ien. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WAN Fu-lin, (萬福麟)** Courtesy name: Shou-shan 壽山 Native of Nungan, Kirin. Sometime commander, northeastern 8th army. 1929-31, chairman, Heilungkiang provincial government. Commander, reorganized 53rd army, since 1932. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG, C. C** see Wang Ching-ch'un. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Chao-ming, (汪兆銘)** Courtesy name: Ching-wei 精衞 Born 1884; native of P'anyu, Kwangtung Graduate, Tokyo Law College. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. December, 1929, expelled from Kuomintang. December, 1931, reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. 1932-5, president, Executive Yuan. Concurrently minister for foreign affairs. Leader of Kuomintang left wing. Constituted with Chiang Kai-shek and late Hu Han-min, Kuomintang's dominant triumvirs. Author, “The World and China after the Paris Conference” (in Chinese and English). November, 1935, wounded by political assassins. Elected chairman, central political council, since December, 1935. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG, C. H** see below. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Cheng-hsu, (王正序)** Prefers English rendering: C. H. Wang. Born 1893; youngest brother of Wang Cheng-t'ing. 1916, B.A. (Yale). 1917, M.A. (Princeton). 1923-32, exchange manager, Ho Hong Bank, then assistant general manager, Oversea-Chinese Bank, Singapore. 1932-5, Shanghai manager, Oversea-Chinese Banking Corporation. New York manager, Bank of China, since May, 1936. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Cheng-t'ing, (王正廷)** Courtesy name: Ju-t'ang 儒堂 Prefers English rendering: Chengting T. Wang. Born 1882: native of Fenghua, Chekiang. B.A. (Yale). Hon. LL.D. (St. John's and Yenching). 1923, also 1925-6, and June, 1928-September, 1931, minister for foreign affairs. 1925-6, concurrently premier and minister of finance. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1930. Managing director, Liuhokou Coal Mining Co., and chairman, board of directors, Kiaochow-Tsinan railway, since 1923. President, National Good Roads Association, since 1926; concurrently chairman, National Amateu-Athletic Federation. President, China University (Peiping), since 1928. Appointed ambassador to U.S.A., August, 1936. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG, Chengting T** see above. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Chih-p'ing, (王治平)** Courtesy name: Hua-ch'ing 化青 Born 1879; native of Peiping. M.A., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1917-9, dean, Chinese department, and professor of history, Peking Methodist University. 1924-8, pastor, Astbury Church, Peiping; general secretary, Tientsin Y.M.C.A. Elected bishop of Methodist Church, since 1930. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Ching-ch'i, (王景岐)** Courtesy name: Shih-sun 石蓀 Prefers own rendering: Wang King-ky. Born 1884; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Ecole Libre des Sciences Politiques, Paris; Hon. LL.D. (Louvain). 1921-9, minister to Belgium; delegate, League of Nations assembly and council. 1930-2, president, National Labour University, Shanghai. 1929-36, adviser, then vice-chairman, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs. Author, “La Voix de la Chine.” Minister to Norway and Sweden since June, 1936. — p. 183 ([scan leaf 206](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n206/mode/1up))

**WANG Ching-ch'un, (王景春)** Courtesy name: Chao-hsi 兆熙 Prefers English rendering: C. C. Wang. Born 1882; native of Luanchow, Hopei. C.E. (Yale). M.A., Ph.D. (Illinois). Sometime chairman, commission on unification of railway accounts and statistics; director-general, Chinese Eastern Railway. Director, Chinese Government railways purchasing commission, London, since 1931. Inventor of phonetic system for telegraphing Chinese characters. Author, “Legislative Regulation of Railway Finance in England” (doctoral thesis). — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Ching-wei** see Wang Chao-ming. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Cho-jan, (王卓然)** Courtesy name: Chün-po 廻波 Born 1894; native of Fushun, Liaoning. M.A. (Columbia). Sometime director of Liaoning provincial government's students in U.S.A. and Europe; counsellor, Peiping branch, national military council. Managing editor, Foreign Affairs (Chinese monthly), Peiping, since 1932. Concurrently acting president, Northeastern University. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Ch'ung-hui, (王寵惠)** Courtesy name: Liang-ch'ou 亮疇 Born 1882; native of Tungkuan, Kwangtung. D.C.L. (Yale); barrister-at-law (Middle Temple). July-December, 1922, again January, 1924, premier. 1927-8, minister of justice. October, 1928-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government and president, Judicial Yuan. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since March, 1929. 1930-5, judge, permanent court of international justice. Resigned, May, 1936. Appointed state councillor of National Government, January 14, 1937; minister of foreign affairs, February 3, 1937; and acting president of Executive Yuan, April 6, 1937. Author of English translation of German civil code (doctoral thesis). — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Fa-ch'in, (王法勤)** Native of Chekiang. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1926. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG, Right Rev. Francis** see Wang Tseh-p'u. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Hsiang, (王驤)** Native of Shansi. Distinguished himself as principal, first Shansi provincial middle school. 1932-4, managing director, Shansi Provincial Bank. Administrative member, and concurrently commissioner of reconstruction, Shansi provincial government, since March, 1935. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Hsiao-lai, (王孝賚)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-lai 曉籟 Born 1886; native of Ch'enghsien, Chekiang. Chairman, Shanghai Chinese ratepayers association, since 1933. Supervisor, Chinese Stock Exchange, since January, 1935. Chairman, Shanghai city government provisional assembly, since February, 1935. Business magnate in silk, tea and cotton yarn trades. “Proud father of the largest family,” with 31 children. Vice-chairman, China Aviation League, since January, 1936. Re-elected chairman, Shanghai general chamber of commerce, since July, 1936. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG, Hsiao-ying, Miss, (王孝英女士)** Born 1890; native of P'ingnan, Fukien. Graduate, Women's Normal College, Peiping. 1927, magistrate, native district—being first of her sex in China to be made civil official. 1928-34, principal, Wu-pen Girls' Middle School, Shanghai. Principal, Shanghai Middle School, since 1935. Concurrently legislative member, Legislative Yuan (1933-4). — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Hsin-kung, (王星拱)** Courtesy name: Fu-wu 撫五 Prefers to sign as: Wang Sing-kung. Born 1889; native of Anhwei. A.R.C.S. and D.I.C. (London). 1917-26, professor of chemistry, Peking Government University. 1926-8, professor of chemistry, Central University, Nanking. 1929-33, professor and dean, school of science, Wuhan University (Wuchang). President, same institution, since November, 1933. Author (in Chinese), “Introduction to Science” and “Scientific Method.” — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Huai-chung, (王懷忠)** Courtesy name: Po-yuan 伯元 Better known as Wong Payuen. Born 1891; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. General manager, China Land Bank, since 1927. Director of several banks and industrial enterprises; interested in education and has endowed eighty odd scholarships. Committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1935. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG I-t'ang, (王揖唐)** Courtesy name: I-t'ang 一堂 Born 1877; native of Hofei, Anhwei. Chin-shih of Imperial examinations. 1913, senator. 1915-6, civil governor of Kirin; minister of interior. 1918-23, speaker, house of representatives; proscribed as leader of Anfu Party until pardoned. Administrative member, Peiping political council, then (its successor) Hopei-Chahar political council, since September, 1933. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG K'eh-min, (王克敏)** Courtesy name: Shu-lu 叔魯 Born 1875; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1917, again 1923-4, minister of finance. 1922-3, governor, Bank of China. 1933-5, director, finance division, Peiping political council. Member, Hopei-Chahar political council, since November, 1935. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG King-ky** see Wang Ching-ch'i. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Ku-p'an, (王固磐)** Native of Tientsin, Hopei. Educated in Germany. 1933-4, commissioner of public safety, Amoy, and concurrently magistrate of Szeming district (Fukien). 1935, promoted mayor of Amoy. Commissioner of police, Nanking, since November, 1935. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Li-ming, Miss, (王立明女士)** Wife of Herman C. E. Liu 劉湛恩夫人 Born 1897; native of Anhwei. B.A. (Northwestern). President, Women Suffrage Association; vice-president, Birth Control League; general secretary, Women's Christian Temperance Union; founder, Shanghai Settlement House for beggars and slave girls. Editor-in-chief, Temperance Monthly. Author, “Happy Home,” “Chinese Women's Movement,” “Life's Uproar,” etc. — p. 184 ([scan leaf 207](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n207/mode/1up))

**WANG Lu-i, (王陸一)** Native of Sanyuan, Shensi. Graduate, Sun Yat-sen University, Moscow. Sometime secretary-general, central Kuomintang headquarters. Secretary-general, Control Yuan, since December, 1931. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since November, 1931.
