Concurrently vice-chairman, committee for direction of people's movement, central Kuomintang headquarters. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG, Lucy C** see Wang Shih-chin. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG, Right Rev. Paul** see Wang Wen-ch'eng. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Po-ch'un, (王伯羣)** Born 1885; native of Hsingi, Kweichow. Studied in Japan. 1923, civil governor of Kweichow. 1928-31, minister of communications. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since June, 1929. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. President, Great China University (Shanghai), since 1928. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Po-ling, (王伯齡)** Courtesy name: Mou-ju 茂如 Native of Yangchow, Kiangsu. Studied in Japan. 1928, commissioner of reconstruction, Kiangsu provincial government. 1929-32, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Administrative member, Kiangsu provincial government, since 1930. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Shih-chieh, (王世杰)** Courtesy name: Hsueh-t'ing 雪艇 Born 1891; native of Hupeh. B.Sc. (London). Docteur en droit (Paris). 1927-8, director of law codification, Nanking. 1929-33, legislative member, Legislative Yuan; president, Wuhan University, Wuchang. Minister of education, since October, 1933. Author of treatises on comparative constitutional law, Shanghai International Settlement, etc. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Shih-chin, Miss, (王世靜女士)** Prefers English rendering: Lucy C. Wang. Born 1899; native of Foochow, Fukien, B.A. (Iowa). M.A. (Michigan). President. Hwa Nan College, Foochow, since January, 1930. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Shu-ch'ang, (王樹常)** Courtesy name: T'ing-wu 庭五 Native of Liaoch'ung, Liaoning. Graduate, Tokyo Military Cadets' School. 1931-2, chairman, Hopei provincial government. 1933-5, garrison commander, Peiping and Tientsin areas. Vice-president, military advisory council, since October, 1935. Appointed pacification commissioner of Kansu, January, 1937. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Shu-fang, (王漱芳)** Courtesy name: Yi-p'u 藝圃 Born 1901; native of Kweichow. Graduate, Southeastern University (Nanking). Sometime secretary-general, Nanking city government: counsellor, Control Yuan. 1933-5, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Reappointed secretary-general, Nanking city government, since April, 1935. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Shu-han, (王樹翰)** Courtesy name: Wei-yü 維宙 Native of Liaoning. Sometime commissioner of finance, Liaoning provincial government: civil governor of Kirin. State councillor of National Government, since December, 1930. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Sing-Kung** see Wang Hsin-Kung. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Teh-lin, (王德林)** Born 1878; native of Yishui, Shantung. Well-known leader of volunteers operating against Japanese and “Manchoukuo” forces in Kirin, is noted for courage and resourcefulness. October, 1931, led 500 of most devoted followers to Tunhwa to organize national salvation army. In seven months his strength increased to 30,000 and won most of the hundred odd engagements with enemy forces. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Tseh-p'u, (王澤溥)** Better known as the Right Rev. Francis Wang. Born 1872; native of Chungking, Szechwan. Studied at Sha-ping-pa. 1909-29, ordained as priest; missionary and editor; professor of Chinese literature, Sha-ping-pa Seminary; vicar of Sha-ping-pa cathedral. Consecrated titular bishop of Sinda and vicar apostolic of Wanhsien (Szechwan), since February, 1930. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Tseng-shan, (王曾善)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-hsien 孝先 Born 1902; native of Linch'ing, Shantung. M.A. (Istanbul). Sometime reorganization officer for Kuomintang affairs in Sinkiang province; assistant editor, Mohammedan section, Mongolian and Tibetan Review; member, Executive Yuan's Sinkiang rehabilitation and planning commission. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1934. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Wen-ch'eng, (王文成)** Better known as the Right Rev. Paul Wang. Born 1881; native of Szechwan. Studied at Ho-pa-chang Seminary. 1911-29, ordained as priest; missionary. Consecrated as titular bishop of Olena and vicar apostolic of Shunch'ing (Szechwan), since February, 1930. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Yen-sung, (王延松)** Prefers English rendering: Y. S. Wong. Born 1900; native of Shangyu, Chekiang. Chairman, board of directors, Shanghai Silk Industry Commercial and Savings Bank, since 1930, and Chinese General Insurance Co., since February, 1932. Standing member, executive committee, Shanghai general chamber of commerce, since 1931. Concurrently member, supervisory committee, Shanghai district Kuomintang headquarters. Committee member, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1934. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Yu-ling, (汪有齡)** Courtesy name: Tzu-chien 子健 Born 1879; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. 1912, vice-minister of justice. 1914-20, senator; chief editor, Kung Yen Pao. Legal practitioner in Peiping and Shanghai, since 1921. Founder and president, Ch'ao Yang University[illegible] Peiping. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG, Y. W** see below. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Yun-wu, (王雲五)** Courtesy name: Chih-jui or Yu-lu 之瑞岫廬 Prefers English rendering: Y. W. Wang. Born 1889; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. 1920-8, editor-in-chief, Commercial Press, Shanghai. Managing director, concurrently head, production department, same institution, since 1928. Well-known author and lexicographer. Chairman, Library Association, Shanghai. — p. 185 ([scan leaf 208](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n208/mode/1up))

**WANG Yung-ping, (王用賓)** Courtesy name: T'ai-sheng 太蕤 Born 1881; native of Yihsien, Shansi. Graduate, Tokyo Law College; member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1911, speaker, Shansi provincial assembly. 1913, senator. 1929-32, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. 1929-34, vice-chairman, then chairman, examinations commission, Examination Yuan. Minister of justice, since November, 1934. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI Ch'iue, (韋愨)** Courtesy name: P'eng-tan 捧丹 Prefers to sign himself: Sidney K. Wei. Born 1896; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. B. A. (Oberlin); Ph.D. (Chicago). Instructor, Canton Christian College, 1920-3; assistant professor, same college, 1923-5; lecturer, Canton Teachers' College, 1920-3; lecturer, National Kwangtung University, 1923-5; secretary, ministry of foreign affairs, Canton Government, 1921; commissioner of education, Kwangtung, 1922-3; director, bureau of international affairs, ministry of foreign affairs, nationalist government, 1925-7; chief of social education department, ministry of education, 1928; commissioner of education, Greater Shanghai, 1928-9; dean, college of education, National Central University, Nanking, 1929-30; now editor and compiler, Commercial Press, Shanghai. Author, “International Relationship in China and Its Ethical, Social and Political Interpretations”; “Fundamental Problems of Educational Reconstruction”; and “Educational Policy of the Nationalist Government.” — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI Cho-ming, (韋卓明)** Prefers English rendering: Francis Cho-min Wei. Born 1888; native of Peihai, Kwangtung. M.A. (Harvard). Ph.D. (London). 1924-7, dean and professor of philosophy, Central China College, Wuchang. President, same institution, since 1929. Author, “Political Principles of Mencius” (doctoral thesis) and “Chinese Moral Tradition and Its Social Values.” — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI, E. F** see Wei I-fu. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI, Francis Cho-min** see Wei Cho-ming. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI Huai, (魏懷)** Born 1882; native of Foochow, Fukien. Graduate, Mawei Naval Academy, Foochow, and Paris Polytechnic Institute. Sometime commissioner of education, Foochow. 1928-31, legislative member, Legislative Yuan. Director, civil affairs department, National Government headquarters, since January, 1932. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI I-fu, (韋以黻)** Prefers English rendering: E. F. Wei. Born 1888; native of Wushing, Chekiang. Graduate, Nanyang College. M.E. (Cornell). Sometime locomotive superintendent, Peiping-Suiyuan railway. October, 1928-February, 1932, administrative vice-minister of communications. Chief technical expert, same ministry, since March, 1932. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI Tao-ming, (魏道明)** Courtesy name: Po-ts'ung 伯聰 Born 1898; native of Kiukiang, Kiangsi. Docteur en droit (Paris). 1928-9, minister of justice. April, 1930-December, 1931, mayor of Nanking. Member, national reconstruction commission, since 1931. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI T'ing-sheng, (衞挺生)** Courtesy name: Shen-fu 探甫 Prefers English rendering: Tingsheng Shenfu Wei. Born 1891; native of Tsaoyang, Hupeh. A.B., M.B.A. (Harvard). 1927-8, chief of tariff division, customs administration, ministry of finance. Legislative member, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1928. Author, “A Case Study on the Utilization of Foreign Capital in the Economic Development of the South American A.B.C. Powers.” — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEI, Tingsheng Shenfu** see above. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEN Ch'un, (文羣)** Courtesy name: Yi-yun 詒雲 Born 1882; native of P'inghsiang, Kiangsi. Graduate, Waseda University. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. 1913, member of parliament. 1916-7, vice-minister of agriculture and commerce. 1919, vice-minister of finance, Canton military government. 1922, commissioner of finance, Kiangsi provincial government. Administrative member, same provincial government, since 1932. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WEN Yu-ch'ing, (溫毓慶)** Born 1893; native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. A.B., A.M., Ph.D. (Harvard). 1927, president, Customs College, Peiping. 1931, counsellor, ministry of finance. 1932-5, director, bureau of international telegraphs. Director-general of telegraphs, ministry of communications, since December, 1935. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WENG Chao-yuan, (翁照垣)** Born 1893; native of Huailai, Kwangtung. Graduate, Canton Military Officers' Training Institute and Military Officers' College, Tokyo, Japan. Studied aviation in France. Became prominent in Shanghai war of 1932, when he was brigade commander of 78th division of 19th route army, and won fame as the “Hero of Woosung Defence.” Commander of 114th artillery division north-eastern army, 1933. Implicated in Fukien independence movement 1933-4, and after collapse of movement, travelled abroad and has since been living in retirement. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WENG Wen-hao, (翁文灝)** Courtesy name: Yung-i 詠霓 Prefers English rendering: W. H. Wong. Born 1889; native of Chekiang. D.Sc. (Louvain). Director, geological survey of China, since 1922. May-November, 1931, acting president, Tsing Hua University. October, 1932, appointed minister of education, but declined. 1935, general manager, Chung Fu Mining Corporation. Secretary-general, Executive Yuan, since December, 1935. Secretary-general to Chinese special delegation to Coronation of King George VI, May, 1937. Author, “Mineral Resources of China” and “Earthquake Regions in China.” — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WONG Payuen** see Wang Huai-chung. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WONG, W. H** see Weng Wen-hao. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WONG, Y. S** see Wang Yen-sung. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WONG, Yuen-su** see Huang Yun-su. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WONG Zoong-fu** see Wang Min-kao. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WOO, James** see Wu K'ai-sheng. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WOU Shao-fong** see Wu Hsiu-feng. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WU, Butterfly** see Hu Tieh. — p. 186 ([scan leaf 209](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n209/mode/1up))

**WU Ch'i-ting, (吳啟鼎)** Courtesy name: Ch'i-ting 芑汀 Born 1891; native of Tz'uch'i, Chekiang. B.S. (Ohio). 1931-3, director, wine, tobacco and stamp tax bureau, Chekiang province, Director, internal revenue administration, ministry of finance, since March, 1934. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Ching-heng, (吳敬恆)** Courtesy name: Chih-hui 稚輝 Born 1864; native of Wusih, Kiangsu. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. One of Kuomintang's “elder statesmen.” Well known scholar: “style characterized by use of common language, slang, vulgar comparisons, keen humour, bold satire and audacity.” Outspoken opponent of Tagore. Member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since 1924. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Ching-hsiung, (吳經熊)** Prefers English rendering: John C. H. Wu. Born 1899; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. LL.B. (Soochow). J.D. (Michigan). 1927, judge, Shanghai provisional court. Dean, comparative law school of China, since 1928. May, 1931-January, 1933, Chinese adviser, Shanghai municipal council. Legislative member, concurrently vice-chairman, constitution drafting committee, Legislative Yuan, since February, 1933. Author, “Human Nature of Law” and “Juridical Essays.” Editor-in-chief, Tien Hsia monthly, since 1935. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Chung-hsin, (吳忠信)** Courtesy name: Li-ch'ing 禮卿 Born 1888; native of Hofei, Anhwei. Graduate, Paoting Military Academy; studied in Japan. Member, T'ung Meng Hui. Sometime commissioner of public safety, Canton city government; garrison commander, Shiukwan (Kwangtung). 1929, chairman, Anhwei provincial government. Chairman, Kweichow provincial government, since April, 1935. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee (1929-31). — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Hsien, (吳憲)** Courtesy name: T'ao-min 陶民 Born 1893; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.S. (Mass. Inst. Tech.). Ph.D. (Harvard). 1921-4, associate in biochemistry; since 1928, professor of biochemistry, Peiping Union Medical College. Co-operating editor, Journal of Biochemistry (Japan). Editor, Chinese Journal of Physiology, since 1926. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Hsiu-feng, (吳秀[illegible])** Prefers own rendering: Wou Shao-fong. Born 1898; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Docteur en droit (Paris). Chinese member, League of Nations secretariat (Geneva), since 1929. Author (in French): “China and the Washington Conference” and “Sun Yat-sen: His Life and His Doctrines.” — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU, John C. H** see Wu Ching-hsiung. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU K'ai sheng, (吳凱聲)** Prefers English rendering: James Woo. Born 1900; native of Ihsing, Kiangsu. Docteur en droit (Lyons). 1929-31, secretary, ministry of foreign affairs; chargé d'affaires, Berne, and delegate, League of Nations conferences. Legal practice in Shanghai, resumed October, 1932. Councillor, French municipal council (Shanghai), 1934-6. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU K'ang, (吳康)** Courtesy name: Ching-hsien 敬軒 Native of Kwangtung. Docteur en lettres (Paris). Dean, college of arts, Chungshan University (Canton), since 1932. 1935, delegate, 19th quinquennial session of International Congress of Orientalists. At present lecturing on Chinese subjects at the Sorbonne, Paris. Honorary member, Institute of Greater India, Calcutta. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Kuo-chen, (吳國楨)** Prefers own rendering: Wu Kuo-cheng. Born 1903; native of Chienshih, Hupeh. B.A. (Grinnell). M.A., Ph.D. (Princeton). 1931-2, commissioner of finance, Hupeh provincial government. Mayor of Hankow, since September, 1932. Concurrently administrative member, Hupeh provincial government, since July 1933. Author, “Ancient Chinese Political Theories” (doctoral thesis). — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Kuo-cheng** see above. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Lei-ch'uan, (吳雷川)** Born 1870; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. October,1928-September, 1929, administrative vice-minister of education. April, 1926-August, 1933, vice-president, then president, Yenching University. Professor of Chinese, same institution, since September, 1933. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Lien-teh, (伍連德)** Courtesy name: Hsin Lien 星聯 Born 1880 in Singapore. Native of Taishan, Kwangtung, B.A., M.B., Ch.B., M.D. (Cantab). Hon LL.D. (Hongkong) and D.Sc. (St. John's). 1912-30, director and chief medical officer, North Manchurian Plague Prevention Service at Harbin. Director, National Quarantine Service, since April, 1930. Delegate to International Opium conferences (1912 and 1918). Past president, National Medical Association. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Nan-ju, (吳南如)** Courtesy name: Ping-wen 炳文 Born 1897; native of Ihsing, Kiangsu. Was in journalistic world before joining diplomatic service. 1927, first secretary, legation in London. 1931-2, section chief, European and American affairs department, then assistant director of publicity, ministry of foreign affairs. Counsellor, embassy in Moscow, since January, 1933. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU, P. S** see below. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Po-sheng, (伍伯勝)** Prefers English rendering: P. S. Wu. Born 1905; native of T'aishan, Kwangtung. 1928, J. D. (Chicago). 1929-30, secretary, ministry of foreign affairs. Counsellor, city government of Canton, since August, 1931. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Sung-kao, (吳頌皐)** Born 1890; native of Wuhsien, Kiangsu. B.A. (Fuhtan). LL.B. (Paris). Research work at London School of Economics. 1929-32, dean of law school, Fuhtan University, Shanghai. 1932-5, counsellor, Executive Yuan; counsellor, ministry of foreign affairs. Director of international affairs, same ministry, since May, 1935. Founder and editor-in-chief, Wai Chiao P'ing Lun (Foreign Affairs Review), since September, 1933. Author of two volumes on extraterritoriality, and the diplomatic history of Europe. — p. 187 ([scan leaf 210](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n210/mode/1up))

**WU Ta-chun, (吳大鈞)** Courtesy name: Ping-ch'ang 秉常 Born 1903; native of Foochow, Fukien. B.S., M.B.A. (Pennsylvania). Director, bureau of statistics, central Kuomintang headquarters, since 1929. Concurrently director, similar bureau, comptroller-general's office, since 1932. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Te-chen** see below. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU T'ieh-ch'eng, (吳鐵城)** Better known as Wu Te-chen. Born 1888; native of Chungshan, Kwangtung. Studied in Japan. 1921, elected magistrate of native district. 1925, garrison commander, Canton; commander, Nationalist 17th division. 1928-30, legislative member, Legislative Yuan; political vice-minister of interior. Mayor of Greater Shanghai, since January, 1932. Appointed chairman of Kwangtung provincial government, March, 1937. Member, Kuomintang central excutive committee, since 1928. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Ting-ch'ang, (吳鼎昌)** Courtesy name: Ta-ch'uan 達銓 Born 1884; native of Chekiang. 1908, Hanlin (D.Litt.). 1912, governor, Bank of China. 1913, director, Tientsin mint. 1915, vice-minister of finance. General manager, Yien-Yieh (Salt) Commercial Bank, since 1917. Chairman of the banking syndicate of Yien-Yien, Kincheng, Continental and China and South Sea Banks, since 1923. Concurrently general manager of the Joint Savings Society of above-mentioned four allied banks and chief superintendent of the joint treasury of the above-mentioned four allied banks. Minister of industries, since December, 1935. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Tsai-chang, (吳在章)** Courtesy name: Wen-chai 蘊齋 Born 1886; native of Chinkiang, Kiangsu. M.Com. (Waseda). Manager, Shanghai branch, Kincheng Banking Corporation. since 1924. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Yi-fang, Miss, (吳貽芳女士)** Born 1893; native of Hangchow, Chekiang. B.A. (Ginling). M.A., Ph.D. (Michigan). Hon. D.Sc. (St. John's). President, Ginling College for Women, Nanking, since 1928. Vice-chairman, then chairman, National Christian Council of China, since 1933. Delegate, Institute of Pacific Relations conferences (1929-33), and International Congress of Women, Chicago (1933). Chairman, executive committee, China Christian Educational Association (1933). Author, “A Contribution to the Biology of Simulium (Diptera)” (doctoral thesis). — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Yu-hsun, (吳有訓)** Born 1900; native of Kiangsi. D.Sc. (Chicago). Sometime research instructor in physics, Chicago University; dean, physics department, Central University, Nanking. Professor and dean, physics department, Tsing Hua University, since 1928. Concurrently research fellow, Academia Sinica. Elected member, Prussian Academy of Natural Sciences, Halle, since January, 1936, in recognition of his scientific researches—being first Chinese to be so honoured. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**WU Yun-ch'u, (吳蘊初)** Native of Chekiang. Proprietor and general manager, Tien Chu Seasoning Powder Factory (Shanghai), since 1924. Philanthropist. His company donated (1934) two aeroplanes to Chinese Government. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG, Right Rev. Boniface** see Yang Fu-chiao. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Chieh, (楊杰)** Courtesy name: Keng-kuang 耿光 Born 1889; native of Tali, Yunnan. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1928, again 1932-3, principal, Central Military Academy, Nanking. 1929-31, departmental director, Generalissimo's headquarters. 1933, investigated military conditions in Europe and U.S.A. Vice-chief of general staff, since April, 1934. Concurrently dean of studies, Central Military Academy, since January, 1935. Promoted member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Ch'ung-jui, Miss, (楊崇瑞女士)** Prefers English rendering: Marion Yang. Born 1892; native of T'unghsien, Hopei. Graduate, North China Union Medical College for Women, Peiping. Postgraduate studies in U.S.A. and Europe. 1927-30, chief, medical service division, Peiping special health area; instructor in hygiene and public health, Peiping Union Medical College. Director, First National Midwifery School (Peiping) and Child Health Institute (co-operating institution), since September, 1930. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Fu-chiao, (楊福爵)** Better known as the Right Rev. Boniface Yang. Born 1878; native of Namyung, Kwangtung. Studied at Canton Seminary. 1906-30, ordained as priest; missionary; vicar of Canton cathedral. Consecrated titular bishop of Eguga and appointed auxiliary bishop to vicar apostolic of Canton, since July, 1931. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG, Grace** see below. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Hsi-chen, Miss, (楊錫珍女士)** Prefers English rendering: Grace Yang. Born 1896; native of Soochow. B.A. (Mount Holyoke). M.A. in education (Columbia). 1919-29, secretary, student department, then executive committee, national committee Y.W.C.A. Principal, McTyiere School (Shanghai), since June, 1929. Delegate, International Federation of University Women, Oslo, and World's Student Christian Federation (1924). — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Hu, (楊虎)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-t'ien 嘯天 Born 1898; native of Ningkuo, Anhwei. 1927, commander, Shanghai-Woosung garrison. Director. Shanghai peace maintenance corps, since October, 1932. Concurrently garrison commander, Shanghai-Woosung area, since May, 1936. Emergency commander of Greater Shanghai during Sino-Japanese hostilities in Shanghai, 1937. Promoted member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Hu-ch'eng, (楊虎城)** Born 1893; native of P'uch'eng, Shensi. 1928, commander, Nationalist reorganized 14th division. 1929, garrison commander, Nanyang (Honan). 1931-2, chairman, Shensi provincial government. Commander, 17th division, since December, 1930. Dismissed but to remain on duty after settlement of Sian coup. Resigned in May, to study military affairs in foreign countries. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Kuang-sheng, (楊光泩)** Prefers English rendering: Clarence Kuangson Young. Born 1900; native of Shanghai. B.A. (Colorado). Ph.D. (Princeton). 1931-3, consul-general in London; assistant director of publicity, delegation to League of Nations assembly. Managing director, Chekiai News assembly. 1933-5, managing director, Chekiai News Agency. Managing director, China Press, since July, 1935. Managing editor, China Quarterly, since March, 1935. Member of Chinese delegation to Coronation of King George VI, May, 1937. — p. 188 ([scan leaf 211](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n211/mode/1up))

**YANG Lien, (楊廉)** Courtesy name: Ssu-mo 思默 Born 1896; native of Szechwan. B.A. (Peking Government). M.A. (Columbia Teachers College). 1929 and 1932, commissioned by ministry of education to study educational conditions in U.S.A. and Europe. 1931, director of social education, ministry of education. Commissioner of education, Anhwei provincial government, since 1933. Author of several volumes on education, social problems, and primary education in Europe and U.S.A. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YANG, Marion** see Yang Ch'ung-jui. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YANG Sen, (楊森)** Courtesy name: Tzu-hui 子惠 Born 1883; native of Kuangan, Szechwan. 1924, director-general of military affairs, Szechwan province. Commander, 20th army, since 1926. Concurrently commander, 5th route army (1927), and administrative member, Szechwan provincial government, (1928-34). — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YANG Shu-k'an, (楊庶堪)** Courtesy name: Ts'ang-pai 滄白 Native of Batang, Szechwan. Active Kuomintang worker. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Reserve member, Kuomintang central supervisory committee, since December, 1931. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YANG Teh-sen, (楊德森)** Born 1882; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. M. Com. (Antwerp). Sometime secretary, legation in Brussels. 1916-21, managing director, Exchange Bank of China and Sin Hang Bank, Peiping. 1928-1934, manager, then director, Bank of Communications, Shanghai. Associate general manager, China Development Finance Corporation, Shanghai, since February, 1935. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YANG Yung-ch'ing, (楊永清)** Courtesy name: Hui-ch'ing 惠慶 Prefers English rendering: Y. C. Yang. Born 1892; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. M.A., LL.B. (George Washington). Sometime secretary and departmental director, ministry of foreign affairs. President, Soochow University, since October, 1927. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEH Cho-t'ang, (葉琢堂)** Better known locally as Yih Chur-tong. Native of Chekiang. Banker in Shanghai for quarter of a century. Managing director, Central Bank and Bank of China, since 1934. General manager, Ningpo Commercial Bank, since September, 1935. Member, national economic council, since 1932. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEH Ch'u-ts'ang, (葉楚傖)** Pen name: Hsiao-feng 小鳳 Born 1883; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. 1916, chief editor, Min Kuo Jih Pao (Shanghai). April, 1930-November, 1931, chairman, Kiangsu provincial government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. State councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Concurrently secretary-general, central Kuomintang headquarters. Vice-president, Legislative Yuan, since December, 1935. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEH Kung-cho, (葉恭綽)** Courtesy name: Yü-hu 譽虎 Born 1880; native of P'anyu, Kwangtung. 1920, 1922 and 1924, minister of communications. January, 1932, minister of railways. Trustee, British Boxer Indemnity fund administration, since 1931. Concurrently member, national economic council, since 1932. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEH Yuan-lung, (葉元龍)** Born 1897; native of Anhwei. 1929, commissioner of finance, Anhwei provincial government. 1930-4, professor of political science, Chinan University and Shanghai Law College. Commissioner of education, Kweichow provincial government, since April, 1935. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Chi-tz'u, (嚴濟慈)** Courtesy name: Mu-kuang 慕光 Prefers own rendering: Ny Tsi-ze. Born 1900; native of Tungyang, Chekiang. 1923, B.Sc. (Southeastern). 1927, D.Sc. (Paris). 1927-30, taught physics in Shanghai. Director, institute of physics, Peiping Research Academy, since 1930. One of leading physicists in China, was elected (February, 1935) member on council of French Society of Physics—first instance in latter's history, only other Oriental on its council being Dr. Raman (Indian Nobel prize winner for physics in 1930). — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Chuang, (嚴莊)** Courtesy name: Ching-chai 敬齋 Born 1889: native of Weinan, Shensi. B.S., M.E. (Michigan). 1927, commissioner of reconstruction, Shensi provincial government. 1930-3, director of labour, then of mines, ministry of industries. Supervisory member, Control Yuan, since 1934. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN, F. C** see below. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Fu-ch'ing, (顏福慶)** Courtesy name: K'e-ch'ing 克卿 Prefers English rendering: F. C. Yen. Born 1880; cousin of W. W. Yen. M.D. (Yale). D.T.M. (Liverpool). C.P.H. (Harvard). 1914-26, president, Yale Medical College, Changsha. Dean, college of medicine, Central University, since July, 1928. Concurrently superintendent, Chinese Red Cross General Hospital, Shanghai. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Hsi-shan, (閻錫山)** Courtesy name: Pai-ch'uan 百川 Born 1882; native of Wut'ai, Shansi. Graduate, Tokyo Military Staff College. 1912-28, civil and military governor, “Model Province.” 1928-30, Peiping-Tientsin garrison commander. April-September, 1930, revolted in coalition with Feng Yü-hsiang and attempted to set up bogus government at Peiping. December, 1931, pardoned, and reinstated member, Kuomintang central executive committee. Reappointed state councillor of the National Government, since January, 1932. Director of peace preservation, T'aiyuan, since February, 1932. Vice-chairman, national military council, since January, 1936. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Hui-ch'ing, (顏惠慶)** Courtesy name: Chün-jen 駿人 Prefers English rendering: W. W. Yen. Born 1877; native of Shanghai. B.A. (Virginia). Hanlin compiler of imperial examinations. 1913-22, minister to Germany and Denmark; minister for foreign affairs; acting premier. 1923-7, chairman, commission for readjustment of finance; minister of agriculture and commerce; premier, concurrently minister of interior; appointed minister to London; premier and acting minister for foreign affairs. October, 1931-December, 1932, minister to U.S.A.; concurrently delegate, League of Nations assembly and council. Ambassador to Soviet Russia, 1933-6 Author, “Anglo-Chinese Dictionary” (two vol.), etc. — p. 189 ([scan leaf 212](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n212/mode/1up))

**YEN Jen-kuang, (顏任光)** Born 1892; native of Chekiang. Ph.D. (Chicago). Sometime head, physics department, National University of Peking. 1926-8, professor of physics, Shanghai University. 1932-5, Director-general of telegraphs, ministry of communications. Considered one of the best physicists in China. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN, James Y. C** see Yen Yang-chu. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN Pao-hang, (閻寶航)** Prefers English rendering: Paul Yen. Born 1897; native of Haicheng, Liaoning. Post-graduate work in Edinburgh University. 1918, founder, school for poor children, Shenyang. 1929-31, general secretary, Y.M.C.A., Shenyang (Mukden). Executive secretary, New Life Movement Association, Nanchang, since April, 1934. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN, Paul** see above. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN Teh-ch'ing, (顏德慶)** Courtesy name: Chi-yü 季餘 Born 1879; younger brother of W. W. Yen. C.E. (Lehigh). Chinshih of imperial examinations. 1922-8, director, technical department, ministry of communications. 1929-30, managing director, Kiaochow-Tsinan railway. Counsellor, then chief technical expert, ministry of railways, since June, 1930. President, Nanking Rotary Club (1934). — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN Yang-ch'u, (晏陽初)** Prefers English rendering: James Y. C. Yen. Born 1894; native of Hunan. B.A. (Yale). M.A. (Princeton). Hon. M.A. (Yale). 1918-9, joint inventor of 1,000 simplest Chinese characters for teaching labourers in France. Founder and general secretary, National Association for Advancement of Mass Education, since 1925. Better known as director, Mass Education Experimental Station, Tinghsien (Hopei), since 1928. Member, national economic council, since 1933. Author, “Mass Education in China.” — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YEN, W. W** see Yen Hui-ch'ing. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YIH Chur-tong** see Yeh Cho-t'ang. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YING Shang-teh, (應尙德)** Prefers anglicized Shanghai rendering: Z. T. Ing. Born 1886; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. B.Sc. (Wooster). M.A. (Columbia). Professor of biology in Nanking (1913-9) and Nankai Universities(1922-6). 1928-35, director of general affairs, then member, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs. Counsellor, Chinese embassy in Washington, since May, 1936. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YOUNG, Clarence Kuangson** see Yang Kuang-sheng. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**“YOUNG MARSHAL,”** see Chang Hsueh-liang. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YOUNG, Samuel S** see Hsiung Ch'ung-chih· — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Chi-ch'uan, (余[illegible]傳)** Courtesy name: Chien-ch'iu 劍秋 Born 1894; native of Changsha, Hunan. M.A. in civil engineering (Illinois). Sometime director of public works, Hankow city government; senior technical expert, national construction commission; director of engineering, Chekiang provincial highway administration; director of public works, Nanking city government. Commissioner of reconstruction, Hunan provincial government, since 1934. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Chi-shih, (兪濟時)** Born 1903; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. Graduate, Whampoa Military Academy. 1930, commander, metropolitan police; garrison commander, Nanking. 1931, commander, 2nd division. 1932, commander, reorganized 88th division. Commissioner of public safety, Chekiang province, since February, 1933. Decorated with “White Sun and Blue Sky” medal for meritorious defence of Shanghai area. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Ch'ing-sung, (余青松)** Born 1897; native of Amoy, Fukien. C.E. (Lehigh).M.S. (Pittsburgh). Ph.D. (California). Fellow, Royal Astronomical Society. 1927-9, professor of astrophysics and chairman, astronomy department, Amoy University. Director, research institute of astronomy, Academia Sinica, and director, Nanking Purple Mountain Observatory, since July, 1929. Scientific research prize winner (1928), China Foundation for Promotion of Education and Culture. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Ching-t'ang, (余井塘)** Born 1897; native of Hsinghua, Kiangsu. M.A. (Iowa). 1928-30, acting director, organization department, central Kuomintang headquarters; dean, Central Political Institute, Nanking. Reserve member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since March, 1929. Administrative member, Kiangsu provincial government, since September, 1933. Concurrently commissioner of civil affairs, same provincial government, since May, 1934. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Ch'ing-t'ang, Miss, (俞慶棠女士)** Wife of T'ang Ch'ing-yi 唐慶詒夫人 Prefers own anglicized rendering: Chindon Yui Tang. Born 1897; native of T'aits'ang, Kiangsu. B.A. (Women's College, Delaware). M.A. (Columbia). 1924-6, professor of education, Great China University. 1927-9, head, division of social education, Central University. 1928, founder and president, Kiangsu Provincial College of Education. Professor and director of research and experimentation, same institution, since 1930. General director, China Association for Adult Education. Author and translator of books on adult education. Co-translator, “Foundations of Method.” — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Chun-chi, (于焌吉)** Courtesy name: Ch'ien-liu 謙六 Prefers English rendering: James T. C. Yu. Born 1899; native of Shengfang, Hopei. B.S., Ph.B. (Denison). M.Sc., D.Sc. (New York). A.B., A.M., Ph.D. (Columbia). 1928-9, senior expert, treaty commission, ministry of foreign affairs. 1930-4, consul-general in Havana. Consul-general in New York, since April, 1935. Author, “The Interpretation of Treaties” (doctoral thesis). — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Fei-peng, (兪飛鵬)** Courtesy name: Ch'iao-feng 樵峯 Born 1882; native of Fenghua, Chekiang. 1928-31, director of communications, generalissimo's headquarters; part time concurrently commissariat director, ministry of military affairs. Administrative, then political, vice-minister of communications, since January, 1932. Appointed minister of communications, March, 1937. Credited with having revolutionized military transportation in China. Director, military mission to U.S.A. and Europe, 1934. — p. 190 ([scan leaf 213](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n213/mode/1up))

**YU Han-mou, (余漢謀)** Native of Kwangtung. Formerly one of Chen Chi-tang's most trusted commanders, was almost the first to desert his former chief and fly to Nanking to pledge his fealty to the Central Government in reply to Chen Chi-tang's refusal to obey Nanking. Appointed to succeed Chen Chi-tang as pacification commissioner and commander-in-chief of Kwangtung forces, since July 20, 1936. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since December, 1931. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Hsia-ch'ing, (虞洽卿)** Courtesy name: Ho-teh 和德 Better known as: Yu Ya-ching. Born 1865; native of Chenhai, Chekiang. One of Shanghai's most prominent merchants. 1925, chairman, general chamber of commerce. 1926, chairman, Chinese ratepayers' association. Managing director, San Peh Steam Navigation Company and Shanghai cotton produce exchange etc. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, since April, 1929. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Hsueh-chung, (于學忠)** Courtesy name: Hsiao-hou 孝侯 Born 1889; native of P'englai, Shantung. 1927, commander, 20th Fengtien army. 1928, commander, Linsui garrison. 1931-2, commander, Peiping-Tientsin garrison. 1932-5, chairman, Hopei provincial government. Concurrently commander, reorganized 51st army. Chairman, Kansu provincial government, since December, 1935. Dismissed from above post after Sian coup and appointed pacification commissioner of Kiangsu, April, 1937. 1st class Paoting decoration. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Hung-chun, (俞鴻鈞)** Prefers English rendering: O. K. Yui. Born 1898; native of Hsinhui, Kwangtung. B.A. (St. John's). 1924-6, assistant editor, Shanghai Gazette. 1927, secretary, ministry of foreign affairs, Hankow nationalist government. Secretary-general, city government of Greater Shanghai, since 1928. Appointed acting mayor of Greater Shanghai on transfer of Wu Te-chen to post of chairman of Kwangtung provincial government in March, 1937. Mayor of Greater Shanghai since August, 1937. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU, James T. C** see Yu Chün-chi. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Nan-ch'iu, (余楠秋)** Born 1897; native of Changsha, Hunan. Studied at Yale-in-China and Tsing Hua College; A.B. (Illinois); professor, National Southeastern University, 1921-2; professor, Fu Tan University since 1923; dean, school of liberal arts, Fu Tan University, since 1927. Author, “International Relationship between America, Japan and China”; “Modern European History”; “History of English,” and a number of other college textbooks. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Ta-fu, (郁達夫)** Courtesy name: Wen 文 Prefers own rendering: Yue Daff. Born 1897; native of Fuyang, Chekiang. B.A. (Tokyo Imperial). 1923-7, professor of literature, Peiping and Canton. Co-founder, “Creative Society” (with Kuo Mo-jo and Chang Tzu-p'ing), also Federation of Leftist Writers. Editor, Creation monthly and The Masses fortnightly. Author of well-known Chinese novels: “The Stray Sheep,” “Forgive Her!” “Nine Fragments from the Diary” etc.—many being available in English, Russian, Japanese and French translations. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Tso-t'ing, (俞佐廷)** Prefers own rendering: Yue Tsu-ting. Born 1887; native of Chenhai, Chekiang. Sometime chairman, Ningpo chamber of commerce; mayor of Ningpo. Manager, Heng Sung Native Bank, since 1928. General manager, Ningpo Savings Society, since 1930. 1934-6, chairman, Shanghai general chamber of commerce. Director of several banks; executive member, Shanghai native bankers association. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Tzu-yi, (俞子夷)** Born 1887; native of Soochow, Kiangsu. Studied at Nanyang College. Sometime professor of education, Southeastern University. Professor of education, Chekiang University (Hangchow), since 1929. Author of numerous volumes on elementary education. Editor, Teachers' Guide (Chinese monthly). — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Ya-ching** see Yu Hsia-ch'ing. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YU Yu-jen, (于右任)** Courtesy name: Po-hsün 伯循 Born 1882; native of Sanyuan, Shensi. Active revolutionary publicist. 1925, appointed minister of interior, but declined. 1928-30, president, board of audit. November, 1930-December, 1931, state councillor of the National Government. Member, Kuomintang central executive committee, since 1924. President, Control Yuan, since November, 1930. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUAN, L. T** see below. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUAN Li-tun, (袁禮敦)** Courtesy name: Lu-teng 履登 Prefers English rendering: L. T. Yuan. Born 1879; native of Ningpo, Chekiang. Graduate, St. John's College. 1904-12, principal, English Methodist College, Ningpo. General manager, Ningpo-Shaohsing Steam Navigation Co. (Shanghai), since 1921. Councillor, Shanghai municipal council, 1928-34. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUAN, T. L** see below. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUAN T'ung-li, (袁同禮)** Prefers English rendering: T. L. Yuan. Born 1895; native of Tientsin, Hopei. B.L.S. (New York State Library School). 1934, delegate, International Committee on Intellectual Cooperation (Geneva) and International Congress of Librarians (Madrid); awarded Columbia University's medal for excellence in recognition of his “library service in China.” Co-director, national library, Peiping, since 1927. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUE Daff** see Yu Ta-fu. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUE Tsu-ting** see Yu Tso-t'ing. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUI, O. K** see Yu Hung-chün. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**YUNG Tsung-ching, (榮宗敬)** Better known as the “Flour King” of China. Resides in Wusih and Shanghai. Philanthropist. President, Sen Sing Textile Manufacturing Co. Member, national economic council, since 1931. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**ZAU, S. U** see Chao Hsi-en. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**ZEE, Z. Z** see Hsü Shan-hsiang. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**ZEN Hung-chiun** see Jen Hung-chün. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**ZIA, Z. K** see Hsieh Sung-kao. — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))

**ZING Zung-ching** see Ch'in Yun-ch'ing — p. 191 ([scan leaf 214](https://archive.org/details/chinayearbook1930000hgww/page/n214/mode/1up))
